# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Model tensors of any dtype anira names:** `ANIRA_DTYPE_F32`, `F64`, `F16`, `BF16`, `I8`, `U8`, `I16`, `I32`, `I64` and `BOOL8`, for every role. The validator no longer refuses a model tensor that is not float32: each tensor travels in its spec's dtype end to end (its ring, the chunk's buffer, its Static store, its State pair), so a registered engine's descriptors carry each tensor's dtype on every call, as its load and prepare records already did. A dtype anira does not name (several lanes, an unsigned integer wider than 8 bits) is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at `anira_handler_create`. Nothing converts: a tensor's ring streams its spec's dtype unless the contract names another, and a host of another sample type names its type in `ring_dtypes` and adds a processor that converts in `pre_process` / `post_process` with its own scaling. Tests: `test/abi/test_Dtypes.cpp` (a model of each of the ten dtypes through a registered engine with no ring setting, a typed Static tensor, the per-call refusal of a host tensor of another dtype, float16 and int8 models behind a float32 host's converting processor).
- **C ABI, handler:** the plan report names each slot's dtypes: `anira_plan_slot` gains the tail fields `dtype` (the spec's, the model tensor's) and `ring_dtype` (the ring's, the host end of a Streamed tensor; 0 for a slot without a ring), the prepare log's slot lines name both, and the TypeScript `PlanSlot` mirrors them (`dtype`, `ringDtype`).
- **Bundled models:** TypedGainNetwork over example-models `28e6dd9` (`extras/fetch-models.cmake`'s pin, which moves from `d1bc5cf`): the gain fixture's tensors at `int16`, `int8` and `float16`, computed in float32 and cast at the boundary, with a row for every engine (`extras/models/model-pool/typed_gain_<dtype>.model.json`) and an ExecuTorch export at a static 512-sample window whose inputs and outputs the memory plan leaves to the caller (`typed_gain_<dtype>_unplanned.model.json`); an ONNX model per dtype anira names (`dtype_matrix_<dtype>.onnx`) and a fully integer TFLite model whose int8 I/O carries a scale and a zero point (`quantized_gain_network_int8.tflite`), named in `extras/models/model_files.h`. Each file loads and runs exactly in anira's pinned runtimes (ONNX Runtime 1.26.0, TFLite 2.17.0, LibTorch 2.12.0, ExecuTorch 1.3.1).

### Changed

- **C ABI, handler:** a slot row's `edge_class` (and `allocate_class`) is the edge the plan's engine takes with the slot's buffer: `ANIRA_EDGE_ZERO_COPY` where the engine binds it in place, `ANIRA_EDGE_HOST_COPY` with the engine's `reason` where it copies through memory of its own (LibTorch's outputs, which a method returns as its own tensors; LiteRT, TFLite and ExecuTorch, which copy through buffers of their own). Every row said zero-copy before, whatever the engine did; the edge-cost policy is not consumed yet, so no plan changes.
- **Engines:** a built-in engine refuses a model tensor it cannot bind with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at load, where the float32 rule of the adapters returned `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`: a limit of an engine is not a configuration error.
- **C ABI, configuration:** a tensor's ring dtype defaults to its spec's dtype (the model's), where it was `ANIRA_DTYPE_F32`: `anira_contract_hard_set_ring_dtype`, `anira::Hard::ring_dtypes` and a contract file's `ring_dtypes` name a ring dtype only where the host's samples differ from the model's, and then a processor that converts goes with it (nothing in anira converts; a ring dtype that differs from the spec's dtype stays `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at prepare without one). `anira_contract_hard_num_ring_dtypes` / `anira_contract_hard_ring_dtype` still list what was set; `anira_ring_dtype` reports the resolved one. Not breaking: every model a v3.0.0-alpha.2 handler can prepare has float32 Streamed tensors only (the validator refuses every other dtype), so for each of them the default is float32 as before, and an explicitly set ring dtype is unchanged.
- **C ABI, handler:** the dtype refusals name their remedy and spell a dtype by its word. A host tensor whose dtype is not its Streamed slot's ring dtype (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` per call, recorded in `anira_handler_rt_error`, nothing staged, as before) logs "… is int16, the slot's ring dtype is float32; nothing converts: pass a tensor of the ring dtype, or name int16 in the contract's ring_dtypes and add a processor that converts in pre_process"; a Static tensor of another dtype says that a Static tensor carries its spec's dtype; a ring dtype that differs from the spec's at prepare names the converting processor or dropping the ring dtype; `anira_em_audio_node_create`, whose node streams float32, names the ring dtype and the converting processor.

### Fixed

- **C ABI, configuration:** `anira_tensor_spec_create` refuses a dtype of 0 (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`), and `anira_model_config_to_json` refuses a tensor of a dtype that has no word in a model file (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`), where it wrote `"float32"` for it.
- **Docs:** `anira_model_config_model_path` returns NULL for an entry without a source too (an `anira_model_config_add_model_path` entry with a NULL path, the C++ `ModelConfig::add_model(engine_id)`), not only for a bytes entry or an index out of range, and `anira::ModelConfig::model_path` throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` for both kinds of entry and `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for an index out of range; the docs named the bytes entry alone. `anira_em_audio_node_create` lists a Streamed slot whose ring dtype is not float32 under `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, the status it returns, where its doc had it under `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`.

## [v3.0.0-alpha.2] - 2026-11-01

The 3.x runtime on the Hard path over the versioned C ABI (`anira/abi/*.h`, ABI 0.2: 228 promised names, 4 draft and 12 Emscripten-only ones): the core runs on the 3.x types behind the C entries, `anira/anira.hpp` is the C++20 face over them and `anira/compat/v2.hpp` the 2.x source-compatible one, and the 2.x C++ classes are gone from the installed package. Nothing in it is a binary promise before v3.0.0: while the ABI major is 0 a library accepts only the exact ABI version its headers were generated with (`anira_check_abi`; a header of v3.0.0-alpha.1, ABI 0.1, is refused), and every pre-release may still change any of it. The shared library is named after the ABI major, `libanira.so.0` (`libanira.0.dylib`, `anira-0.dll`), and exports the `anira_*` entries alone; tanh-lib, concurrentqueue and nlohmann_json are private and not installed, and anira's copies of the first two carry names of their own (tanh-lib's core in the inline ABI namespace `thl::anira`, tanh-lib v0.5.0; the queue as `anira_moodycamel`), so a host's own tanh-lib or concurrentqueue shares no code and no state with anira's, with a shared or a static libanira, on every platform. What this pre-release does not run yet: the Async contract (refused at prepare until M3); the Hard defaults `ANIRA_BUDGET_MEASURED` and `ANIRA_WARMUP_UNTIL_STABLE` (refused until M2b: a Hard contract sets an explicit budget and a FIXED or NONE warm-up); device blocks on the context (every context is Host-only, and the probe and the capabilities report the host domain alone); and model tensors of another dtype than float32, declared state excepted. The GPU and draft tensor factories are field fills that no adapter consumes yet (the field-fill ledger under Added names them). The npm package `@anira-project/anira` 3.0.0-alpha.2, published under the `next` tag, is the typed layer over the module with the names of `anira.hpp` (`Anira`, `Context`, `ModelConfig`, `ContractHandle`, `Pipeline`, `InferenceHandler`, `PlanReport`, `createAudioNode`, `defineProcessor`, `defineEngine`), with the ONNX Runtime Web engine at `@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web`.

### Added

- **C ABI, headers and enums:** ABI 0.2. `abi/symbols-0.txt` promises 228 names at this tag (88 at v3.0.0-alpha.1), `abi/symbols-draft.txt` four and the new `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` twelve; `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt` exports the names of the three lists plus `_malloc` and `_free` (246). The entries of this cycle recorded the promised count as they landed: 114 with `anira/abi/context.h`, `core.h` and `thread.h`, 132 with the handler, 150 with the tensor, 160 with the Hard entries over host tensors, 161 with the miss function (and 161, with 167 wasm exports, once the twelve float32 entries of the snapshots left), 182 with the Static entries, 183 with the state source, 184 with the processor, 185 with the host domain, 188 with the engine, 191 with the providers, 226 with the read-back, 229 with the declared latency, 234 with the engine word, 231 with the one pair rule of `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM`, 227 once the pair getters read both halves (no function carries `_id`), and 228 with `anira_plan_report_find`.
- **C ABI, headers and enums:** the umbrellas and the platform headers. `<anira/anira_all.h>`, generated by `tools/abi/gen.py` from the registry's new `umbrellas:` list: every header of the C ABI in registry order, the draft headers of `anira/abi/draft/` and the platform companion `anira/anira_em.h` (which declares nothing outside WebAssembly) included; an umbrella is an include list beside the platform headers in `include/anira/`, and gate 4 compiles it alone as C11 and C++17 (`<anira/anira.h>`, the C11 umbrella of the promised headers, is under Changed). The registry knows platform headers: a header with `platform: emscripten` (`file: anira_<name>.h`, its own `guard:`) is generated into `include/anira/` beside the umbrellas, its body inside `#ifdef __EMSCRIPTEN__` (an empty companion on every other target, so a native consumer may include it; the generator wraps the body in `#if defined(__EMSCRIPTEN__) || defined(DOXYGEN)`, so the reference documents it from any host); it declares functions, callbacks, typedefs and defines only, and no header may include it. Its names go to the new `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` and to `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt`, never to `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt`, the link probe or the layout test's include list; its defines reach `web/src/abi/enums.ts`, and `abi/anira.json` records the key. Its first user is `anira/anira_em.h` (below), whose names make up `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` (nine and then ten in the snapshots that introduced it, twelve at the tag).
- **C ABI, headers and enums:** the values appended to the enums alpha.1 pinned, each reported as an addition by `gen.py --diff-against`: `ANIRA_MISSED` (5, a positive status: a Hard entry's missed block, below) and `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_FOUND` (-23, for every lookup that finds nothing that matches) in `anira_status`; `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR = 8` in `anira_tensor_flags`; `ANIRA_STRUCT_ENGINE_CTX = 12` and `ANIRA_STRUCT_PLAN_QUERY` (13) in `anira_struct_id` (alpha.1's id 4 is `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX` now, Changed below); the role `ANIRA_ROLE_STATE = 3` in `anira_role`; the miss policy `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK = 3`; `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` (with the renumbering of both enums, Changed below); and `ANIRA_DTYPE_F64`, the 64-bit float dtype (`ANIRA_MAKE_DTYPE(ANIRA_DTYPE_FLOAT, 64, 1)`), beside the nine scalar dtypes the headers already pinned, `"float64"` in the JSON dtype vocabulary. New defines: `ANIRA_INSTANCES_AUTO`, `ANIRA_VARIANT_ANY`, and the flag bits of `anira/abi/enums.h` `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`, `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_REALTIME_SAFE`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_DYNAMIC_TIME`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS`, `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` and `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE`, each described with its consumer below. `web/src/abi/enums.ts` and `exports_wasm.txt` follow every addition.
- **C ABI, tensor (`anira/abi/tensor.h`, `anira/abi/draft/tensor_platform.h`):** `anira/abi/tensor.h`, the runtime tensor of section 1. Three Tier-1 records, frozen with no `struct_size` and no version field, identical on wasm32, LP64 and LLP64: `anira_tensor` (216 bytes: `domain`, `dtype`, `ndim`, `flags`, `shape[8]`, `strides[8]` in elements with all-zero = packed row-major, `byte_offset`, `handle`, `manager_ctx`, `release`, `acquire`), the union `anira_memory_handle` (24 bytes, the thirteen arms `host`, `cuda`, `gl`, `vk`, `opaque`, `mtl`, `iosurface`, `wgpu`, `dmabuf`, `ahb`, `d3d12`, `planes`, `raw[3]`, every vendor handle at its wire width) and `anira_sync_token` (24 bytes: `kind`, `flags`, the payload union `u`), with the release callback `anira_tensor_release_proc`, a function type that sits in an `ANIRA_PTR` slot (`[thread-safe, !audio-thread]`, not real-time: it runs on an inference thread or on the caller of poll / wait / prepare / destroy, never on the driver thread). The factories `anira_tensor_init_host` / `pinned` / `cuda` / `gl_buffer` / `vulkan` / `opaque_fd` / `wgpu_buffer` / `dmabuf` zero the caller's record and then fill it, `[thread-safe]` `[callback-safe]` `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, and return nothing: a dtype of 0, a rank above `ANIRA_MAX_RANK`, a NULL shape with a rank above 0 or a negative extent leave the record all-zero, which no filled record is; every argument is read before the record is zeroed, so `shape` and a token may point into the record being filled (`anira_tensor_init_host(&t, p, t.dtype, t.ndim, t.shape)` re-points a tensor), and the previous content is overwritten, never released; a NULL fence, a timeline semaphore of 0 and a negative `sync_fd` mean `ANIRA_SYNC_NONE` (anira never fabricates a fence), a token passed by pointer is copied and that copy is the hand-off, a `sync_fd` of 0 or more is owned by the record's `acquire` from there on, and a refused call takes neither the token nor the fd. Planar host memory is part of the tensor, contained: `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR = 8` is appended to `anira_tensor_flags`, the `planes` arm holds `ptrs`, `count` and `reserved`, `anira_tensor_init_host_planar(tensor, planes, count, dtype, ndim, shape)` fills it from one pointer per plane of axis 0 (the channel pointers of an audio host; `planes` is `const void*`, so a `float**` and a `const float* const*` convert without a cast in C and in C++; `count` must equal `shape[0]` and the rank be 1 or more, else the record stays all-zero) and `anira_tensor_plane(tensor, plane, dtype)` reads one plane with `byte_offset` applied. It is a boundary representation of the two host domains: only an entry whose own documentation says so accepts a planar tensor (the Hard entries, below), every other consumer and every other domain refuses the flag with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, and `anira_tensor_data` / `anira_tensor_data_f32` return NULL for one. `anira_tensor_init_dlpack(tensor, dl_managed_tensor_versioned, err)` (`[main-thread]`; `anira_error*` last, which the design document's sketch lacks) bridges a DLPack 1.x `DLManagedTensorVersioned` handed over as `void*`: `kDLCPU` is `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST`, `kDLCUDAHost` `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST_PINNED`, every other device and every dtype code above 6 `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, as is a major other than 1 (nothing past the version is read); shape and strides are copied (NULL strides = packed row-major; strides that are present and all zero over more than one element, a fully broadcast view, are `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, because all-zero is this record's spelling of packed), `DLPACK_FLAG_BITMASK_READ_ONLY` sets `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY`, `release` is a trampoline that disarms the record and calls the producer's deleter exactly once (a NULL deleter is a borrowed tensor); a refused call leaves `*tensor` untouched and never calls the deleter. The accessors `anira_tensor_data_f32` (host and pinned float32 only, `byte_offset` honoured, NULL otherwise; the `ANIRA_DTYPE_F32` doc names it: float32 is the only type it returns), `anira_tensor_data(tensor, dtype)` (NULL unless `dtype` is the tensor's own; never converts), `anira_tensor_num_elements` (1 at rank 0, 0 with a zero extent and for the dtype-0 record of a refused factory) and `anira_tensor_extent` (0 at and beyond `ndim`), and `anira_sizeof(anira_struct_id)` for allocators that cannot see the header: every record the headers define answers (eleven ids when the header landed; with `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX` of `anira/abi/processor.h`, `ANIRA_STRUCT_ENGINE_CTX` and `ANIRA_STRUCT_PLAN_QUERY` every id of `anira_struct_id`), every unknown id returns 0. `anira_sync_token_reset` / `anira_sync_token_dup` (`[thread-safe, !audio-thread]`: closing and duplicating are system calls) end and duplicate the descriptor of the two owning fd kinds, a plain zero and a plain copy for every other kind: an fd of 0 or more on POSIX (`close`, and a close-on-exec duplicate), an NT handle above 0 on Windows (`CloseHandle`, `DuplicateHandle`), nothing on WebAssembly, where reset closes nothing and a duplicate of an owning kind is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`. Tests: `test_Tensor` (`AbiTensor`, `AbiTensorSync`, `AbiTensorDlpack`: zero-then-fill of all thirteen factories byte for byte, the planar tensor, the NULL rules, fd ownership over a pipe, the bridge over the tests' own spelling of dlpack.h in `test/abi/dlpack_producer.h`) in `test_abi`; gate 3 pins 216/24/24 and every offset through the union arms, gate 4 covers the header from C11, C++17 and alone.
- **C ABI, tensor (`anira/abi/tensor.h`, `anira/abi/draft/tensor_platform.h`):** `anira/abi/draft/tensor_platform.h`, the first header of the draft folder, and with it the first non-empty `abi/symbols-draft.txt` (four names): `anira_tensor_init_metal`, `anira_tensor_init_iosurface`, `anira_tensor_init_ahardwarebuffer`, `anira_tensor_init_d3d12`, the factories of the platform arms that have no measured edge yet. The arms they fill are frozen with `anira_memory_handle`; the factories are declarations, not promises: a signature may change until its platform column is measured, promotion moves a name into `abi/symbols-0.txt` and its declaration into `anira/abi/tensor.h` and never renames it. No umbrella includes the header: include it by its own path. `anira_tensor_init_iosurface` takes its size as `uint64_t`, mirroring the frozen arm, which makes it the sixth name on the generator's allowlist of 64-bit parameters on `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` declarations. The link probe, the symbol baseline and the WebAssembly link treat the four names like promised ones: each has a body on every leg. The field-fill ledger of this pre-release, which the tag procedure checks: the bodies of `anira_tensor_init_cuda`, `anira_tensor_init_gl_buffer`, `anira_tensor_init_vulkan`, `anira_tensor_init_opaque_fd`, `anira_tensor_init_wgpu_buffer`, `anira_tensor_init_dmabuf` and of the four draft factories `anira_tensor_init_metal`, `anira_tensor_init_iosurface`, `anira_tensor_init_ahardwarebuffer`, `anira_tensor_init_d3d12` only zero the record and fill the descriptor, the arm and the fence; no adapter consumes those arms yet, the accessors read `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` and `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST_PINNED` alone, and nothing validates a handle. `anira_tensor_init_host`, `anira_tensor_init_pinned` and `anira_tensor_init_dlpack` are complete.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** the read-back: thirty-two `[main-thread]` entries that answer what the setters and the loaders stored, a file-loaded handle alike, appended after `anira_contract_from_json`. The tensor spec: `anira_tensor_spec_name`, `_dtype`, `_role`, `_ndim`, `_latency`, `_state_source` (value getters) and `_axis(spec, i, &tag, &extent)` (a hole below the rank reads `{ANIRA_AXIS_ANY, 0}`), `_window`, `_time_ratio` (status getters). The model config: `anira_model_config_num_inputs` / `_num_outputs`, `_input(config, i)` / `_output(config, i)` (the config's own copy of the spec, for the spec getters only, valid until the next `add_input` or `add_output` moves the specs), `_default_engine` (the pair: a status, the engine and its nullable id), `_state`, `_max_instances`, `_anchor`, `_tensor_name(config, model_index, canonical)`, `_model_ext(config, model_index, kind)` (the typed record of a known extension kind on one entry, set as a struct or as JSON; `NULL` for an unknown kind) as value getters, and `_tensor_layout(config, model_index, canonical, &count, axes)` with the count protocol of `anira_handler_get_latencies` (`NULL` axes asks for the length, a short array is `ANIRA_INCOMPLETE`, no record is count 0); `anira_model_config_model_provider` and `anira_model_config_default_provider` (below) read the provider pairs. The contract: `anira_contract_hard_host_blocks`, `_hard_budget`, `_hard_warmup`, `_hard_on_miss`, `_hard_miss_fn`, `_hard_wait_ratio`, `_hard_ring_dtype(contract, index, &canonical, &dtype)` (an enumeration in bytewise name order, beside the value getter `_hard_num_ring_dtypes`) as status getters answering `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async contract, and `anira_contract_edge_cost` for both kinds. The context config: `anira_context_config_threads` and `anira_context_config_log(config, &desc)` (every log field in the `anira_log_desc` of the setter, written within its `struct_size`). A value getter answers a no-value constant for `NULL` (0, `NULL`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE`, a `_FORCE32` value) and takes no `anira_error`; a status getter writes its out-parameters on success only, after the checks in the order `NULL` handle, `NULL` out-parameter, contract kind, index. Tests: `test/abi/test_ReadBack.cpp` (suite `AbiReadBack`); gate 4 (`header_c.c`, `header_cxx17.cpp`) covers every shape.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** providers are the engines' twins in the two-axis id: a provider the `anira_provider` enum does not name travels as a string beside it, `provider_id`, in the engine's own vocabulary (the runtime's name for a built-in engine, an entry of the descriptor's list for a custom one), NULL for one the enum names, exactly as `engine` / `engine_id`. `anira_backend_id` and `anira_plan_info` carry the tail field `provider_id`; `anira_engine_load_info` carries `provider` and `provider_id`, then `option_keys` / `option_values` / `num_options` with a reserved word at the tail (96/64 bytes): a provider is part of the loaded model, two providers of one model load twice, and a load that cannot serve the one it is asked for returns `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; `anira_engine_desc` carries `providers` / `num_providers` (the providers the engine serves, exactly the listed ones, the CPU path only when the list has `"cpu"`, as strings: the enum's JSON spellings or any custom name, copied into the carrier, refused at create when NULL with a count or with a NULL or empty entry; a caller whose header ends before them serves the CPU path alone) and the flag `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS = 8u` (accepted at create; honoured by the state pair, below). A model entry may be **pinned** to the provider its file is built for (an ExecuTorch export lowered for a provider (an ExecuTorch delegate), an ONNX Runtime .ort compiled for an execution provider): `anira_model_config_set_model_provider(config, index, provider, provider_id, err)` and the getter `anira_model_config_model_provider` (the pair: a status, the provider and its nullable id). The pair of an engine and a provider is spelled as two keys everywhere in JSON, `"engine"` and `"provider"`, as the C records spell it as two fields (`anira_backend_id`, `anira_provider_option_set`, `anira_plan_info`): a model entry pins itself with `"provider": "xnnpack"` beside `"engine": "executorch"` (`"coreml"` or `"cpu"` for the enum's spellings, `"com.example.npu"` for a custom name, written back by `to_json`; a neutral entry has no `"provider"` key and runs on any provider of its engine, the candidate deciding; `"cpu"` pins to the CPU path, and `"default"` and `"none"` are refused), and a `provider_options` set names its backend as `{"engine": "onnxruntime", "provider": "cuda", "options": {...}}`. A `:` in an engine word is refused naming the key; `engine:provider` survives as a label in messages and log lines (`backend_label` of `src/capi/words.h`), where every spelling lives in one vocabulary. Gates: `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` probe the new fields and the flag; `AbiModelConfig.ProviderPinAndItsRefusals`, `AbiJsonModel.AProviderKeyPinsTheEntry`, `AbiEngine.TheProvidersListIsCopiedAndAnswersServes`, `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionsRoundTripInCAndJson`.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** a default provider beside the default engine: `anira_model_config_set_default_provider(config, provider, provider_id)` (a provider of the enum, or `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with a custom name, the pair rule; `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` sets none; `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL config, an unknown provider or a pair against the rule) with the read-back `anira_model_config_default_provider(config, &provider, &provider_id)`, and the key `"default_provider"` of a model file beside `"default_engine"`, spelled as an entry's `"provider"`: the handler starts on the first plan in plan-table order of the default engine (of any engine without one) on the default provider, so a default engine with several plans starts on the plan of the default provider, not on the first of them. One rule for both defaults: what the configuration alone decides is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_create` (a default engine naming no model entry, as before; a default provider no entry of the default engine could run, every one pinned to another provider: "default_provider 'x' runs no model entry ..."); when no plan runs the default here (the engine not in this build or not a candidate, the provider not a candidate or not usable here) the handler starts on the default engine's first plan, else on plan 0, and `anira_handler_prepare` logs one Warning naming what was asked and the plan it starts on (for the default engine too, which fell back silently in the snapshots).
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** provider options travel as a context extension. The kind `"provider_options"` (version 1, on the context config; `anira_ext_provider_options` of `anira/abi/config.h` with `ANIRA_EXT_PROVIDER_OPTIONS_INIT`) carries one `anira_provider_option_set` per backend (`ANIRA_PROVIDER_OPTION_SET_INIT`: the two-axis id as `anira_backend_id` spells it, then `num_options` key/value string pairs in the runtime's own vocabulary, ONNX Runtime's execution provider option keys; both arrays read at the stride of their first record's `struct_size`, as the candidates of `anira_pipeline_add_inference` are, everything copied); in JSON `{"sets": [{"engine": "onnxruntime", "provider": "cuda", "options": {"device_id": "0"}}]}`, the pair as its two keys, as a model entry spells them, a set's `"provider"` required and never `"cpu"`, and every value a string, written back by `to_json`; in C++ `anira::ext::ProviderOptions` through `ContextConfig::ext`, whose minted record stands on its own. A malformed set (a count of options without arrays, a misspelled backend word) is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at the set call (`anira_context_config_set_ext`) and at parse; a set for `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` or the CPU path is refused (the CPU path takes no options). Each set is checked against the backend it names, per backend, not per kind: its engine must read the kind (the ONNX Runtime adapter, `"context:provider_options"` in the build's consumer table; a custom engine whose descriptor lists `"context:provider_options"`, which receives its backend's set in `anira_engine_load_info.option_keys` / `option_values`, C++ `EngineLoadInfo::option_keys()` / `option_values()`), else the set is refused as unconsumed naming the backend, at context create for a built-in engine and at handler create for a custom engine of the pipeline, and its provider must be one the engine serves here, else `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at handler create; a set for a backend no plan of a handler runs on is not an error. At prepare the handler copies the set of every plan's backend whose engine reads it into the loaded model's record (`engine::Model::m_options`, part of the pool key, so two contexts with different options for one backend load twice; the options of a set are kept in key order, so two sets with the same pairs are one key whatever their order, and they join a record only where its engine reads them), and at load the adapter hands them to the execution provider, CUDA's through `CreateCUDAProviderOptions` / `UpdateCUDAProviderOptions` / `AppendExecutionProvider_CUDA_V2`, every other through the generic entry's map. Gates: `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` probe both records; `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionsRoundTripInCAndJson`, `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionSetsAreReadAtTheCallersStrideAndCheckedAtSet`, `AbiContext.ProviderOptionsAreConsumedByTheOnnxRuntimeAdapter`, `AbiContext.AProviderOptionsSetForAnEngineWithoutAReaderIsRefused`, `AbiEngine.AProviderOptionsSetReachesARegisteredEngineThatConsumesIt`, `AbiEngine.AProviderOptionsSetIsCheckedAgainstItsBackendAtCreate`, `AbiEngine.CandidatesAreReadAtTheCallersStride`, `AbiCxx.ProviderOptionsExtensionMintsASelfContainedRecord`, and the record's key and the CUDA refusal with options in the engine-room tests.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** `anira_contract_set_host_domain(contract, canonical, domain)`, `[main-thread]`, common to both contract kinds like `anira_contract_set_edge_cost`: the declared host-end domain of one tensor, any tensor of either side, Streamed, Buffer, Static and State alike, by canonical name; `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` for every tensor never set. It is the domain anira allocates the ring, the model tensor, the Static store and the state buffers of the slot in, and the domain all four processor phases, the feed and the capture work in; the planner joins it to the engine's domain per slot with the edge of the registry's rows, and the plan report's slot rows carry it as `domain_in` of an input and `domain_out` of an output against the engine's domain on the other side. A processor carries no domain of its own: the ring, the model tensor and the tensors the context accessors fill share the declared one, readable from the `domain` field of any tensor an accessor fills or from the plan row. Resolved at `anira_handler_prepare`: a name that matches no tensor is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, and in this pre-release any domain but `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` naming the tensor (the declaration is data; the runtime allocates in host memory only). `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL contract, a NULL or empty name, or a domain that is no `anira_domain` value. In a contract file the top-level key `"host_domains": {"<name>": "<domain word>"}`, beside `"hard"` or `"async"` like `edge_cost`, the words the lower-case suffixes of `anira_domain` (`"host"`, `"host_pinned"`, `"cuda"`, ...). C++: `anira::ContractHandle::host_domain(name, Domain)`.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** the declared stream latency of an output on a Hard contract: `anira_contract_hard_set_latency(contract, canonical, samples)` names one output by canonical name and sets its stream latency in samples of that output, what `anira_handler_get_latency` / `_get_latencies` report for the slot and what its receive ring is primed with, replacing the figure anira computes (the adaptation, the queue, the wait credit and the model's internal latency); every output never named keeps the computed figure. `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at the setter for a NULL contract, a NULL or empty name or a figure above `INT32_MAX`, `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async contract; `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_prepare` for a figure below the model's internal latency (the spec's `latency`), on an input, on a non-Streamed output and on a name that is no tensor. The read-back pair `anira_contract_hard_num_latencies` / `anira_contract_hard_latency(contract, index, &canonical, &samples)` (the enumeration shape of `_hard_ring_dtype`), and the contract-file key `"latencies": {"audio_out": 4096}` in the hard object (non-negative integers, at most `INT32_MAX`; a version 2 document carries none). It is what the 2.x custom latency of `InferenceHandler::prepare(host_config, custom_latency, ...)` did, minus the silent clamp to the internal latency, and what the compat shim's two custom-latency `prepare` overloads stand on. Tests: `test_Handles`, `test_Json`, `test_Prepare` (`LatencyRulesAreConfigAtPrepare`: the four refusals, a two-output model whose declared slot reports 4096 while the other keeps its computed figure, and the 4096 primed zeros, popped in blocks of the declared 512), `test_ReadBack`.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** fractional host blocks on the Hard contract. The host blocks `host_block_min` and `host_block_max` are doubles (Changed, below), so a model with no stream at the host rate (every stream slower than one sample per host block) gets a fractional anchor block, the 2.x `samplesPerBlock / 2048.f` idiom: `contract.hard_host_blocks(samples_per_block / 2048.0, samples_per_block / 2048.0, sample_rate / 2048.0)`. The rules, in `anira_contract_create_hard`, `anira_contract_hard_set_host_blocks` and the contract file's `host_block_min` / `host_block_max` (any JSON number): both blocks 0 (unset) or both finite and > 0, `host_block_min <= host_block_max`, a host_rate of 0 or more; a NaN, infinite, negative or half-zero pair of blocks is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`ANIRA_ERROR_JSON` in a file) with a message naming the value. The core takes the block as the rational `LatencyCalculator::rationalize` recovers (1/3 and 1/4 exactly); a flexible window under a fractional `host_block_max` is pinned from the anchor hop like any other (Changed, below). The per-call rule is the latency calculation's accounting: by its k-th call the host has moved `floor(k * b)` samples of a stream whose block is `b`, so a call carries `floor(b)` or `ceil(b)` (0 or 1 under 0.25); a larger count is refused (Changed, below), and a pop within the bound that the stream cannot cover is `ANIRA_MISSED` (documented in `anira/abi/handler.h`, `usage.rst` section 1.3 *Fractional blocks*, `latency.rst`). Tests: `AbiContract.TheHostBlockRules`, `AbiJsonContract.FractionalHostBlocks`, `AbiValidate.AFractionalHostBlockIsTheCalculatorsRational` and `AFractionalBlockPinsTheFlexibleWindowFromTheAnchorHop`, `AbiHandlerTensor.AFractionalBlockMovesASampleOnceAWholeOneHasAccumulated` and `AHostAheadOfTheFractionalBlockStarves`, `AbiCompat.AFractionalBlockMatchesTheOracle`.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** `anira_vulkan_desc` gains the tail slots `int32_t device_index`, the index of the physical device anira picks when it owns the device (the record already ends in `void* device`, the VkDevice, so the slot takes the name of its sibling `queue_index`), and `uint32_t reserved` (zero), which keeps the record free of tail padding on 64-bit targets: a setter copies within `struct_size`, so padding a caller never wrote could not become a slot later; gate 3 asserts it (`padding_free: true` in the registry). `ANIRA_VULKAN_DESC_INIT` gains a trailing `0, 0u`. A Tier-2 append: a shorter record of an alpha.1 caller is read within its `struct_size` and the slot keeps 0. The JSON key stays `vulkan.device` and now reads and writes the slot, so an index set through `anira_context_config_set_vulkan` survives `anira_context_config_to_json` (alpha.1 kept the index beside the descriptor, which had no slot for it). A positional initializer that spells every member gains one (`-Wmissing-field-initializers`); the `_INIT` macro and designated initializers need nothing.
- **C ABI, context, core, thread and log (`anira/abi/context.h`, `core.h`, `thread.h`, `log.h`):** `anira/abi/context.h`, `anira/abi/core.h` and `anira/abi/thread.h`, the runtime half of section 4 and the user-driven threads of section 6. `anira_context_create` / `destroy` / `probe` / `capabilities` over this copy's core: the config is reconciled into the core the way sessions are (the first user's config takes effect whole; later contexts and sessions reconcile per field: log level most verbose wins; wait strategy, drain mode, queue capacity and interval first win with a warning; the thread count only shrinks, never to zero; once the last user is gone the next config takes effect whole), the config's sink is registered for the context's lifetime, its `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_DISABLE_PLATFORM_SINK` and `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_TRACE_FAILURES` are held while it lives (counted across contexts), a device block is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` in this pre-release. `anira_context_byte_image_bytes` (the dense encoding) and `anira_num_inference_threads` (`anira/abi/thread.h`: the core's pool size, 0 before the first handler); the queue and the pool are the core's, so `anira_drain_log` (`anira/abi/log.h`) and `anira_num_inference_threads` are the entries, with `anira::drain_log()` and `anira::num_inference_threads()` in `anira/anira.hpp`, and no per-context twin exists. `anira/abi/core.h` holds the steady clock `anira_now_ms` / `anira_now_ns` (`[callback-safe]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`) and the shutdown family `anira_shutdown` / `anira_release_core_if_idle` / `anira_has_core`. `anira_shutdown` (and the CLAP example's `clap_deinit`, which calls it) is effective only when no context and no handler exist in this copy of anira; otherwise nothing happens and it returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, so one client of a shared `libanira` cannot silence another's sessions; `anira::Core::shutdown()`'s unconditional form stays internal for the library-unload hook, and a plugin destroys every context and handler before the module-exit entry point runs, which it does anyway. `anira_enabled_engines` lists the engines the build compiled in, one row per engine on the CPU path, without a context (C++ `anira::enabled_engines()`). `anira_inference_thread_create` (taking an `anira_context*`) / `run_loop` / `execute` / `start` / `stop` / `has_exited` / `should_exit` / `is_running` / `destroy` over `Core::make_inference_thread`; `has_exited` is a new atomic set on every loop exit; `start` returns a status (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` while running, `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` when the operating system refuses the thread) instead of failing silently, and the scheduler's `InferenceThread::start()` returns the same as a `bool` (the pool start throws instead of spinning forever on a refused thread). Tests: `test_Context`, `test_Thread` in `test_abi`; gate 4 covers the headers.
- **C ABI, context, core, thread and log (`anira/abi/context.h`, `core.h`, `thread.h`, `log.h`):** the capability report over the Tier-2 records `anira_backend_id` and `anira_edge_info`: `anira_capabilities_backends` / `domains` / `ext_kinds` / `edges` / `edge` (stride-explicit enumeration: `out == NULL` asks for the count, a short buffer returns `ANIRA_INCOMPLETE`), Host rows only. `anira_capabilities_backends` reports one row per compiled-in engine and provider its runtime says is usable here, the CPU path (`ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`) first: ONNX Runtime's `Ort::GetAvailableProviders()` (the enum's value for `CUDAExecutionProvider`, `DmlExecutionProvider`, `CoreMLExecutionProvider`, `WebGpuExecutionProvider` and `XnnpackExecutionProvider`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with the runtime's registered name in `provider_id` for every other, the CPU provider being `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`), LiteRT's registered accelerators (a fresh environment's automatic registration, by the hardware they support: `"gpu"`, `"npu"`, `"webnn"` as custom providers, where the LiteRT library exports its accelerator query: every package but the Windows DLL, whose export list holds the public C API and the accelerator registration entries alone, so `cmake/engines/litert.cmake` defines `ANIRA_LITERT_ACCELERATOR_QUERY` for every other package, and without it LiteRT lists the CPU path alone and a named accelerator is refused at load saying why), ExecuTorch's registered and available delegates (Engines, below), LibTorch and TFLite on the CPU path alone in this pre-release. `anira_edge_info` carries the tail fields `to_provider_id` (a custom provider of the engine by its name) and, after it, `anira_edge_info.to_engine_id` (the custom engine's id on the rows of `anira_pipeline_capabilities_edge`, NULL for a built-in engine; `ANIRA_EDGE_INFO_INIT` holds its NULL, and a caller whose header ends before it reads the rows without it), and the registry carries one host edge per backend row: zero-copy with `ANIRA_RUNG_STATIC` to a CPU provider (the CPU path, XNNPACK), a host copy the engine makes for itself with `ANIRA_RUNG_IDENTITY` to a device one; `anira_capabilities_edge` matches a custom provider by its name (read when the caller's record has the slot). `anira_context_probe` asks the runtimes again (nothing is cached); `anira_enabled_engines` stays the compiled set on the CPU path. The adapters' own query, `anira::engine::builtin_providers` (`onnxruntime_providers`, `litert_providers`), is what the probe and the tests read, and `anira_handler_create` admits a built-in engine's provider exactly when the runtime reports it (CPU-only on the Linux CI legs, so every provider but the CPU path is refused there). A custom engine's rows are pipeline-scoped, since an engine belongs to a pipeline, not to the context (ids are unique per pipeline): `anira_pipeline_capabilities_backends(pipeline, context, element_size, count, out)` lists the context's rows and then one row per added engine and provider usable here, the listed providers the engine's query keeps in list order or the CPU path alone without a list (engine `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM`, `engine_id` the engine's id; every call runs the queries, the built-in rows are `anira_context_probe`'s), and `anira_pipeline_capabilities_edge(pipeline, context, from, to, out)` answers for a custom backend as `anira_capabilities_edge` does for a built-in one (the host edge: zero-copy to the CPU path and XNNPACK, a host copy the engine makes itself to every other, the reason saying the engine declared and measured it); the rows' strings point into the pipeline's engines and the context's store, valid until the pipeline is destroyed and the context's next probe. C++: `Pipeline::capabilities(context)` returning `anira::PipelineCapabilities` with `backends()` and `edge(from, to)`. The enumeration helpers of the context's entries live in `src/capi/enumerate.h`, shared with the pipeline's. Docs: `usage.rst` (a pipeline's capabilities). Gate: `header_c.c` probes the edge row's tails; `AbiContext.TheCapabilitiesListTheRuntimesProviders` (which replaces the snapshots' `HostOnlyCapabilityRows`), `AbiEngine.ThePipelinesCapabilitiesListTheCustomEnginesRows`.
- **C ABI, context, core, thread and log (`anira/abi/context.h`, `core.h`, `thread.h`, `log.h`):** log sinks. An `anira_log_fn` set on a context config receives every record the platform sink would, as the `anira_log_record` projection of `anira/abi/log.h` (`level`, `flags` with `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_REALTIME` / `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_CONTRACT_VIOLATION`, `dropped_before`, `sequence`, the timestamps, `group`, `message`), filtered by that context's level. Behind it one registry per copy of anira (`anira::detail::add_log_sink` / `remove_log_sink` in `src/utils/Logger.h`, one `thl::Logger` callback installed while a sink exists): two contexts in one copy are two sinks over one core, `anira_context_destroy` waits for its sink's in-flight calls and is refused with one Error record from inside that sink. The test collector (`test/support/log_record_collector.h`) is such a sink.
- **C ABI, lifecycle, processor and engine (`anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, `processor.h`, `engine.h`):** `anira/abi/processor.h` and `anira_pipeline_add_processor` in `anira/abi/handler.h`, which includes it: the one processor (the custom pre- and post-processing) of a C-created handler. A pipeline holds at most one processor (a second `anira_pipeline_add_processor` is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`); the processor owns every phase it fills for every slot and composes what it does not handle itself by calling the default bodies, explicitly, in its own code. A processor is a descriptor, `anira_processor_desc` (Tier 2, 104 bytes on LP64 and 60 on ILP32, no implicit padding; `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_DESC_INIT`): `struct_size`, `abi_version`, `user_data`, `consumed_kinds` (`"<host>:<kind>"` strings that join the consumed-or-fail walk and appear as `anira_plan_ext` rows whose consumer reads `processor`), `flags` (the processor's real-time promise: `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST = 1u` says `pre_process` and `post_process` allocate nothing, lock nothing and block on nothing, `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS = 2u` says the same of `before_inference` and `after_inference`, both in `anira/abi/enums.h`; 0 promises nothing. `anira_handler_prepare` checks the promise against the placement: under a Hard contract a filled `pre_process`, `post_process` or `reset` runs on the driving thread and requires the PRE_POST bit, else `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the flag and the phase (a processor that fills `reset` alone, or `reset` beside the hooks, is held to it as well); under an Async contract nothing is required; a bit the header does not define is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at add), then nine slots from the innermost level out. The four phase slots `pre_process`, `post_process`, `before_inference`, `after_inference` (`anira_processor_fn`, called as `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`, a typedef without a real-time attribute: the promise differs per processor and is the descriptor's, not the type's; an author who wants clang's check declares the function `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`); `reset` (`anira_processor_reset_fn`, `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`: the first chunk of a new stream, after prepare and after `anira_handler_reset`, before that chunk's `pre_process` and on its thread, with the chunk's context in the phase `ANIRA_PHASE_RESET = 7`, where the accessors answer the role only and refuse a ring or a model tensor; what a processor keeps between chunks starts over there); `prepare` (`anira_processor_prepare_fn`, `(info, user_data, out_prepared)`, called at the end of every `anira_handler_prepare`, after the plan report is built and while the handler counts as prepared, with an `anira_prepare_info`, the record of the new header `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` (which `processor.h` and `engine.h` include) that the engine's prepare receives too, a Tier-2 record of 72 bytes on LP64 and 48 on ILP32 without implicit padding, `ANIRA_PREPARE_INFO_INIT`: the handler, the plan report, `num_entries`, a template of the model end of every slot of either list, State tensors included, in the spec's dtype and shape at the pinned window with the slot's host domain, all-zero strides and no memory, the canonical names, the `flags` of the prepare (`ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE = 1u` of `anira/abi/enums.h` when the handler's inferences run one at a time and in order, a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair, with a reset at every stream start, 0 otherwise; informational for a processor, what an engine builds its per-stream state on) and a `reserved` word; the processor hands back a per-handler `prepared` pointer through `out_prepared`, which every phase call, the reset and the unprepare of that handler receive beside the registration's `user_data`, so what a processor keeps per handler, its per-entry scratch, lives behind `prepared` and never behind the `user_data` two handlers of one pipeline share; a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails the prepare with `the processor refused prepare`, and no unprepare follows); `unprepare` (`anira_processor_unprepare_fn`, `(prepared, user_data)`: once per successful prepare, at the next `anira_handler_prepare` once the old session is released and at `anira_handler_destroy`, after the last phase call of the handler; `ANIRA_PHASE_UNPREPARE = 8` names it); `init` (`anira_processor_init_fn`, `(info, user_data)`, `[main-thread]`: once per registration, at the first `anira_handler_prepare` of a handler of the pipeline, on that caller's thread, before that handler's prepare and before any model loads (a refused init throws no loads away), with an `anira_init_info`, the other record of `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` (Tier 2, 24 bytes on LP64 and 20 on ILP32, `ANIRA_INIT_INFO_INIT`): the `log_level` in effect in this copy of anira, `num_threads` (the size of the inference-thread pool, what `anira_num_inference_threads` answers; 0 when the context brought its own threads or on a target without threads), a `reserved` word and the `context` of the handler whose prepare reached the registration first, whose capabilities are legal to query, valid until init returns and never kept; it may allocate and log and call the `[callback-safe]` entries, never one that takes the lifecycle lock; what a processor keeps for its whole registration lives behind `user_data`, whether init filled it or the caller did before `anira_pipeline_add_processor`; a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails that prepare with `the processor refused init` and leaves the registration uninitialised, so the next prepare calls init again; `ANIRA_PHASE_INIT = 9` names it, beside the engine's `ANIRA_PHASE_LOAD = 10` and `ANIRA_PHASE_UNLOAD = 11`, and `web/src/abi/enums.ts` follows, with the two flag bits; NULL: nothing to initialise); and `release` (`anira_processor_release_fn`, `(user_data)`: once, when the last carrier dies, after every unprepare, whether or not init ever ran). One lifecycle, one slot order from the innermost level out (the per-chunk calls, `reset`, `prepare`, `unprepare`, `init`, `release`), one callback shape `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`, two shared records and one naming scheme on both descriptors: the engine descriptor of `anira/abi/engine.h` (below) is the processor's twin with one level more, its `load` and `unload` between `unprepare` and `init`, and the flag defines carry `FLAG` on both sides. The descriptor carries no domain: a processor works at the host end of every slot and never crosses one. The processor has no name: with one per pipeline a name identifies nothing (a record's ambiguity between handlers is the handler's, not the processor's), so every record about it says `the processor` and the consumer column of its `anira_plan_ext` rows reads `processor`; `anira::Processor` of `anira/anira.hpp` takes no constructor argument. The phases of one chunk: `pre_process` runs on the thread where the host end is produced (the driving thread under Hard) and takes the host end of every slot, the ring of a Streamed tensor, to the model tensor, chunking and every conversion included; on an inference thread anira feeds the State inputs, `before_inference` runs, anira crosses the edge into the engine's domain, the engine runs (`ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE`), anira crosses the edge back, `after_inference` runs, anira captures the State outputs; `post_process` takes the model tensor of every output slot back to the host end. A NULL phase slot means anira's default body runs (the window pop or push of every Streamed slot; a Static slot is materialised ahead of `pre_process` and captured behind `post_process` by anira itself). `anira_pipeline_add_processor` copies `min(struct_size, sizeof)` bytes into a refcounted carrier that the pipeline and every handler created from it share, so `release` fires exactly once, with the last of them. Every phase callback receives an `anira_processor_ctx`, the fourth Tier-1 record (64 bytes, align 8, `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX`, so `anira_sizeof` answers 64; rows in `abi/layout-0.txt` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts`): `phase`, the `engine` and `provider` of the plan the chunk was stamped with, `variant`, `num_inputs`, `num_outputs`, `ticket` (`ANIRA_TICKET_INVALID` under Hard), `entry` (the entry this chunk occupies, `0 .. anira_handler_num_entries(handler) - 1`, the same value in all four phases of the chunk and distinct across the chunks in flight at once, reused once the chunk completed: the index of a per-chunk scratch a processor sizes in its prepare function, where the handler counts as prepared, so that what `pre_process` pops or computes is found again by `before_inference` on another thread without an allocation), and four pointer slots: `frame`, an opaque pointer that is anira's, valid for the duration of the callback and never dereferenced by a processor, `engine_id` and `provider_id`, and one reserved slot that reads NULL. `anira_handler_num_entries(handler)` (`[main-thread & prepared]`, `uint32_t`, 0 while unprepared) is the count. The record carries no tensor and no ring: a callback asks per slot, the tensor's position in the model config's list of its side, through the context accessors (`[driver-thread | inference-thread] [callback-safe] ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, no `anira_error*`), each returning an `anira_status` and filling an out-parameter that it resets on any status but `ANIRA_OK`. `anira_processor_input_role(ctx, slot, &role)` / `anira_processor_output_role` answer what the tensor is, the `anira_role` of its spec, the first question about a slot the processor does not know from its model config (`ANIRA_ROLE_FORCE32`, which is no role, on a refusal). `anira_processor_input_ring(ctx, slot, &ring)` / `anira_processor_output_ring` hand out the ring of a Streamed tensor in the phase that moves it (`pre_process` for an input, `post_process` for an output). `anira_processor_input_tensor(ctx, slot, &tensor)` / `anira_processor_output_tensor` fill a borrowed descriptor of the model end of the slot, the tensor the engine binds: the inputs in `pre_process` (a Streamed or a Static input; a State input has no host end there, it is fed behind `pre_process`) and `before_inference` (every role, the State inputs fed), the outputs in `after_inference` (every role, the State outputs not yet captured) and `post_process` (Streamed and Static; a State output was captured ahead of it). The descriptor is built by the call over the memory the tensor has right now (an engine may swap it between two inferences), so a processor asks again in every callback and keeps nothing of it. `anira_processor_engine(ctx, &engine, &engine_id)` and `anira_processor_provider(ctx, &provider, &provider_id)` hand out the plan's two pairs (the id NULL beside every value but CUSTOM and its out-parameter nullable; a NULL value out-parameter is refused and latched like the six slot accessors'), reading the record's value fields and its `engine_id` / `provider_id` pointer slots. A slot always has a role; asking for a ring or a model tensor the slot does not have in this phase (a ring of a Static or a State tensor, an input ring outside `pre_process` or an output ring outside `post_process`, the host end of a State tensor in `pre_process` or `post_process`, a side outside its phases) is a bug in the processor and not an answer: a processor knows what a slot is, from its model config or by asking the role first, so the accessor answers `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` with the out-parameter reset (NULL, an all-zero record) and latches it into `anira_handler_rt_error` with one record per kind naming the entry, the slot and the phase, as a slot at or beyond the side's count and a NULL out-parameter are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, latched the same way, on the inference threads too; a NULL ctx names no handler to record into. Every ring accessor still takes a NULL ring and returns 0 for it. The two default bodies read the rings off anira's own frame and ask nothing a slot lacks, so a pipeline without a processor records nothing. Further accessors may be appended in a later version; the record's layout does not change for them. The rings stay inside anira: the new handle `anira_ring` and its ten accessors `anira_ring_dtype`, `anira_ring_num_channels`, `anira_ring_available`, `anira_ring_available_past`, `anira_ring_pop_block`, `anira_ring_peek_past_block`, `anira_ring_push_block`, `anira_ring_push_fill`, `anira_ring_discard` and `anira_ring_pop_windows` (`[driver-thread] [callback-safe] ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`); a data accessor states the dtype it believes the ring holds and, when it is another one, returns 0, moves nothing and records `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` in `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record (a channel out of range or NULL memory: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; a NULL ring: 0). The two default bodies `anira_processor_default_pre_process` (the receptive-field fill of the 2.x default processor, channel `c` of a tensor at `c` times its elements per channel) and `anira_processor_default_post_process` (the plain push) are callable from a processor ("call super"), real-time by construction, and return `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` for a slot whose ring dtype is not its tensor's, leaving it untouched. anira counts: after `pre_process` every Streamed input ring must have given up exactly its hop per channel and after `post_process` every Streamed output ring must have gained it, a shortfall being repaired (discarded, or topped up with zeros) and either deviation recorded as `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` with one latched record naming the phase, the tensor and both counts (the guard of a buggy processor); a phase callback that returns anything but `ANIRA_OK` puts its status into `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record naming the phase and makes the chunk deliver zeros at its stream position (a failed `pre_process` is not submitted, a failed hook skips the engine call or zeroes the outputs, a failed `post_process` is topped up). The context accessors and the two defaults carry the dual thread tag `[driver-thread | inference-thread]`: `pre_process` and `post_process` run on the thread that drives the Hard entry, the two hooks on an inference thread; anira's side is real-time whatever the flags say. `test_rt_contract.c` gains a phase callback over every accessor and both defaults, declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` by its author and handed to the four slots, and the processor's reset body; `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` pin the descriptor's initializer, the typedefs in its slots, the two shared records with their `INIT`s (`test/abi/generated/test_layout.c` their layout), the phase numbers, `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` and the 64 bytes; `AbiProcessor.ARefusedInitFailsThePrepareAndIsTriedAgain` pins the init rule, `AbiProcessor.TheRealTimePromiseIsCheckedAtPrepare` (the reset-only cases among them) the flag rule, `AbiProcessor.EveryPhaseHasOneWord` the words.
- **C ABI, lifecycle, processor and engine (`anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, `processor.h`, `engine.h`):** `anira/abi/engine.h` and `anira_custom_engine_create`, `anira_custom_engine_destroy`, `anira_custom_engine_detach` and `anira_pipeline_add_engine` in `anira/abi/handler.h`, which includes it: a custom engine as a refcounted object under a reverse-URI id of its own (the handle `anira_custom_engine`), added to any number of pipelines, the processor's twin in shape, lifecycle and words. A custom engine belongs to the pipeline, never a stage of its own: it is one more implementation the inference stage's candidates resolve to (legal before or after `anira_pipeline_add_inference`, whose doc says the same), it takes no place beside the pipeline's processor, and its `process` runs in `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` (`docs/anira-v3-architecture.md`, `anira/anira.hpp`: the spelling is `Pipeline::add_engine(impl)`, the id being the engine object's own). `anira_engine_desc` (Tier 2, 120 bytes on LP64 and 72 on ILP32, no implicit padding; `ANIRA_ENGINE_DESC_INIT`): `struct_size`, `abi_version`, `user_data`, `consumed_kinds` / `num_consumed_kinds`, `flags` (the engine's promises `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL = 1u`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_REALTIME_SAFE = 2u` and `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_DYNAMIC_TIME = 4u` of `anira/abi/enums.h`, declared and reported in this pre-release and consumed by later contract options, NEEDS_NO_MODEL already (Changed, below), and `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS = 8u` for the aliased State pair; a bit the header does not define is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at `anira_custom_engine_create`), then eight slots in the processor's order, from the innermost level out, and at the tail `providers` / `num_providers` (above) and `query` (below). `process` (`anira_engine_process_fn`, required, `[inference-thread]`, `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE`, `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`: one inference over the context's tensors, on the shared slot the context names (`ctx->instance`) or, under `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE`, on what the handler's prepare built; it may block and must not allocate per call). `reset` (`anira_engine_reset_fn`, `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`: for an exclusive handler (`ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` at its prepare), at the first inference of a new stream (after prepare, a re-prepare included, after `anira_handler_reset`), on the inference thread, with that inference's context and the handler's prepared pointer, right before its process; the boundary is the plan's, not the session's: after `anira_handler_reset` every plan of an exclusive handler is reset at its own first inference of the new stream, so a plan switched to afterwards never runs its executor on state from before the reset; never for a handler whose model is stateless, whose calls run on the shared slots and keep nothing between them). `prepare` (`anira_engine_prepare_fn`, `(info, loaded, user_data, out_prepared)`, typed `(const anira_prepare_info* info, void* loaded, void* user_data, void** out_prepared)`, `[main-thread]`: once per handler and loaded model the handler runs on, at `anira_handler_prepare` on its caller's thread, after the plan report is built and while the handler already counts as prepared, so its getters answer, with the `anira_prepare_info` of `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` the processor's prepare receives (the handler, the report, `num_entries`, the model-end templates, the canonical names and the flags of the prepare) and the loaded pointer; it hands back through `out_prepared` the prepared pointer every process, reset and unprepare of that handler on that loaded model receives (NULL is legal: a handler whose model is stateless needs nothing here); under `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` this is where the engine builds what it keeps per stream, its own executor (a session, an interpreter, a method) included, since the process calls of an exclusive handler claim no shared slot of the loaded model; of anira it may call the `[callback-safe]` entries, the handler's getters and the two Static entries, never prepare, destroy or a Hard entry; the prepares and unprepares of one loaded model never overlap (those of different loaded models may): a pooled loaded model is every equal handler's and a handler's prepare runs outside the core's lifecycle lock, so `Loaded::prepare` and a custom engine's unprepare hold a lock per loaded model (`Loaded::prepare_mutex`; the docs of `anira_engine_prepare_fn`, `anira_engine_unprepare_fn`, `Engine::Loaded::prepare` and the guide say so; `AbiEngine.PreparesOfOneLoadedModelNeverOverlap`); a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails the prepare naming the engine (`the engine 'id' refused prepare: it returned N (...)`), no unprepare follows, and the loaded model the failed session had acquired goes back, unloaded when it was the last holder). `unprepare` (`anira_engine_unprepare_fn`, `(prepared, user_data)`: once per successful prepare, at the handler's next `anira_handler_prepare` (once the previous session is released, before the new prepare; a later prepare that fails earlier unprepares the previous one all the same) and at `anira_handler_destroy`, after the handler's in-flight inferences drained and before the loaded model it ran on is released, and before that entry returns (Fixed, below); `ANIRA_PHASE_UNPREPARE`). `load` (`anira_engine_load_fn`, `(info, user_data, out_loaded)`, typed `(const anira_engine_load_info* info, void* user_data, void** out_loaded)`, `[main-thread]` under the core's lifecycle lock: once per loaded model anira pools, at `anira_handler_prepare`, over an `anira_engine_load_info` (Tier 2, 96 bytes on LP64 and 64 on ILP32, `ANIRA_ENGINE_LOAD_INFO_INIT`): the variant and the entry's `row`, a template of every tensor on the engine's side, State tensors included, in the engine's order at the pinned window, the name each slot binds to (the entry's `tensors` record, else the canonical name), `num_inputs`, `num_outputs`, `instances`, the shared call slots of this loaded model (the process calls that may run at once on it, each on its own instance below the count: the model's `max_instances`, clamped to the size of the inference thread pool, for a stateless model; 0 for a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair, whose handlers are exclusive and run on what their prepare builds, never on a shared slot: an engine sizes no shared executor for 0), the `provider` / `provider_id` and the provider options (above) and a `reserved` word; this is where the weights are loaded, the slots bound by the record's names (an engine that cannot bind a slot refuses) and the shared slots sized; it may allocate, read files, log and call the config getters and the tensor accessors, and must not call an entry that takes the lifecycle lock (context create and destroy, handler create, destroy and prepare, `anira_inference_thread_create`, `anira_shutdown`, `anira_release_core_if_idle`); a process that exits from inside a load exits with its own status (Fixed, below; the docs of `anira_engine_load_fn` and `anira_engine_init_fn` say so); it hands back through `out_loaded` the loaded pointer every prepare of that loaded model receives, every process and reset call sees in `anira_engine_ctx.loaded` and unload gets back (NULL is legal); a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails the prepare naming the engine (`the engine 'id' refused load: it returned N (...)`) and no unload follows; `ANIRA_PHASE_LOAD = 10` names it). `unload` (`anira_engine_unload_fn`, `(loaded, user_data)`, typed `(void* loaded, void* user_data)`: once per successful load, on the thread of the `anira_handler_prepare` or `anira_handler_destroy` that drops the last handler holding the loaded model, after every unprepare of its prepared handles and after their in-flight inferences drained, so no process, reset or prepare of the loaded model runs afterwards (for one handler alone a re-prepare unloads and loads again); it may run under the lifecycle lock (it does when a later plan of the same prepare fails and the plans loaded before it are unloaded on the way out), so like load it must not call an entry that takes it; `ANIRA_PHASE_UNLOAD = 11` names it). `init` (`anira_engine_init_fn`, `(const anira_init_info* info, void* user_data)`, `[main-thread]`: once per engine object, at the first `anira_handler_prepare` that reaches it (a row of the engine survives validation), under the core's lifecycle lock, before the engine's first load, with the `anira_init_info` of `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` the processor's init receives (the log level in effect, the size of the inference thread pool, 0 when the context brought its own threads, and the context of the handler asking, whose capabilities are legal to query until init returns); this is where an engine builds what it keeps for its whole life beside `user_data` (a device context, a thread pool, a weights cache); it may allocate, log and query the capabilities, never call an entry that takes the lifecycle lock; a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails that prepare naming the engine (`the engine 'id' refused init: it returned N (...)`) and leaves the object uninitialised, so the next prepare calls init again; the engine's init runs before the processor's, since the handler's session is created, loading its models, before the processor is prepared; `ANIRA_PHASE_INIT = 9` names it). `release` (`anira_engine_release_fn`, `(user_data)`: once, when the last reference to the engine object dies (its handle, the pipelines it was added to, their handlers, the loaded models), after every unload, whether or not init ever ran). `process` is required; every other slot may be NULL (a NULL `load` leaves `loaded` NULL in every later call, a NULL `prepare` leaves `prepared` NULL). `anira_engine_ctx`, the seventh Tier-1 record (64 bytes, align 8, `ANIRA_STRUCT_ENGINE_CTX = 12`, so `anira_sizeof` answers 64; rows in `abi/layout-0.txt` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts`): `instance` (the shared slot of the loaded model the call runs on, `0 .. instances - 1`, never two calls at once on one; 0 under `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE`, where no slot was claimed), `entry` (the chunk's position in the handler's inference queue, the processor context's `entry`), `num_inputs`, `num_outputs`, `ticket`, `flags` (`ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE = 1u` of `anira/abi/enums.h` for a call of an exclusive handler: it runs on what that handler's prepare built, no shared slot was claimed and `instance` is 0; an engine that reads `instance` as a shared executor checks the bit first, or reads what it needs from `prepared`, which is the handler's on every call; no other bit is defined in this pre-release) and two reserved words, then the pointer slots `inputs` (`const anira_tensor*`, the descriptors in slot order, State included), `outputs` (`anira_tensor*`), `loaded` (at offset 48: what this loaded model's load handed back; NULL for an engine without a load slot) and one reserved. The adapter rule, stated on the typedef: an engine reads every extent and every memory handle from the tensors of THIS call, never from a value kept at load or prepare, and never assumes a tensor is anira's own buffer; the call's descriptors carry the engine-side extents of the load record's templates over the chunk's memory, for an entry with a layout record too, as the promise of `anira_engine_ctx.inputs` says (the descriptor adapter's `DescriptorPrepared::engine_view`, one copy per shared slot sized at prepare, nothing allocated per call; `AbiEngine.ProcessSeesTheEngineSideExtentsOfALayoutRow`); a failing process lands in `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record, the chunk delivers zeros, a State pair keeps its last good value; a throw across the boundary is undefined. `anira_custom_engine_create(engine_id, desc, out, err)` (`[main-thread]`) takes the engine's id and copies it, `min(struct_size, sizeof)` bytes and the kinds into the engine object, whose handle holds one reference; the id names the engine for the object's life, in every pipeline it is added to, every message and every plan report row, so the id and the object cannot disagree (one engine under two ids is two objects, each with its own `init` and `release`, loaded twice, since the id is part of the variant's text): `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL id, descriptor or `out`, an id without a `.` or with the prefix `anira.` (checked before the object exists, so a refused id never calls `release`), a `struct_size` below the three leading slots, a flags bit the header does not define, a NULL `process` or a NULL `consumed_kinds` (or entry) with a count; `ANIRA_ERROR_ABI_VERSION` per `anira_check_abi`; a refused create hands out nothing and never calls `release`. The one `anira.`-prefixed id a host may create an engine under is `anira.v2.custom`, the id of a 2.x custom backend (a 2.x model file's `"CUSTOM"` row names it, and `anira/compat/v2.hpp` creates its custom backend under it); it is one `inline constexpr` in `src/capi/validate.h` (`k_v2_custom_engine`), shared by the JSON upgrade, the validator and the engine's create. `anira_pipeline_add_engine(pipeline, engine, err)` (`[main-thread]`) adds the engine under its id: one engine may sit in several pipelines; the engines of one pipeline have distinct ids, while two engine objects may carry one id in two pipelines and share nothing (two plugin instances that each create an engine under one id add them to their own pipelines and never share a loaded model, since the pool keys a registered engine's loaded model on the engine object, not the id, `engine::Model::operator==`); the pipeline and every handler created from it hold a reference, so the handle may be destroyed right after (`anira_custom_engine_destroy`, NULL-safe); legal before or after `anira_pipeline_add_inference`; a handler copies the pipeline at create, so a later addition does not reach it; an added engine no entry names is not a plan and not an error; `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL pipeline or engine; `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` naming the id for an engine whose id the pipeline already has, another object under the id or the engine already there; a refused addition takes no reference. `anira_custom_engine_detach(engine)` detaches a handle from the engine's lifetime: from then on the handle names the engine for `anira_pipeline_add_engine` only while a pipeline, a handler or a loaded model holds it (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` once the engine was released) and keeps it alive no longer; `anira_custom_engine_destroy` still frees it; for a caller that adds an engine to pipelines yet to come without keeping it alive itself. The plan report carries two tail fields for it: `anira_plan_info.engine_flags` (a registered engine's flags, 0 for a built-in engine) with `reserved`, and `anira_plan_slot.binding`, an `anira_binding` (`ANIRA_BINDING_POSITION`, `ANIRA_BINDING_NAME`, `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`) saying how the plan bound the slot to the engine's tensor; both `INIT`s read 0. At run time a registered engine runs on the C handler as a built-in one does: a model entry that names an added id (`anira_model_config_add_model_path` / `_add_model_bytes` with `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` and the id) is a plan like a built-in engine's, its levels called as the slots above say (`init` at the first prepare that reaches the object, `load` in the session's create under the lifecycle lock, `prepare` once the plan report is built), every inference one `process` call over the `anira_engine_ctx` the scheduler builds, a shared handler's on a claimed slot of the loaded model, an exclusive handler's flagged `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` with `instance` 0 on what its prepare built. anira never opens a custom row's path or reads its bytes: the engine's load does, through the config getters on the variant the record names. Two handlers share one loaded model when they run the same engine object on an equal variant resolved to equal tensors, whichever pipelines they were created from (the pool key is the model record, the whole variant as canonical JSON, since the engine's load may read any of it, and the engine object; two engine objects never share, even over equal descriptors; whether a handler is exclusive is no part of the key): a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair is loaded once too, with `instances` 0, and every handler of it is prepared under `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`, its inferences running one at a time and in order under the dispatch gate. A status other than `ANIRA_OK` from `init`, `load` or `prepare` fails the prepare naming the engine and the level ("the engine 'org.example.gain' refused load: it returned N (...)"), owing no unload or unprepare for the refused level; one from `process` fails the chunk as an engine failure does: `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` on `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record naming the status, zeros at the chunk's stream position, a State pair keeping its last good value. The plan report says `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with the id in `anira_plan_info`, the descriptor's flags in `engine_flags` and `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE` in every slot row of the plan; the extension rows of a registered engine's plan name it by its id, since the engine's `consumed_kinds` join the consumed-or-fail walk keyed by the id (its `model:` kinds read its own entries alone, its other kinds any host, while it is a candidate). Several registered rows on one variant are several plans (`anira_handler_set_plan` switches between them), resolved by row, never by the 2.x backend they map to. A custom row whose id is not added to the pipeline, `anira.v2.custom` included, is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at `anira_handler_create`, naming the id ("custom engine 'org.example.x' is not added to this pipeline (anira_pipeline_add_engine)", "custom engine 'anira.v2.custom' is not added to this pipeline"); the engine added under the id serves what its descriptor lists. Internally the engine room's `DescriptorAdapter` (`src/engines/DescriptorAdapter.{h,cpp}`: `DescriptorLoaded` over the carrier, whose `init` goes through the carrier's once-only `ensure_init`, with `load` and `unload`; `DescriptorPrepared` per handler, with `prepare` and `unprepare`) is the adapter over the carrier, the request of a registered row brings it to the core, and the validator's `EngineFacts` (`engine_facts`, the twin of `processor_facts`) carry the ids and the consumers. The doc of `anira_handler_reset` says what a reset re-initialises through the two reset slots; the registry's docs (`anira_model_config_add_model_path`, `anira_backend_id`, the head of `anira/abi/engine.h`) and the guides (`custom_engines.rst`, `usage.rst`, `migration.rst`, `benchmarking.rst`, `troubleshooting.rst`) describe the id at create. The gates: `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` pin both descriptors' initializers and slot typedefs, the three lifecycle records (`anira_init_info`, `anira_prepare_info`, `anira_engine_load_info`) with their `INIT`s, the 64 bytes of the engine context, the two flag bits, the phase numbers and the tail fields; `test_rt_contract.c` gains an engine process body declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, reading its tensors through the context and the accessors, with a `[main-thread]` function creating and adding it. Tests: `test_Engine.cpp` covers the create, the addition, the sharing and the levels (`AbiEngine.CreateRefusals` (the id's refusals, `anira.v2.custom` created, the id copied into the engine), `AbiEngine.AddRefusals`, `AbiEngine.ADuplicateIdIsInvalidState` (another object under the id, and the engine already there), `AbiEngine.InitOncePerEngineObjectAtTheFirstPrepareThatReachesIt`, `EqualConfigsShareOneLoadedModelStatefulOrNot`, `ASessionExclusiveHandlerHasNoSharedSlotOnAnyEngine`, `UnprepareAndUnloadOncePerPrepareAndLoad`, `ARefusalAtAnyLevelFailsThePrepareAndOwesNothing`, `AbiEngine.OneEngineInTwoPipelinesSharesOneLoadedModel`, `OneEngineOverTwoVariantsLoadsTwice`; the accumulator engine of `test_State.cpp` hands back NULL from prepare and reads its slots through `ctx->loaded`; `AbiPrepare.StructuralRulesAtCreate` refuses an `anira.v2.custom` row without an engine). The test rigs add engines to their pipelines: `test/abi/handler_support.h` has the pass-through engine (`passthrough_desc`, `make_passthrough`, `add_passthrough`: one object per pipeline, since the object is the pool's identity), which every `anira_test::Handler` adds under `anira.v2.custom` unless a rig hands it a `GateEngine` (a C engine object over a virtual body: the gate, a one-shot or a standing failure with the record the legacy adapter logged for a throw, the pass-through), and `SleepingParamFillEngine`; `test_State`'s `AccumulatorGate`, `test_Processor`'s `WindowSumEngine` and `copy_oracle.h`'s `ParamRampEngine` are gate engines (`oracle::settle` takes either gate), `test/contracts/unload/module.cpp` adds a static pass-through descriptor, and the custom plan's slots report `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`; both copy-oracle goldens are unchanged.
