- **C ABI, lifecycle, processor and engine (`anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, `processor.h`, `engine.h`):** a custom engine's capabilities are declared, measured and visible as a built-in engine's are. The descriptor's tail slot `query` (`anira_engine_query_fn`, `(const anira_init_info* info, void* user_data, uint64_t* out_available)`; `ANIRA_PHASE_QUERY = 12`) is the engine's own `GetAvailableProviders`: which of its declared `providers` are usable here, now, as a bitmask over the list (bit i: `providers[i]`; the mask covers the list alone, and a descriptor without a list serves the CPU path), run before `init` and any number of times, on the main thread, with the facts of the core; NULL means every listed provider. `anira_handler_create` checks every candidate's provider of the engine against the answer (a declared provider the query cleared is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`: "declares provider 'x' but its query reports it unavailable here (usable here: ...)"; a query that fails fails the create with its status, naming the engine and the phase), and every kind of engine is asked one way, `src/capi/providers.{h,cpp}` (`served_providers`: a built-in engine by the context's probed rows, a custom engine by its declared list through its query), which the provider-options sets are checked through too. The query keeps its `[main-thread]` contract: `anira_handler_create` asks each engine once (the plan check and the `provider_options` check share the answer) and keeps the answers per engine id (`anira::capi::QueryAnswers` on the handler, `QueryUse::Ask`), and `anira_handler_prepare` checks the plans against those answers without calling a query (`QueryUse::Reuse`), where the provider check (`check_providers`, `src/capi/handler.cpp`) of the snapshots asked again at every prepare, from every thread that prepared a handler, concurrently when two were prepared at once (TSan reported the race on a test engine's counter); so a custom engine whose query answer changes between a handler's create and its prepare is judged by the create's answer, and a new handler asks again; a built-in engine's providers are checked against the context's capabilities as they are at prepare. The pipeline's capabilities run the queries (above). C++: `Engine::query(const InitInfo&)` (virtual, the base answers every bit, a throw the status). The query slot's documentation (`abi/anira.yml`, `Engine::query`, `custom_engines.rst`: the `query` slot, "What a custom engine can serve here", the C++ `available` of the snapshots) and `anira_handler_prepare`'s say so. Tests: `AbiEngine.TheQuerySaysWhichDeclaredProvidersAreUsableHere`, `AbiCxx.AvailableIsTheEnginesQueryAndThePipelinesCapabilitiesListIt`, and `AbiEngine.TheQueryRunsAtCreateAndPrepareReusesItsAnswer`, which creates two handlers on the main thread, clears the query's bit, then prepares both at once on two other threads, three rounds: the query ran twice, never off the main thread, and every prepare succeeds; a third create asks again and is refused (against a prepare that queries, it fails and TSan reports the race in the query); the gates pin the slot, the entries and the C++ view.
- **C ABI, lifecycle, processor and engine (`anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, `processor.h`, `engine.h`):** the C++ face of the lifecycle in `anira/anira.hpp`. `anira::PrepareInfo` (one class for both descriptors, with `flags()` and `exclusive()`), `anira::EngineLoadInfo` and `anira::InitInfo`; `Engine::load(const EngineLoadInfo&)` returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Loaded>`, whose `prepare(const PrepareInfo&)` returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Prepared>`; `Processor::init(const InitInfo&)` and `Engine::init(const InitInfo&)` are virtuals with an empty base body. A `Loaded` subclass loads the weights and the shared executors in `load`, a `Prepared` subclass that `Loaded::prepare` returns per handler keeps `process` and `reset`, what it keeps per handler or per stream built under `PrepareInfo::exclusive()`, and `EngineContext::loaded()` hands the `Loaded` back to a `Prepared` that kept no pointer to it. An `anira::Engine` is constructed with its id (`explicit Engine(std::string id)`, read back by `id()`; there is no default constructor), and `Pipeline::add_engine(impl)` takes the object alone: a subclass passes its id to the base constructor (`MyEngine() : anira::Engine("com.example.my") {}`). The id is checked when the object's C engine is created, at its first addition: an id that is no reverse-URI name, or anira's own, throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` there and creates nothing; an addition refused because the pipeline already has an engine with the id throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, and a C engine the call created goes again, answered by `release()`. Tests: `AbiCxx.AddEngineRefusesANullEngineABadIdAndAnUnknownFlag` (the bad id before the C engine exists, another object under a taken id after it), `AbiCxx.AnEngineAddedToTwoPipelinesIsOneCEngine`; gate `anira_header_cxx20` pins the member-pointer types and `id()`.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** `anira/abi/handler.h`, section 6's handler surface. The config object `anira_pipeline` (`anira_pipeline_create` / `add_inference` / `destroy`: one inference stage, one variant, the candidate backends as `anira_backend_id` rows, read at the stride of the first record's `struct_size`, or, for a NULL list, the default set: every engine of the build on the CPU path, each custom engine an entry names and every provider a pinned entry names, on that entry's engine; an entry for an absent engine is skipped rather than refused) and the runtime object `anira_handler` over a context (`anira_handler_create` adds a reference to the context, copies the pipeline, validates the variant's structure and the named candidates against this build, counts as a user of the core, and is unprepared until `anira_handler_prepare` takes a Hard contract, validates the configuration against it, loads the surviving candidates' models, sizes the rings and builds the plan report; `anira_handler_destroy` releases the session and joins the pool with the last one; `anira_shutdown` is refused while a handler lives). `anira_handler_prepare` runs the processor's init before any model loads, checks every plan's provider against what its engine serves now (a context probed again since the create is seen; `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` as at create; a custom engine by its query's answer at create, above), validates the variant once, and publishes the session to the Hard entries and reset only after its last callback returned (the getters answer inside the callbacks; a process call that races a prepare, which the contract forbids, is refused with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_PREPARED` instead of reading a table under construction). The plan report as the Tier-2 records `anira_plan_info` / `anira_plan_slot` / `anira_plan_ext` behind `anira_handler_plan_report` and the stride-explicit enumerators `anira_plan_report_num_plans` / `plans` / `slots` / `exts` (dense-indexed, Host rows only, the wait strategy the core runs; `anira_plan_report_to_json` of the design document is cut). `anira_handler_set_plan` / `get_plan` (`[thread-safe]`) are one relaxed store and one load of one atomic, the dense plan index (`SessionElement::m_current_plan` behind `InferenceManager::set_plan` / `get_plan`), never the backend, since two plans may share an engine (two variants of a model, two providers), so two interleaved calls cannot leave `anira_handler_get_plan` naming one plan while another plan's engine runs; the plan table is the session's from create (`PlanSwitch.ThePlanTableIsTheSessionsFromCreate`), each chunk is stamped with the index at submission (`ThreadSafeStruct::m_plan`, Fixed, below), and the docs of both entries in `anira/abi/handler.h` say so. The Hard entries take host tensors and have their own entry below (`anira_handler_process` / `push_data` / `pop_data`, their `_multi` forms and the `_wait` twins): `[driver-thread]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, and every one returns an `anira_status`: `ANIRA_OK`, `ANIRA_MISSED` (the block's inference had not completed, the miss policy filled the memory, every delivered count is 0, nothing is recorded) or a failure, where every delivered count is 0 as well; never waiting: a block whose inference has not completed is an on_miss event. The `_wait` twins are `[any-thread, blocking]`: `timeout_ms` explicit, `ANIRA_WAIT_CONTRACT` = the contract's `wait_ratio` times the block duration (the call's block on the process forms, `host_block_max` on the pop forms), `ANIRA_WAIT_FOREVER`; the completion semaphore under `wait_ratio > 0`, a 1 ms poll otherwise; without an inference thread inside its loop the twin does what the nonblocking entry does and returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` at once, the count holding what that delivered. `anira_handler_get_latency` / `get_latencies` (index-aligned with the output list, one entry per output tensor, 0 for an output that is not Streamed), `anira_handler_get_available_samples` (a status, the count through `size_t*`; it collects the completed inferences for an output without a ring too, Static or State, as its documentation says, so a push-only host that polls a Static output through it sees the value change, the 2.x idiom `get_available_samples(0)` on an analyser; `test_OneSidedStreaming`'s analyser cases through `anira/compat/v2.hpp` cover it), `anira_handler_reset`, and `anira_handler_rt_error`, the last real-time failure recorded on the handler (a contract violation of an entry above, `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` (`anira::RtKind::NotSupported`) for a flag it does not know, a processor's or a ring accessor's refusal, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` after a failed inference, `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY` for a dropped hop (Changed, below) but never for an entry's own CAPACITY return; last-wins; cleared by prepare and reset). Refused at prepare in this pre-release: an Async contract, `ANIRA_BUDGET_MEASURED` and `ANIRA_WARMUP_UNTIL_STABLE` (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; the estimator follows), and a ring dtype that differs from its spec's while the pipeline's processor does not fill the phase that moves that ring (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`; a processor that fills it takes the conversion on itself, since nothing in anira converts). Tests: `test_Handler` (the in-binary oracle: the bundled gain model, and the CNN where LibTorch or ONNX Runtime is present, driven through the C entries, bit-equal with the 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` in one binary while that class lived), `test_HandlerWait`, `test_Prepare`, `test_RtError` in `test_abi`; `test/contracts/unload` drives the C entries; gate 4 covers the header.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** one plan per model entry and candidate, the provider in the pool key. The plan table of a handler is one plan per entry of the variant and candidate that names its engine and a provider the entry accepts (a neutral entry any, a pinned entry its pin alone), in entry order and, within an entry, candidate order, an equal provider once: one entry under `onnxruntime:coreml` and `onnxruntime` is two plans, which `anira_handler_set_plan` switches between; the uniqueness rule of the entries is one per (engine, id, pin), so two ExecuTorch entries lowered for two providers are legal and two plans; under the default set every entry is one plan, a pinned entry on its pin, a neutral one on its engine's home provider (Changed, below). A candidate's provider is checked for its syntax at `anira_pipeline_add_inference` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a value the enum does not name, a provider of the enum and a `provider_id` at once, or an empty `provider_id`) and for its availability at `anira_handler_create` (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` naming the entry, the engine, the provider and what is served: a built-in engine's provider must be in the context's capabilities, a custom engine's in its descriptor's `providers` list and its query's answer). The provider is part of the loaded model: the pool key (`engine::Model`) carries `provider` and `provider_id`, two handlers share a loaded model on the same provider and load twice on two, `anira_engine_load_info.provider` / `provider_id` say which one a load is for (the C++ `EngineLoadInfo::provider()`, an `anira::ProviderRef`), and every adapter refuses a provider it does not serve at the door (`Loaded::serves`; the descriptor adapter leaves the answer to the engine's load). `anira_plan_info.provider` / `provider_id` and `anira_processor_ctx.provider` report the plan's; the prepare log names it. `anira::Engine::providers()` fills the descriptor's list. The extension walk skips an entry no candidate runs (its engine, or its pin), through the validator's one rule (`engine_is_candidate`, `matching_plans`, `row_is_candidate`). Tests: `AbiEngine.OneRowUnderThreeProvidersIsThreePlansAndThreeLoads`, `AProviderTheEngineDoesNotListIsNotSupportedAtCreate`, `ThePoolKeyHoldsTheProvider`, `APinnedEntryRunsOnItsProviderAlone`, `AbiCxx.ProvidersAreTheEnginesTwins`; the `add_inference` refusals of `AbiPrepare` are the syntax rule and the create-time availability check.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** `anira_plan_report_find(report, const anira_plan_query* query, uint32_t* plan)` (decision 10.19): the first plan, in dense-index order, with every property the query names. `anira_plan_query` (Tier 2, `ANIRA_STRUCT_PLAN_QUERY`, `ANIRA_PLAN_QUERY_INIT`) holds a variant (`ANIRA_VARIANT_ANY`, `0xffffffffu`, matches every variant; 0 is the first) and an `anira_backend_id`: `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` match every engine and provider, a custom engine is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with its `engine_id` and a custom provider `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with its `provider_id`, each compared as a string. A record shorter than the caller's header reads its missing tail as the INIT holds it. `[thread-safe]` and `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, allocation-free over the report's rows, so a host looks the plan of a backend choice up on the thread that applies it with `anira_handler_set_plan`. A miss is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_FOUND` (-23, for every lookup that finds nothing that matches), with `plan` untouched; NULL arguments, a query below its head and a backend that breaks the pair rule are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`. In `anira.hpp`, `anira::PlanQuery` (an aggregate: `report.find({.backend = {.engine = ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME}})`, `report.find({.backend = {.provider = ANIRA_PROVIDER_COREML}})`) and `PlanReport::find(const PlanQuery&) const noexcept`, which returns `std::optional<uint32_t>`. Tests: `AbiEngine.FindLooksAPlanUpByEngineProviderAndVariant` (two custom engines on one pipeline, a custom provider given, absent or another, provider alone, engine and provider together, the variant, the short records, the refusals) and `AbiCxx.PlanReportFindIsTheFirstRowWithTheQuerysProperties` (every engine of the build against the report's rows); gate 6 and `anira_header_cxx20` call it from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** the plan report in the log. Every successful `anira_handler_prepare` logs the report it built as Info records of the group `anira.capi`, on the control thread: a head line (`plan report: plans N, input slots I, output slots O, selected plan P`), then per plan its row (variant, engine or custom engine id, provider, budget), one record per input and output slot (the tensor's canonical name, the domains, the edge and allocate classes, the wait strategy, the recipe, `bound by name` / `bound by position`, the reason when the row carries one) and one per extension it consumes (host, kind, consumer). One record per row keeps a line readable and well inside the synchronous logger's 1023 characters (these records do not pass the real-time queue, whose records hold 255). Visible when the context's log level is `ANIRA_LOG_INFO` or `ANIRA_LOG_DEBUG` (every level is compiled into every build type); compiled out under `ANIRA_WITH_LOGGING=OFF`. Tests: `AbiPrepare.ThePlanReportIsLoggedAtInfo` (its records asserted under `ENABLE_LOGGING` only, like every other record assertion in `test_abi`), `AbiPrepare.ThePlanReportIsNotLoggedUnderAnErrorLevel`.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** the Hard entries over host tensors, under the bare names of `anira/abi/handler.h`, which includes `anira/abi/tensor.h`: `anira_handler_process(handler, in, in_slot, out, out_slot, delivered)`, `anira_handler_process_multi(handler, inputs, num_inputs, outputs, num_outputs, delivered)`, `anira_handler_push_data(handler, in, slot)`, `anira_handler_push_data_multi(handler, inputs, num_inputs)`, `anira_handler_pop_data(handler, out, slot, delivered)`, `anira_handler_pop_data_multi(handler, outputs, num_outputs, delivered)` (`[driver-thread]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`) and the four `_wait` twins `anira_handler_process_wait`, `process_multi_wait`, `pop_data_wait`, `pop_data_multi_wait` (`[any-thread, blocking]`; one rule for the four: a `_wait` twin is its bare form with `double timeout_ms` appended as the last parameter: `anira_handler_process_wait(handler, in, in_slot, out, out_slot, delivered, timeout_ms)`, `anira_handler_pop_data_wait(handler, out, slot, delivered, timeout_ms)`, and likewise the two multi forms). They are the only Hard entries: there is no float-only family (a host that holds channel pointers builds one planar tensor over its pointer array with `anira_tensor_init_host_planar`, once, and stores `shape[1]` before each call; the borrowed pointer array is read at each call, so a host that rewrites the pointers needs nothing else, and one whose array itself moves between callbacks fills the tensor in the callback, which is legal there, the factory being a field fill, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`), no `_inplace` name (in place is one tensor handed over as input and output) and no second count protocol. **One slot space:** a slot is the tensor's position in the model config's input list or output list, State tensors included, and that one number is used everywhere: `slot`, `in_slot` and `out_slot` of every `anira_handler_*` entry (every single form names its index `slot`: `anira_handler_push_data`, `pop_data`, `pop_data_wait`, `get_latency` and `get_available_samples`), the positions of the arrays and `delivered` counts of the `_multi` forms, the arrays `anira_miss_fn` receives, the vector of `anira_handler_get_latencies`, the plan report's slot rows, and the `slot` of a processor's context accessors (`anira_processor_input_role`, `anira_processor_input_ring`, `anira_processor_input_tensor` and their output twins); `anira_handler_process` takes one slot per side, so a model with `data` at input 1 and `processed_data` at output 0 (declared state first in, last out) calls `anira_handler_process(h, &in, 1, &out, 0, &delivered)`. The role of the tensor decides which entries take its slot: a Streamed one the block calls (`process`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, their `_multi` and `_wait` forms), a Static one `anira_handler_set_static_input` / `anira_handler_get_static_output` or an element of a `_multi` form, a State one no Hard entry; a single form (`anira_handler_process`, `push_data`, `pop_data` and the `_wait` twins) whose slot names a Static or a State tensor on either side is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, so a generator with Static input 0 calls `anira_handler_set_static_input(h, 0, ..)` and `anira_handler_pop_data(h, .., 0, ..)`. A host that does not want to hard-code a slot resolves it by the tensor's canonical name at setup, and the plan report says what a slot is: `anira_plan_slot.role`, the last field of the row, is the `anira_role` of the slot's spec (the same in every plan), which a processor reads in its `prepare` through the report it receives; the slot refusals of the Hard and the Static entries name the tensor's canonical name beside the slot number in their log record. A host block is one `const anira_tensor*` per slot, an output's included: a descriptor is never written, the memory it names is, so a host builds its tensors once and reuses them. The logical shape of a Streamed slot is `[channels, samples]`, the sample count is `shape[1]` (no `num_in` / `num_out`), and the memory is planar (`ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR`: these are the entries that accept the flag), one block read by `strides` (contiguous `{samples, 1}`, interleaved `{1, channels}`) or one packed block (all-zero strides), for any channel count, copied straight between the host memory and the ring, one strided ring call per channel (a host with an interleaved device callback de-interleaves nothing into scratch buffers before it calls anira); the two sides of a call may be described differently, and one tensor handed over as input and output is in place. The tensor's dtype must be the slot's (the ring dtype of a Streamed slot): nothing converts, and another dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`. A malformed descriptor is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` (a rank other than 2, which includes the zeroed record a refused factory leaves, a domain other than `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` and `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST_PINNED`, `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY` on an output, `shape[0]` other than the slot's channel count, a negative `shape[1]`, and with samples: NULL memory, a plane count other than `shape[0]`, a NULL plane, a negative stride, a stride of 0 on an axis longer than 1 unless every stride is 0, a channel that does not start on a multiple of the element size), a flag bit this library does not know is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, and the check order is fixed: rank, domain, flags, shape, dtype, memory and strides, the input before the output. An empty tensor (`shape[1] == 0`, or generally a rank of 1 or more with any extent 0; its memory arm is not read, NULL is legal) leaves a slot out. The `_multi` forms take exactly one tensor per slot, Static and State slots included, and refuse another `num_inputs` / `num_outputs` (the lengths of the model config's lists) with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; a `_multi` call is *defined* as the sequence it abbreviates, over the same functions: `set_static_input` for every non-empty Static element of `inputs`, the streamed call over the Streamed elements, `get_static_output` for every non-empty Static element of `outputs`. The element of a Static slot is the whole tensor in the spec's shape and dtype, exactly what `anira_handler_set_static_input` / `anira_handler_get_static_output` take, or an empty tensor (another rank or extent is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, another dtype `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR` `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; there is no partial Static tensor and no count); the empty element of a Static or a State slot is a rank of 1 or more with any extent 0 (nothing else of it is read; a zeroed record has rank 0 and is refused), and the position of a State tensor must hold it, its `delivered` count always 0. Every tensor of a call is validated before anything is set or pushed, so a refusal writes no ring. `delivered`, single and multi, is a nullable pure out parameter written on every return: zeroed first, then `shape[1]` of each Streamed output and the element count of each non-empty Static output on `ANIRA_OK`, 0 on `ANIRA_MISSED` (a Static output's element count there too, below) and on a failure, and on the `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` of a `_wait` twin what the nonblocking stem delivered. It is never read: the request is `shape[1]` of each output tensor, so a miss leaves nothing to refill. `release`, `manager_ctx` and `acquire` are not read: the memory is borrowed until the call returns. A call that carries more than the declared block on a stream is refused (Changed, below). The latched log records name the tensor entry (`anira_handler_process: handler not prepared`). `anira_handler_rt_error` latches one more kind, `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` (`anira::RtKind::NotSupported`). Tests: `test_HandlerTensor` (`AbiHandlerTensor`) in `test_abi`: every description against absolute expectations (each channel its own ramp, late by the reported latency) for 1, 2, 3 and 6 channels, crossed descriptions, in place, the multi forms with a Static slot, the single forms beside unused slots, the three miss policies, every refusal with its status, its latched record and the check order, and every descriptor compared byte for byte around its call; the named comparisons that stand in for one oracle (the entries share the copy path, and a pass-through cancels a symmetric stride bug): a white-box look at the send ring after `anira_handler_push_data` under every description, a planar against an interleaved description over one script with missed blocks under the three policies, bit for bit (`AbiHandlerTensor.APlanarAndAnInterleavedBlockAgreeBitForBitAcrossMisses`), and an interleaved stereo block with the Static gain through `anira_handler_process_multi` on the bundled stereo gain model (`stereo_gain_with_custom()`), on every plan this build has an engine for and on the custom row (through a real engine that covers two channels; three and six channels run through the engine-free pass-through); `test_Prepare` runs its miss sequence over planar and over packed tensors, under `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` too (`AbiPrepare.TensorsBuiltOnceSurviveAMiss`), `test_HandlerWait` drives the four tensor twins (a deadline is `ANIRA_MISSED`, `ANIRA_WAIT_FOREVER` delivers); the `test_abi` suites call the entries through one helper, `test/abi/float_face.h` (`planar_f32()`, `empty_planar_f32()`, and `FloatFace`, which owns an `anira::PlanarFloatAdapter`), and `TensorStems.TheFloatAdapterNullsThePlanesOfASlotACallDidNotCarry` pins the adapter's rule without the C entries; `test_rt_contract.c` calls the six nonblocking entries from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function, `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` call one.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** the fourth miss policy, `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK = 3` (appended to `anira_miss_policy`; `"on_miss": "callback"` in a contract file), with the backup function `anira_miss_fn` and its setter `anira_contract_hard_set_miss_fn(contract, fn, user_data)` in `anira/abi/config.h`, which includes `anira/abi/tensor.h` now (`[main-thread]`; `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async contract, `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL contract, a NULL `fn` clears the pair). `typedef anira_status (ANIRA_CALL* anira_miss_fn)(anira_handler* handler, const anira_tensor* inputs, uint32_t num_inputs, const anira_tensor* outputs, uint32_t num_outputs, void* user_data) ANIRA_NONBLOCKING;` is the first `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function-pointer typedef of the ABI (`[driver-thread]`; a `_wait` twin calls it on the waiting thread). It fills a whole missed block, every slot at once and whatever the slots' dtypes: one call per missed block on the thread that called the entry, with the very arrays the copy path was handed, so nothing is built on a miss: the caller's own tensors under a tensor entry (a single-tensor form on a side with several slots passes the handler's array: the caller's descriptor in its slot, empty tensors beside it, which name no memory, since a staged slot is the empty tensor of prepare again when its call returns and no pointer of an earlier call stays in it; a float host's planar tensors reach it as they are, `AbiHandlerTensorMiss.AFloatHostsPlanarTensorsReachTheMissFunctionAsTheyAre`), and empty inputs in a pop; it is also called once per dropped hop (Changed, below). The input block of a process form is already pushed and still intact in host memory, in place too. Before the call the handler fills the non-empty Static output elements of the missed block with the stored value; the function may leave or overwrite them and has the last word on every output of the block, the get step behind the streamed call is skipped for that block, and what the function writes never enters the store. `ANIRA_OK` means the outputs are filled; any other status makes anira zero-fill every requested output. Either way the block counts as missed: the entry returns `ANIRA_MISSED` with a delivered count of 0 and the stream stays time-aligned, and a `_wait` twin without an inference thread still returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, after the function ran. The function must not call a Hard entry, `anira_handler_reset` or `anira_handler_prepare` of the same handler, and under clang a function converted to `anira_miss_fn` must itself be declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`. The two setters work in either order; the policy without a function is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_prepare`, naming `on_miss` (that is where a contract parsed from a file, which cannot carry a function, is caught: set the pair on the parsed contract first); the pair is copied with the contract and must outlive the prepared handler. Prepare also refuses `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` when a streamed output's ring dtype differs from the anchored input's (it cannot fire while every ring is float32). Scheduler: `InferenceManager::set_miss_hook(hook, ctx)` and a `case ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` in the starvation path of `process_output`, called once before the per-output fill; the accounting of a missed block is the same under every policy. Tests: `AbiHandlerTensorMiss` in `test_HandlerTensor` (the function declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, no assertion inside it; an allocation planted in it is reported by RealtimeSanitizer through `anira_handler_process`), `test_Json`, `test_Handles`, `test_anira_hpp`; `test_rt_contract.c` holds a nonblocking `anira_miss_fn` body that reads and fills its tensors through the `[callback-safe]` accessors and hands it to the setter, so `-Werror=function-effects` proves the conversion clean.
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** `anira_handler_set_static_input(handler, slot, tensor)` and `anira_handler_get_static_output(handler, slot, out)`, `[driver-thread]` and `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`: the whole-tensor entries of the Static tensors. A Buffer spec is not served this way: a Buffer tensor is a per-job payload that arrives with the Async contract, so `anira_handler_prepare` refuses a Buffer spec under a Hard contract with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, naming the tensor (the spec stays valid in a model config and a model file; a persistent side input under a Hard contract is the Static role). A Static tensor has one description everywhere, the whole tensor in the spec's shape and dtype (any dtype, the spec's; a Channel axis of any extent), over one block of host memory read and written by its strides (all-zero strides are packed row-major): the two Static entries, the element of a `_multi` call and the arrays of `anira_miss_fn`. The handler keeps the values in a store of its own, one typed buffer per Static slot, sized and zeroed at `anira_handler_create`, never resized, untouched by `anira_handler_prepare` and `anira_handler_reset`, so both entries are legal on an unprepared handler, a value set before prepare survives it, and a processor's `prepare` may call them. Each slot has a single-writer sequence counter over atomic words: a reader never sees a torn tensor and the driving thread never waits (under a Hard contract both sides of a slot are the driving thread; a host that writes from another thread breaks the tag and can make the driving thread retry). The processor runner materialises every Static input into the model's input tensor when the chunk is formed, ahead of the processor's `pre_process` (where `anira_processor_input_tensor` reads it), and captures every Static output from the model's output tensor behind the processor's `post_process`; a chunk that completed as zeros (dropped, or failed in a processor or in the engine: `SessionElement::ThreadSafeStruct::m_completed_as_zeros`) and a failed `post_process` capture nothing, so the store holds what the model produced last. `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS`, `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST` and `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` define nothing for a Static output: on a missed block a carried Static output holds the stored value (the latest the model produced), and its `delivered` count is its element count on `ANIRA_OK` and on `ANIRA_MISSED` alike (0 for an element left out). 'Fits' is a check, never a clamp: a slot out of range or a Streamed slot, another rank or extent (an empty tensor and a zeroed record included), NULL or misaligned memory, a negative stride, a stride of 0 on an axis longer than 1 unless every stride is 0, and `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY` on the output are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR` or an unknown flag is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; another dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`; checked in the order slot, domain, flags, shape, dtype, memory, and every refusal but the NULL handler is recorded in `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record. `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST_PINNED` is accepted. A non-float32 spec is still refused by the validator (the built-in engines bind float only), so a store of another dtype is reachable through the handle body alone until the typed chunk storage arrives; `test/abi/test_HandlerStatic.cpp` covers an int32 and an int16 store that way. A host builds the Static tensor once with `anira_tensor_init_host` over the spec's extents (`[1]` for the bundled gain models).
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** declared state passing, by pointer over two buffers per pair. A model whose state is explicit (an output tensor that is the next inference's input: an exported GRU hidden state, a streaming-convolution cache) declares the pair in its model config and anira runs the feedback itself; no `before_inference` / `after_inference` processor is needed for it. Both halves carry the role `ANIRA_ROLE_STATE = 3` (appended to `anira_role` in `anira/abi/enums.h`; `"role": "state"` in a model file) and the pairing is stated once, on the input: `"state_source": "<the output's canonical name>"`, or `anira_tensor_spec_set_state_source(spec, output_canonical)` in `anira/abi/config.h` (`[main-thread]`, no `anira_error*`, the shape of `anira_tensor_spec_set_latency`: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL spec, a NULL or empty name, or a spec whose role is not `ANIRA_ROLE_STATE`; the name is copied and resolved when the model is validated; `state_source` on an output spec, or a value that is not a string, is `ANIRA_ERROR_JSON` with the key path; `anira_model_config_to_json` writes the key on the input half; `gen.py --diff-against` reports the role and the setter as additions, and `web/src/abi/enums.ts` and `exports_wasm.txt` follow). A model with a declared state pair runs as `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` (an exclusive handler, one inference at a time and in order) whatever its `state` says. The handler owns the state: two buffers per pair, on the port of the State input, each in the spec's shape and dtype and starting on a 64-byte boundary, both zeroed at `anira_handler_create` and at every `anira_handler_prepare`; the session and the scheduler know nothing of it. On the inference thread the processor runner binds the model's State input to the read buffer (the state the last inference produced) and the model's State output of the pair to the write buffer as the first step of an inference, ahead of the processor's `before_inference` (which sees the read side through `anira_processor_input_tensor` and may read or alter it), and flips the pair as the last step, behind the processor's `after_inference` (which sees the write side through `anira_processor_output_tensor`): what an inference wrote is what the next one reads, and anira copies nothing (the engine binds the two buffers as it binds every slot: ONNX Runtime in place both ways, LibTorch's input in place and its result copied out, ExecuTorch, TFLite and LiteRT copied both ways, a registered engine as its prepare bound them, the one the `anira.v2.custom` row runs on included). Both steps are field fills without an allocation or a lock. A failed inference (an engine failure, a non-OK `before_inference` or `after_inference`) does not flip, so the read side keeps the last good state; a dropped chunk never runs and does not advance it. The state is the handler's and not a session's or a processor's, so it survives `anira_handler_set_plan`: every plan of the one variant runs the same declared State pairs (one `StateSlot` per handler and pair, in the spec's dtype and shape), and the next chunk's inference reads what the last one produced, whichever plan produced it. The pair's two buffers live in the engine domain of the plans that run the pair, the declared host-end domain of the slot (`anira_contract_set_host_domain`) overriding it; every engine domain is host memory in this pre-release, so one pair serves every plan and a non-host declaration stays `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at prepare; the bound State descriptors report the pair's domain. A plan whose engine's flags carry `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS` (`PlanSlot::m_state_alias`, read off the loaded model's flags) sees one stable buffer bound as both halves of every pair on every call and nothing flips: the engine updates the state in place and every address stays put across calls, what a captured graph needs; the one buffer is what a plan without the flag reads next, so a switch between an aliasing and a flipping engine keeps the state too. `anira_handler_reset` and `anira_handler_prepare` re-initialise declared state to zeros (the first inference of a new stream reads zeros), and `anira_handler_reset` reaches the processor's own state and the engine's own state through the two reset slots. The reset itself is one generation bump: the state is zeroed on the inference thread, at the first inference of the new stream (keyed on the chunk's dispatch generation stamp), so an inference still in flight across the reset cannot seed the new stream with its capture; the processor's `reset` slot runs for the first chunk of the new stream, before that chunk's `pre_process` and on its thread, and so it does for the first chunk after `anira_handler_prepare` (`ANIRA_PHASE_RESET`), the processor runner telling that chunk by its dispatch stamp, never by the session's atomic, which is why `Core::pre_process` stamps the chunk's generation ahead of the `pre_process` virtual, on the same thread, instead of behind it; the engine's `reset` slot of `anira/abi/engine.h` is the same boundary on the inference thread for an exclusive handler's prepared handle (`ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL`, a declared pair), run with the first inference's context right before its `process` (TensorFlow Lite resets the variable tensors of the handler's own interpreter there, a registered engine whatever it keeps; a handler whose model is stateless is never reset). A State spec may stand anywhere in the inputs list and in the outputs list, so the lists follow the model file's own tensor order. Validation, at `anira_handler_create` and again at `anira_handler_prepare`, `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the tensor: a State input without a source; a source that names no output, or an output of another role; a State output named by no State input, or by two; a source on an output spec or on a spec of another role; two halves of unequal dtype or shape; a Time axis, a window, a time ratio or a latency on a State spec; a ring dtype or the anchor naming one. `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` in this pre-release: a transposed layout on a State spec (a view layout prepares). A pair of another dtype than float32 is legal (its buffers never travel through the queue, whose storage is float32 in this pre-release): a registered engine binds what its prepare accepts (an int32 pair runs), a built-in adapter refuses it at prepare with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the engine and the tensor. A Channel axis of any extent is legal, as on every non-Streamed spec. Initial values other than zeros and host-readable state are not part of this pre-release. Tests: `test/abi/test_State.cpp` runs the engine-free twin of the example models' `StatefulAccumulatorNetwork` against its closed form, the same accumulator as a registered C engine (the pointer identity of the two buffers through a processor's hooks, a failed inference that does not promote, a reset that zeroes the read side alone, the state surviving a plan switch between two registered engines, an int32 pair) and the bundled file on every engine of the build (the pointer identity on each, the closed form, and on ONNX Runtime the pair bound by name under other canonical names); `AbiState.AnAliasingEngineSeesOneStableBufferAsBothHalves` (one address for both halves across the stream, the carried state in both before the write, the closed form in place, the domain on the descriptors, the reset), `AbiState.APlanSwitchBetweenAnAliasingAndAFlippingEngineKeepsTheState` (the three legs, the counter, the addresses at the seams); the registered rig takes an engine's flags.
- **C ABI, Emscripten companion (`anira/anira_em.h`):** `anira/anira_em.h`, the Emscripten companion of the C ABI (registry `platform: emscripten`, ABI 0.2, its names in the new `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` and in `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt`; an empty header on every other target). JavaScript reaches processors, engines and the log through the registry of the scope that runs the C call, `globalThis[Symbol.for('anira.registry')]` (`{ version, processor(Module, key, phase, a, b), engine(Module, key, phase, a, b, idPtr), log(Module, recordPtr), boot(Module) }`, `ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION` 2, the value the registry object's `version` carries), called with the instance's `Module`; the trampolines read only the scope's registry, never `Module.anira`. `anira_em_processor_desc_init(desc, js_key, hook_mask, processor_flags)` fills an `anira_processor_desc` whose slots are C trampolines into the registry's `processor`, one per bit, and whose flags are the stated `processor_flags`, never derived from the bits (a Hard contract's prepare refuses a pre/post/reset hook without `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`); the processor hook bits are one index space with the phases, `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*` is `1u << ANIRA_PHASE_*` (`ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_PRE` 1, `_POST` 2, `_BEFORE` 4, `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_AFTER` 16, `= anira::Processor::k_after_inference`; `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_PREPARE` 32, `RELEASE` 64, `RESET` 128, `UNPREPARE` 256, `INIT` 512), checked by a `static_assert` in `em_hooks.cpp`. `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_processor(pipeline, js_key, hook_mask, processor_flags, err)` adds it. `anira_em_engine_desc_init(desc, js_key, hook_mask, engine_flags)` fills an `anira_engine_desc` whose `process` (always), `reset` and `query` (`ANIRA_EM_ENGINE_HOOK_RESET` 1, `_QUERY` 2) cross into the registry's `engine`, its flags the stated `engine_flags`, and `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine(pipeline, engine_id, js_key, hook_mask, engine_flags, err)` creates the engine under its id with the flags JavaScript cannot write, adds it and drops the handle. The trampolines keep `{js_key, engine id}` in a heap record freed by the descriptor's `release`; a hook that throws is caught in its thunk, reported with `console.error` (rate-limited), and fails the call (`ANIRA_ERROR_UNKNOWN` for a processor, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` for an engine), so no JavaScript exception unwinds through a C frame. `anira_em_set_log_hook(enable)` registers the registry's `log` as a log sink beside the C sinks. The threads and the audio path have their own entries (Web). Implemented in `src/platform/emscripten/em_hooks.cpp`, compiled into `anira` on the Wasm binary format only; Emscripten gives every Wasm Worker and the worklet its own stack and thread-local block. The node gate `anira_em_smoke_thread` runs the host-driven loop on a Wasm Worker the test creates, with a C engine; `anira_em_smoke`'s hook object is a registry.
- **Engines:** ONNX Runtime, LiteRT and ExecuTorch take the provider of the plan; TFLite and LibTorch say why they serve the CPU path alone. ONNX Runtime appends the loaded model's provider to the shared session options at load, ahead of the CPU provider it keeps last: CUDA through `AppendExecutionProvider_CUDA_V2`, every other through the generic entry under the registered name (`CoreMLExecutionProvider`, `DmlExecutionProvider`, `WebGpuExecutionProvider`, `XnnpackExecutionProvider` for the enum's values, a `provider_id` as it is); it serves a provider exactly when `Ort::GetAvailableProviders()` lists it, never Vulkan, and a refused append is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` with the runtime's text. LiteRT takes an accelerator by its hardware's name as a custom provider (`ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM`) (`"gpu"`, `"npu"`, in the web build `"webnn"`; no provider of the enum names one) and sets the compilation options' accelerator bits from it; a name whose hardware no registered accelerator supports is refused at load naming the registered ones. ExecuTorch serves the delegates (its backends) registered to its runtime and available (`get_num_registered_backends`, `is_available`), which the capabilities list too (`XnnpackBackend` as XNNPACK, `CoreMLBackend` as COREML, `VulkanBackend` as VULKAN, any other by its registered name); a pinned entry names the provider (the ExecuTorch delegate) its export was lowered for, and a method that does not use it (the method meta's backends) is a mislabeled export, `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at load naming the pin, the backend and the method. Every adapter's refusal of a provider it does not serve carries the reason (`Loaded::provider_reason`): the runtime's own list for the three above, the pre-release's limit for TFLite (no delegate factory in the C API package) and LibTorch (a device needs the device-domain edges of a later pre-release). Tests: `AdapterOnnxRuntime.TheProviderIsServedWhenTheRuntimeListsIt`, `AdapterLiteRt.AnAcceleratorIsNamedByItsHardware`, `AdapterExecuTorch.TheProviderIsTheExportsDelegate`.
- **Web:** the TypeScript layer follows `anira.hpp` (decision 10.17): `web/src/` and `anira.hpp` are one layer in two languages, with the same class names, the same member words (camelCase for snake_case), the same return values and defaults; a difference exists only where the platform forces it (`Anira.create`, the worker module, `Context.ready`, the audio node, `measureRenderGeometry`, `fromUrl`, the MANUAL drain and log pump, the per-realm registry, `destroy()` instead of RAII, a thrown `AniraError` instead of a returned status), and each class's doc comment names it. `InferenceHandler.prepare(contract)` and `audioNode.prepare(contract)` return the prepare's `PlanReport` (`numPlans()`, `plans()`, `slots(plan, inputs)`, `extensions(plan)`, `native()`, over `anira_plan_report_*`, as `anira::PlanReport`), so `InferenceHandler.create(...).prepare(contract)` returns the report, not the handler: create, then prepare; `planReport()` reads the last one. The thread-safe members have their twins: `rtError()` (the status returned, not thrown), `setPlan(plan)` / `getPlan()`, `numEntries()`, and `handler.getLatencies()` beside `handler.getLatency(slot)`. `setStaticInput` is dtype-generic on the handler and the audio node, in the spec's dtype and extents: a typed array of the spec's element type, any view of exactly the spec's bytes, or plain numbers converted to it. `Context` says nothing of a host that drives inference itself: the web has no inference thread a host drives. The web tests, the examples (whose model-file check reads the plan report: one plan, the ONNX Runtime engine, the contract's 5 ms budget), `web/README.md` and the web pages of `docs/sphinx` use these names; the reference pages are `InferenceHandler`, `ContractHandle` and `PlanReport`.
- **Extras and examples:** the bundled models' processing as 3.x processors and engines, for the examples and the benchmarks: `extras/models/hybrid-nn/HybridNNProcessor.h` (`HybridNNProcessor`, an `anira::Processor` whose `pre_process` pops the model's 256 windows of 149 old and one new sample with one `RingView::pop_windows`), `extras/models/BypassEngine.h` (`BypassEngine`, the custom engine `org.anira.examples.bypass` with `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL`: the newest samples of each row of the input window into the output, the bypass of the CNN and of the HybridNN model) and `examples/benchmark/advanced-benchmark/ClearEngine.h` (`ClearEngine`, `org.anira.examples.clear`: zeros, the advanced benchmark's baseline). The CNN model needs no processor: anira's default fill is the receptive-field window `CNNPrePostProcessor` built.
- **ABI tooling and gates:** presence gates. `anira_abi_link` (the generated `test/abi/generated/link_probe.c` takes the address of every promised and draft entry point and links `anira::anira` the way a consumer does, on every leg that builds the tests, static ones included; the probe compiles as C, and only a UBSan build links it through the C++ driver, since the C driver leaves out the C++ half of the UBSan runtime that libanira's `vptr` checks need on clang 20), `anira_symbol_baseline` (`cmake/abi-symbols.cmake`: every promised and draft name is in the shared library's real export table, and nothing else, below), and the WebAssembly link on the generated `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt` (`-sEXPORTED_FUNCTIONS=@...`): a registry entry without a definition on Emscripten fails `build_web`. The sanitizer legs run in the merge queue (`v3`'s exists since 2026-09-20; the legs are stubs on a pull request): under RTSan the recording pre/post-processor of `test_PlanSwitch.cpp` has room for its records reserved at construction, so nothing allocates inside the handler's real-time `process` call.
- **ABI tooling and gates:** gate 6, seeded: `test/abi/test_rt_contract.c`, one `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` C function that calls every `[callback-safe]` entry of the two tensor headers, built as the consumer-shaped OBJECT `anira_abi_rt_contract` with `-Werror=function-effects` on clang 20 or later (plain C11 elsewhere): clang needs the attribute on every callee's declaration, so a generated declaration that loses `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, or a blocking entry called from a real-time body, is a compile error in a consumer's own build. The first of the consumer-shaped targets the handler PR announced; the processor, engine, miss-function and handler items above say what each later entry adds to it.
- **ABI tooling and gates:** `web/src/abi/layout.ts`, generated by `tools/abi/gen.py` from the same natural-alignment model as `abi/layout-0.txt`: size, alignment and every member offset of the Tier-1 records as `as const` objects under their C names, a member of a record type expanded in place with offsets from the outer record (so `anira_tensor.fields.handle.fields.host.fields.ptr.offset` is 152), `ptr: true` on an 8-byte `ANIRA_PTR` slot, `count` on an array. Prettier-clean under `web/.prettierrc` and type-checked by `npm run build`. A stated deviation from the design document, where a node run of the layout executable emits the file; that run will diff its table against `abi/layout-0.txt` instead.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** `anira::Context` (over `anira_context_create`, created from an `anira::ContextConfig`, with `capabilities()`, `probe()` and `byte_image_bytes()`), `anira::Capabilities` (the enumerations as vectors, `edge()` throwing `ANIRA_ERROR_EDGE_UNREACHABLE`), `anira::BackendId`, `anira::enabled_engines()`, `anira::now_ms()` / `now_ns()`, the free functions `anira::drain_log()` and `anira::num_inference_threads()` (the queue and the pool are the core's, so they are no members of a context), and `anira::shutdown()` / `release_core_if_idle()` / `has_core()` in `anira/anira.hpp`; gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes them. The name `anira::Context` was alpha.1's 2.x singleton (Changed, the core).
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** `anira::Tensor` and `anira::SyncToken` in `anira/anira.hpp`: the C structs with names and factories on them, no member added, so a `Tensor*` is an `anira_tensor*` (`static_assert` on size, trivial copyability and standard layout). `Tensor::from_host` / `from_pinned` / `from_cuda` / `from_gl_buffer` / `from_vulkan` / `from_opaque_fd` / `from_wgpu_buffer` / `from_dmabuf` over a `std::span<const int64_t>` shape are `noexcept` field fills that refuse like the C entries (the all-zero record, also for more than `ANIRA_MAX_RANK` extents); `Tensor::from_dlpack` throws `anira::Error` with the entry's status and message; `Tensor::from_host_planar<T>(planes, shape)` and `plane<T>(i)` are the typed spellings of the planar entries (the element type names the dtype, a const element sets `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY`); `data_f32()`, `data(DType)`, `num_elements()`, `extent(axis)`; `SyncToken::reset()` and `dup()` (throwing). `SyncToken` is deliberately not RAII: it travels inside a trivially copyable record and every hand-off is a transfer. The four draft factories have no C++ spelling while they are unmeasured (`anira_tensor_init_metal(&tensor, ...)` on a `Tensor`), and there is no `anira_all.hpp`. Gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes them; tests: five `AbiCxx` cases.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** `anira::Pipeline` (over `anira_pipeline_create` / `add_inference` / `destroy`; move-only) and `anira::PlanReport` (the rows of a handler's report copied into vectors; no `to_json`) in `anira/anira.hpp`; gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes them. The pipeline has one way to be filled (decision 10.18): one member per C entry, named as the entry without its `anira_pipeline_` prefix, each returning the pipeline. `Pipeline::add_inference(model, {candidates})` takes a model config with its candidate `BackendId`s, and its multi-variant overload is spelled and refused by the C entry (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`) until plan sets land; `Pipeline::add_processor(std::shared_ptr<Processor>)` adds the pipeline's one custom processor and `Pipeline::add_engine(std::shared_ptr<Engine>)` a custom engine (both below). An engine belongs to the pipeline, which the inference stage's model entries name by id, never to a stage of its own, and "stage" stays the generic word (the inference stage). A pipeline reads `anira::Pipeline p; p.add_engine(e).add_inference(model).add_processor(s);`.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** The handler half of section 6 in `anira/anira.hpp`. `anira::InferenceHandler(Context&, const Pipeline&)` over `anira_handler`: `prepare(Hard | Async | Contract | ContractHandle)` returning the `PlanReport` (a view, valid until the next prepare), `plan_report()`, `num_entries()`; the Hard entries as `noexcept ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` forwarders over `Tensor` (`process` over one slot of each side or over every slot, `push_data`, `pop_data`, each with an optional `delivered` out-parameter; no `float***` spelling: a float host builds its planar tensors once with `Tensor::from_host_planar`) and their `_wait` twins (`process_wait`, `pop_data_wait`); `set_static_input(slot, const Tensor&)` / `get_static_output`; `get_latency(slot)`, `get_latencies()`, `get_available_samples`; `reset`, `rt_error`, `set_plan` / `get_plan`; `native()`. `anira::InferenceThread(Context&)` over `anira_inference_thread` (`start`, `stop`, `execute`, `run_loop`, `should_exit`, `is_running`, `has_exited`) for a context without pool threads; `anira::log_rt`. `set_model` / `set_backend` arrive with the variant axis: select a plan of the report with `set_plan`. Both class names were alpha.1's 2.x classes (the 2.x handler is under Removed, the 2.x thread class under Changed); no class of namespace `anira` is defined both in libanira and in the header-only `anira.hpp`.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** Custom processors in `anira/anira.hpp`, over `anira/abi/processor.h`: `anira::Processor`, `anira::Processor::Prepared`, `anira::InitInfo`, `anira::PrepareInfo`, `anira::ProcessorContext`, `anira::RingView`, `anira::Pipeline::add_processor` and `anira::TensorSpec::state_source`; `PrepareInfo` and `InitInfo` are the records processors and engines share. A processor is a subclass of `anira::Processor`, the registration, handed to a pipeline with `Pipeline::add_processor(std::make_shared<MyProcessor>())`; a `Pipeline` holds at most one (a second `add_processor` throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`). The registration states the phases it fills (`phases()`, pure virtual: an OR of `Processor::k_pre_process`, `k_post_process`, `k_before_inference` and `k_after_inference`, `Processor::phase_bit(phase)`, read once when the processor is added; only those phases reach the `anira_processor_desc`, the others stay NULL, which keeps the C rule exact: a processor of `post_process` alone leaves the default `pre_process` running, whatever it overrides), its real-time promise (`flags()`, `virtual`, default 0: the OR of `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST` and `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS` that `add_processor` writes into `anira_processor_desc::flags`; under a Hard contract a processor that fills `pre_process` or `post_process` must promise the former or `anira_handler_prepare` refuses it with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` by name), `consumed_kinds()`, `init(const InitInfo&)` (`virtual`, the base body empty, may throw: once per `add_processor` of the processor, by the first `anira_handler_prepare` of a handler of that pipeline, on its caller's thread, before that handler's prepare, with the facts of the core in effect; what the processor keeps for its whole registration, a lookup table, a thread of its own, is built here as a member of the object; a throw fails that prepare with the mapping `prepare`'s throw gets, the registration counts as uninitialised and the next prepare calls `init` again) and `release()`. `prepare(const PrepareInfo&)` (pure virtual, may throw) is called once per `anira_handler_prepare` of each handler, after the plan report is built, and returns `std::unique_ptr<Processor::Prepared>`, the C lifecycle's per-handler `prepared` pointer as a class: the four phase functions `pre_process`, `post_process`, `before_inference` and `after_inference` (a `ProcessorContext&`, an `anira_status`, `noexcept`, no real-time attribute) and `reset(ProcessorContext&)` (`noexcept`; the first chunk of a new stream, before its `pre_process` and on its thread, the context answering the role only) are the `Prepared`'s virtuals, so what a processor keeps per handler, its per-entry scratch sized by `PrepareInfo::num_entries()`, is a member of the `Prepared` and never of the registration two handlers of one pipeline share. `PrepareInfo` is the shared record `anira_prepare_info` as views, the class an `Engine::Loaded`'s prepare receives too: `handler()` (the `anira_handler*`), `report()` (a `PlanReport`), `num_entries()`, `inputs()` / `outputs()` (`std::span<const Tensor>`, the templates), `input_names()` / `output_names()`, `flags()`, `exclusive()` (whether `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` is set: the handler's inferences run one at a time and in order, with a reset at every stream start), `native()`; `InitInfo` is `anira_init_info` as views: `log_level()` (an `anira_log_level`), `num_threads()`, `context()` (the `anira_context*` of the handler whose prepare reached the registration first, valid until `init` returns), `native()`. Why the ownership spelling: the `Prepared` is polymorphic and lives on the heap; `prepare` returns a `std::unique_ptr` because anira is its one owner from then on (the trampoline releases it into the C `out_prepared` on success and deletes it at the C unprepare, once per successful prepare, at the next prepare of the handler or its destroy; a throw destroys nothing that was not returned; a null return is `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL`), the registration is a `std::shared_ptr` because it is shared by the pipeline and every handler created from it. A processor that fills `pre_process` or `post_process` owns the phase for every slot and composes the default through the base class (`Processor::Prepared::pre_process(ctx)` is `anira_processor_default_pre_process`, `Processor::Prepared::post_process(ctx)` is `anira_processor_default_post_process`); the base `before_inference`, `after_inference` and `reset` do nothing, and the reset slot is always filled. A bit that names none of the four phases, a `flags()` bit the C header does not define, and a null processor, are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at `add_processor`. `ProcessorContext` is the `anira_processor_ctx` with the six context accessors on it, one C call per method: `phase()`, `engine()`, `provider()`, `variant()`, `num_inputs()`, `num_outputs()`, `ticket()`, `entry()` (the chunk's position in the handler's inference queue, below `anira_handler_num_entries`), and per slot `input_role(slot, Role&)` / `output_role`, `input_ring(slot, RingView&)` / `output_ring` and `input_tensor(slot, Tensor&)` / `output_tensor`, each returning the C status and filling its out-parameter (a refused ring leaves a `RingView` of no ring, which is false; asking for what the slot does not have in this phase is the processor's bug and is recorded, as in C). `RingView` is the ten `anira_ring` accessors over `std::span<T>`, the element type mapped onto its `anira_dtype` at compile time (float, double, bool, the fixed-width integers; nothing converts, so another type than the ring's moves nothing and returns 0 like the C entry); `pop_windows` refuses a span too short for its windows. Everything a phase function reaches is `noexcept`, allocates nothing and returns a status or a count. Lifetime: `add_processor` hands the pipeline's carrier a heap copy of the `std::shared_ptr<Processor>` as `user_data`, so the registration lives at least as long as the last pipeline or handler that carries it; `Processor::release()` runs once per `add_processor`, when that last carrier dies, after every `Prepared` of it was deleted, and the copy is deleted there (a refused `add_processor` deletes it at once and never calls `release`). A throw never crosses the C boundary: an `init` or a `prepare` that throws fails `anira_handler_prepare` with the status of an `anira::Error`, `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` for `std::bad_alloc` and `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL` for anything else, and `what()` goes to the log under the group `anira.hpp`, because the C callback carries no error record. `TensorSpec::state_source(output_canonical)` is `anira_tensor_spec_set_state_source`. Deviations from the design document's sketch, stated in the header: `Processor::prepare` takes a `PrepareInfo` whose handler is the `anira_handler*` (a processor of a handler created through C has no `InferenceHandler` object) and returns the `Prepared`, `phases()` and `flags()`, `input_tensor` / `output_tensor` in place of `model_input` / `model_output` references, `entry()`, spans in place of pointer and count, no domain parameters and no single-callable form, one custom processor per pipeline. Gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes the classes (the registration abstract, the `Prepared` concrete, both non-copyable, every per-chunk virtual `noexcept`, `init` and `prepare` not; `AbiCxx.InitRunsOncePerRegistrationAndAThrowFailsThePrepare` runs the init rule through a C handler) and, on clang 20 or later, compiles with `-Werror=function-effects` and calls every method of `ProcessorContext` and `RingView` from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function, so a method that allocates or calls a blocking function is a compiler error; API pages for the classes.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** Custom engines in `anira/anira.hpp`, over `anira/abi/engine.h`: `anira::Engine`, `anira::Engine::Loaded`, `anira::Engine::Prepared`, `anira::EngineLoadInfo`, `anira::EngineContext`, the shared `anira::InitInfo` and `anira::PrepareInfo` (above) and `anira::Pipeline::add_engine`. An engine is a subclass of `anira::Engine`, the engine object, constructed with its reverse-URI id (`explicit Engine(std::string id)`, read back by `id()`), added to pipelines with `Pipeline::add_engine(std::make_shared<MyEngine>())`, and named by a model entry (`ModelConfig::add_model_path(id, path)`); the plan is then `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with the id, every slot `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`. The object states its promise (`flags()`, `virtual`, default 0: the OR of `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_*` that `add_engine` writes into `anira_engine_desc::flags`, reported in `anira_plan_info::engine_flags`), `consumed_kinds()` (the walk keyed by the id), `init(const InitInfo&)` (`virtual`, the base body empty, may throw: once per C engine of the object, by the first `anira_handler_prepare` that reaches it, under the core's lifecycle lock, before the engine's first load, with the facts of the core in effect; what the engine keeps for its whole life, a device context, a thread pool, a weights cache, is built here as a member of the object; a throw fails that prepare, the C engine counts as uninitialised and the next prepare calls `init` again) and `release()`; the levels below are the C lifecycle's as classes. `load(const EngineLoadInfo&)` (pure virtual, may throw; `[main-thread]` under the lifecycle lock, so it may allocate, read files and log but must not call an entry that takes that lock) is called once per loaded model anira pools (two handlers running the same `Engine` object on an equal model configuration resolved to equal tensors share one, whichever `Pipeline`s they were created from, a stateful model included: it is loaded once, with no shared instance) and returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Loaded>`, the C `loaded` pointer as a class: the weights and the executors of the shared instances (`EngineLoadInfo::instances()` of them, never two calls at once on one) live there, and `load` binds the slots by the record's names, so every slot of the plan reports `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`. `Loaded::prepare(const PrepareInfo&)` (pure virtual, may throw) is called once per handler that runs the model, after the plan report is built, with the record the processor's prepare receives, and returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Prepared>`, the C `prepared` pointer as a class: `process(EngineContext&)` (pure virtual, `noexcept`, an `anira_status`, no real-time attribute; on `ctx.instance()` of the `Loaded`, or on this object's own executor when `ctx.exclusive()`) and `reset(EngineContext&)` (`noexcept`, the base body empty; the first inference of a new stream of an exclusive handler, with that inference's context, right before its process; never for a handler whose model is stateless) are the `Prepared`'s virtuals; under `PrepareInfo::exclusive()` the handler's calls claim no shared instance, so the `Prepared` builds the executor they run on and what it keeps per stream, a stateless handler's `Prepared` routes its calls to the `Loaded`'s instances, and nothing an inference needs is a member of the `Engine`. `EngineLoadInfo` is `anira_engine_load_info` as views: `row()`, `model()` (the variant's `anira_model_config*`), `model_count()`, `model_engine(i)` (an `anira::EngineRef`, the pair), `model_path(i)`, `model_bytes(i)` (the config getters, `noexcept`, empty for what an entry has not; anira never opens a registered row's path, the engine's load does), `inputs()` / `outputs()` (`std::span<const Tensor>`, the engine-side templates), `input_names()` / `output_names()` (the name each slot binds to), `instances()` (0 for a model whose handlers are exclusive), `native()`. `EngineContext` is the `anira_engine_ctx` as a view: `instance()`, `entry()`, `ticket()`, `flags()`, `exclusive()` (whether `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` is set), `loaded()` (the C loaded pointer: the `Engine::Loaded` the call runs on, which its `Prepared` holds already), `inputs()` (`std::span<const Tensor>`), `outputs()` (`std::span<Tensor>`), `native()`; every method `noexcept` and a field read, so a `process` composed of them and the `Tensor` reads is nonblocking (gate `anira_header_cxx20` calls them from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function, under `-Werror=function-effects` on clang 20 or later). Lifetime and ownership: the object is the engine's identity, as `anira_custom_engine` is in C. Its first `add_engine` creates its C engine (`anira_custom_engine_create`, under the object's id) with a heap copy of the `std::shared_ptr<Engine>` as `user_data`, and every later addition of the same object, on another `Pipeline`, adds that same C engine for as long as anything holds it (a `Pipeline`, a handler created from one, a loaded model in the pool): the object keeps a detached handle of its C engine (`anira_custom_engine_detach`), which names it while anything holds it and keeps nothing alive, so nothing cycles; a registration that lost the race against the C engine's release creates a fresh one, and `Pipeline` holds no handles of its own, so a `Pipeline` destroyed while a handler created from it lives leaves the object its one C engine (one init, no release while the object is in use, one shared loaded model; `AbiCxx.AHandlerOutlivingItsPipelineKeepsTheObjectsCEngine`). The plugin-instance idiom is one object per binary behind a function-local `std::weak_ptr`. The C engine keeps the object alive at least as long as the last pipeline or handler that carries it; `Engine::release()` runs once per C engine, when its last reference dies, after every `Loaded` of it was deleted, whether or not `init` ever ran, and an addition after that creates a fresh C engine; `load` and `prepare` return a `std::unique_ptr` because anira is their one owner from then on (the trampoline releases the `Loaded` into the C `out_loaded` and deletes it at the C unload, when the last handler holding the model is re-prepared or destroyed, after every `Prepared` over it; the `Prepared` into `out_prepared`, deleted at the C unprepare, once per successful prepare, at the handler's next prepare or its destroy, after its in-flight inferences drained); a throw never crosses the C boundary (from `init`, `load` and `prepare` alike: an `anira::Error` its status, `std::bad_alloc` `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`, anything else `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL`, `what()` to the log under the group `anira.hpp`, a null return `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL`), and the handler's prepare fails naming the engine and the level. `add_engine` throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a null engine before the C call and what `anira_custom_engine_create` and `anira_pipeline_add_engine` refuse (an id without a `.` or with the prefix `anira.`, a `flags()` bit `anira/abi/enums.h` does not define: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, before the C engine exists; an engine whose id the pipeline already has: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`), keeping no copy; a refusal after the call created the object's C engine drops that C engine again, and `release()` answers it. Gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes the classes (the three abstract, non-copyable and non-movable, `process` and `reset` `noexcept`, `init`, `load` and `prepare` not, the member-pointer type of `add_engine`, `EngineKind` the enum); `AbiCxx` runs a C++ engine end to end through a C handler (the records, the context, the plan report, the consumed extension under the id, the exception mapping of every level, one `Loaded` per pooled model and one `Prepared` per handler deleted once at the re-prepare or the destroy of its handler, `reset` at every new stream: `AThrowingEngineLoadOrPrepareFailsTheHandlersPrepare`, `InitRunsOncePerRegistrationAndAThrowFailsThePrepare`, `APreparedPerHandlerDiesWithItsUnprepareAndResetSeesEveryNewStream`, `OneEngineObjectOnTwoPipelinesSharesOneLoadedModel`); API pages for the classes.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** The read-back in `anira/anira.hpp`, over the getters of `anira/abi/config.h`. `anira::SpecView`, a trivially copyable view of a tensor spec whose every read is `noexcept` (`name()`, `dtype()`, `role()`, `ndim()`, `axis(k)` as `{tag, extent}` with `{ANIRA_AXIS_ANY, 0}` for a hole and at or beyond `ndim()`, `window()` as `{min, max, overlap}`, `time_ratio()` as `{num, den}`, `latency()`, `state_source()`), handed out by `TensorSpec::view()` and by `ModelConfig::input_spec(i)` / `output_spec(i)` (the config's own copy, valid until the config is mutated, moved or destroyed). The `ModelConfig` getters `input_count()`, `output_count()`, `default_engine()` (an `anira::EngineRef`), `state()`, `max_instances()`, `anchor()` (`noexcept`), `tensor_name(i, canonical)`, `tensor_layout(i, canonical)` and `model_ext<Ext>(i)` (`std::optional<Ext>`; `detail::ExtTraits<ext::Entry>` gains `k_kind` and `read`), the indexed ones throwing `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` out of range like `model_path(i)`. `anira::Hard` gains `anira::Hard::miss_fn` / `miss_user_data` after `on_miss` (minted through `anira_contract_hard_set_miss_fn`; a raw function pointer called on the driver thread, which, with what `miss_user_data` points at, outlives the prepared handler and under clang is declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`), and `ring_dtypes` (`std::map<std::string, DType>`) and `latencies` (`std::map<std::string, uint32_t>`) between `wait_ratio` and `edge_cost`, still an aggregate, minted through `anira_contract_hard_set_ring_dtype` / `_set_latency`; `anira::ContractHandle::hard_miss_fn(fn, user_data)` and `ContractHandle::hard_latency(canonical, samples)` sit beside `hard_ring_dtype`; and `ContractHandle::hard()` reads a Hard handle back into the aggregate, a loaded or legacy one alike, the budget as an `anira::Budget` whose value is rounded to the nanosecond (`std::chrono::round`: the handle stores double milliseconds, and a `duration_cast` would truncate 4.1 ms to 4099999 ns), throwing `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async handle. The alpha.1 deviations that a handle could not be read back into a `Hard` aggregate (`take_legacy_contract`, a loaded contract file) are closed. Tests: `test_ReadBack.cpp` (suite `AbiReadBackCxx`), `test_anira_hpp`'s `ContractHandleMintsAHardAggregate` reads through `hard()`; gate 4 (`header_cxx20.cpp`) pins the view, the getters, the maps and a designated initializer that names them.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`, not ABI-stable):** The provider beside the engine in `anira/anira.hpp`: `ModelConfig::default_provider(provider)` and `default_provider(name)` (a custom provider by its name) with the getter `default_provider()`; the pin getter `ModelConfig::model_provider(i)` beside `model_engine(i)`, the setter `model_provider(i, provider)` and `model_provider(i, name)` (a pin to a custom provider by its name). Every getter of a pair returns it whole, `anira::EngineRef{kind, id}` / `anira::ProviderRef{kind, id}` (trivially copyable aggregates; `id` empty beside every value but CUSTOM): `ModelConfig::model_engine(i)`, `default_engine()`, `model_provider(i)`, `default_provider()`, `EngineLoadInfo::model_engine(i)` and `provider()`, `ProcessorContext::engine()` and `provider()` over the two processor accessors.
- **Compat (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`, C++ header-only):** `anira/compat/v2.hpp`, the 2.x source-compatible face of anira over the 3.x C ABI: `namespace anira::v2`, C++20 over `anira/anira.hpp` and the `anira/abi` headers, nothing of the 2.x tree, the names of v2.3.0. A 2.x host includes it in place of `<anira/anira.h>`, adds `using namespace anira::v2;` and runs on the C handler. The configuration half: `InferenceBackend` under the 2.x names with the `anira_engine` values (every enumerator in every build; `to_engine` / `to_backend` / `names` / `is_available`; `CUSTOM` is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with `k_custom_engine_id`, `"anira.v2.custom"`), `ModelData`, `TensorShape`, `ProcessingSpec`, `InferenceConfig` (a copyable value whose 2.x constructors write a version-2 document and load it through `anira::ModelConfig::from_json`, the one upgrade path, then read the 2.x fields and getters back from the handles; the backend-qualified shape getter applies the entry's axis layout; `model_config()`, `model_config_copy()`, `hard()`), `JsonConfigLoader`, `ContextConfig` (`to_context_config()`), `HostConfig`, `LogConfig` and the log enums. The processor half: `RingBuffer` and `BufferF` as views of a slot's ring and model tensor, `PrePostProcessor` with the 2.x virtuals, bodies, atomics and helpers, `LegacyProcessor` (a 2.x processor as the `anira::Processor` of a 3.x pipeline), `PassthroughEngine` (the 2.x `BackendBase::process` as an `anira::Engine` under `anira.v2.custom`) and the `Context` statics. The handler: `InferenceHandler` over one `anira_handler` built from a private copy of the model config, the custom engine (the caller's `anira::Engine&` or the `PassthroughEngine`) and the `LegacyProcessor`, with every 2.x member under its 2.x signature: `prepare` over the legacy contract with the host blocks and the reference tensor as the anchor (an unchanged prepare only resets the stream), the two custom-latency forms over `anira_contract_hard_set_latency`, the process, push and pop forms over planar float tensors and the `_multi` entries and their `_wait` twins, the non-streamable values through the processor's atomics, `set_inference_backend` over the plan report; `native()` and `rt_error()` are additions. `anira::v2::InferenceHandler::prepare` accepts a fractional `HostConfig::m_buffer_size` as 2.x did and passes it through to the Hard host blocks (`host_block_min` is half the block when smaller blocks come and the block is one sample or less); a block that is not finite and above 0 is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` (`AbiCompat.ANonPositiveHostConfigIsRefused`). `InferenceHandler::set_inference_backend` and `get_inference_backend` are callable from any thread while audio runs, but **not while `prepare()` or the destructor runs** on another thread, the rule `anira_handler_set_plan` states: `prepare` rebuilds the plan table and may replace the C handler they read, so a concurrent call races it (#202). 2.x promised the switch while audio runs, never alongside `prepare`; a host that switches from a UI thread and re-prepares from another serialises the two; the class comment, both members and the compat page of `migration.rst` say so. What differs from 2.x (errors are `anira::Error`; nothing loads before `prepare`; single forms carry one slot; `push_data` never waits; the engine code is the host's; the not-source-compatible members) is the compat page of `docs/sphinx/migration.rst`. Tests: `test/abi/test_Compat.cpp` (suites `AbiCompatConfig`, `AbiCompat`, `AbiCompatForm`), first a differential proof against the live 2.x classes on the same arguments (every getter, the loader, every plan through the five forms, the extras' CNN and HybridNN processors copied verbatim, the Static values, misses, latencies, waits), whose arms check their 3.x expectations since the 2.x classes left, and the shim's own rules; the consumer gates `anira_header_compat` (the shim alone under the strict flags, `-Werror=function-effects` on clang 20 or later over an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` probe) and `anira_header_compat_with_hpp` (the C umbrella `<anira/anira.h>`, `anira.hpp` and the shim in one TU, both orders); the install consumer `consumer_compat`. The api pages of every `anira::v2` class and of `anira::SpecView`, `anira::EngineRef` and `anira::ProviderRef` are in `docs/sphinx/api`.
- **Web (module, `anira/anira_em.h`):** the context's inference threads are Wasm Workers anira starts, as natively they are OS threads. `InferenceThread::start()` creates a Wasm Worker on the module's shared memory (`src/platform/emscripten/em_worker.cpp`: a 4 MiB stack and the thread-local block, from the heap), which imports the context's worker module, awaits the boot of its scope's registry and then runs the loop; `stop()` waits for it to leave the loop (a Worker still importing never enters it) and terminates it. So `anira_context_config_set_threads` and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` (half the cores, now on WebAssembly too: `default_num_threads()`) size a real pool on the web, and `anira_inference_thread_start` starts a Wasm Worker for a user-driven thread.
