Two `anira/anira_em.h` entries: `anira_em_context_config_set_worker_module(config, url, err)`, the one web-only field of a context config (the first context's module is the pool's, a later one naming another is warned about), and `anira_em_inference_threads_in_loop()`, the threads inside their loops now, which a page waits for before it waits for inferences on the main thread (a Worker starts only once the main thread yields). `InferenceThread::any_loop_active()` counts a started Wasm Worker during its import, so a wait right after the prepare is not refused. An idle Worker parks instead of spinning a core: `InferenceThread::notify_work()`, the wake word, runs after every enqueue into the inference queue (`Core::enqueue_inference_or_drop`, a stateful session's next dispatch, the drain's requeue) and in `request_stop()` / `stop()`; on WebAssembly it bumps a word in shared memory and wakes every Worker parked on it (`memory.atomic.notify`), and `run_loop()`'s last backoff phase parks there with `memory.atomic.wait32` for at most 5 ms instead of `yield` + `sleep_for`, which spin on the clock in Emscripten's libc. A missed notify costs a parked Worker at most those 5 ms, never a hang; the worklet and the main thread never wait. Natively nothing changes: `notify_work()` is empty, the polling loop still sleeps and the blocking one still wakes on the queue's semaphore. Stopping a context's Workers while they still boot logs nothing and leaks nothing: the stop cancels the boot (`em::cancel_boot`: the bootstrap's record, which outlives the thread object, holds the state the entry and the cancel race on and the left word), the Worker finishes its boot without entering the loop and stores its left word after its last C frame, and a main-thread reaper (a timer every 100 ms while cancelled Workers remain) terminates it and frees its region and record; one whose boot has not finished after 10 s is terminated where it stands with a warning, its region left allocated. (A stop that terminated a Worker still loading the glue or importing its module made Firefox report console errors, `NS_ERROR_FAILURE` from `AniraWeb.js` and `uncaught exception: undefined`, and left its 4 MiB region allocated for good.) Tests: the node gate `anira_em_pool` (`test/abi/em_pool.c`, `em_pool_module.mjs`) runs a JavaScript engine on the pool; the browser test's `boot-stop.html` stops 64 contexts of two Workers 0 to 200 ms into their boot (an engine whose `init` takes 150 ms) and fails on any console error or uncaught exception: none in Chromium and Firefox; the cycles would exhaust the module's memory if a region were leaked (checked: a reaper that does not free fails it with OOM), and a context afterwards boots and enters its loops.
- **Web (module, `anira/anira_em.h`):** the audio path in C: a Wasm Audio Worklet node of a prepared Hard handler whose render callback is C (`src/platform/emscripten/em_audio.cpp`). `anira_em_audio_node_create(handler, audio_context, input_slots, num_input_slots, output_slots, num_output_slots, param_slots, num_param_slots, tensor_slots, num_tensor_slots, flags, wait_ms, out_status, out_node, err)`: the first node of a context starts the context's worklet thread, a node of n AudioParams registers the processor `anira-node-n` once per context, and the creation completes on the main thread's event loop (`out_status` turns from `ANIRA_INCOMPLETE` to the result; `written_later: true` in the registry keeps such a parameter a pointer in `bindings.ts`). Input k of the node feeds the k-th input slot and output k plays the k-th output slot, each with the slot's channels (from the prepared port), and NULL for a side serves every Streamed slot in slot order. The render callback hands the node's buffers to the handler as planar host tensors without a copy and calls `anira_handler_process_multi` (`_multi_wait` with `wait_ms`) once per quantum, with the handler's empty tensors at every other position, so a model with several streams keeps BYPASS on every output, the wait credit and one miss callback per block; driving one handler from two nodes, one slot each, keeps the streams aligned but loses those three. A single-slot node uses the multi call too: the single entry processors into the same per-slot arrays and calls the same manager path (`handler.cpp`), so there is one path; not measured separately. `anira_handler_process`'s doc says a model with several Streamed inputs is driven by one multi call per block. The miss rule: `ANIRA_MISSED`, a success whose `delivered` count is 0 by the ABI while the output tensor holds the miss policy's block (BYPASS by default, HOLD_LAST, a CALLBACK), keeps that fill; a failure is silence; the undelivered rest is silence. The callback allocates and locks nothing; a quantum other than the declared one is silence. The creation refuses a handler whose Hard block range excludes the context's render quantum, a slot that is not Streamed, a slot listed twice, a non-float32 ring and a Streamed slot no input or output serves, unless `ANIRA_EM_AUDIO_NODE_FLAG_PARTIAL_STREAMS` (new define) leaves it out. The node serves the model's Static inputs: a float32 scalar is an AudioParam of the node (k-rate, automatable; the callback pushes its k-rate value each quantum it changed), and a Static tensor of any shape and dtype is a seqlock block per slot in the shared heap, written whole from the main thread with `anira_em_audio_node_set_static_input`: the write makes the word odd, writes, makes it even; before each quantum's process call the callback copies a block whose word is even, unchanged across the copy and changed since its last push, and hands the copy to `anira_handler_set_static_input`, so a torn value is never pushed and the handler keeps the last good one until the next quantum. The callback never waits and allocates nothing for it. `anira_em_audio_node_hold` holds a node (silence, its handler left alone; a hold bit of its own beside the re-prepare's); `anira_em_audio_node_prepare` re-prepares the handler of a running node: the node's render callback is held off (silence) while the main thread waits for the quantum in flight and re-prepares, then resumes (about 2 ms in headless Chromium), and `anira_handler_prepare` refuses with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` while an audio node streams through the handler (`anira_handler.m_audio_nodes`). `anira_em_audio_node_destroy` stops the callback (a quantum inside it finishes first) and releases the node. A JavaScript processor runs its worklet phases on the worklet scope's registry, which anira boots over the worklet's instance at every create. The module exports `emscriptenGetAudioObject`; the node gates of `test/abi` link `-sAUDIO_WORKLET` too. The browser test's `streams.html` runs a two-stream model (a mono, b stereo) through one node exactly, the refusal of an unserved slot, and a slow engine under BYPASS: every bypassed block carries the anchored input on both outputs (Chromium and Firefox).
- **Web (module, build):** the module's glue works under Vite's dev server (decision D2): `cmake/wasm-glue-rewrite.cmake`, a post-link step of `AniraWeb`, rewrites the pthread pool's literal `new Worker(new URL("AniraWeb.js", import.meta.url))` (which the dev server turns into a worker import with its client, which an AudioWorkletGlobalScope cannot load) to go through `locateFile`, matched over either quote and any whitespace so the minified glue of a Release build and the `-O0` glue of a Debug build (whose options object also differs: `ASSERTIONS` appends the worker's ID to its name) are both rewritten, and fails the build on a glue without it; the Wasm Workers' import of the worker module is a template literal, which the dev server leaves alone but cannot analyse (it warned "The above dynamic import cannot be analyzed by Vite" of `AniraWeb.js`, once per server), so the same step marks it ``import(/* @vite-ignore */ `${url}`)``, matched over any whitespace in both glues (an `EM_JS` body cannot carry the comment: the preprocessor strips it), and fails the build on a glue without that import too; the dev server no longer warns. A glue rewritten already is left as it is. `web/test/browser/run.mjs` runs the example's check page under the dev server as its last page.
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`):** the typed layer over the generated bindings, the package's whole runtime, one layer with `anira.hpp` in two languages (the same class names and member words, camelCase for snake_case; a difference only where the platform forces it, which each class's doc comment names). `Anira.create({ memory, wasmBinary, abiVersion })` instantiates the module on the page's main thread (its `locateFile` maps the glue and the binary to `new URL(..., import.meta.url)`, which bundlers see) and boots the main registry, with `abi`, `module`, `memory`, `version`, `setLogSink`, `drainLog` and `startLogPump`. `Context.create(anira, { threads, workerModule, logLevel, json })`, with `numInferenceThreads()`, `ready()` and `destroy()`: without `threads` it keeps the context config's default, `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` (half the cores, at least one), the native default, so one config serves native and web; pass `threads` for an exact count (the browser tests that need one do); the examples, `basic_usage` and the web README use the default. `Pipeline.create(anira).addInference(models).addEngine(id, { flags, hooks }).addProcessor(name, { phases, flags })`, one member per C entry (`addInference` is `anira.hpp`'s `add_inference`; `phases` are `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*` bits, `flags` `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_*`). `InferenceHandler.create(context, pipeline)`, then `prepare(contract)` (it returns the prepare's `PlanReport`), with `getLatency`, `setStaticInput`, `createAudioNode` and `destroy`. Beside them `ModelConfig`, `ContractHandle`, `createAudioNode`, `defineProcessor` (the interfaces `Processor` / `ProcessorFactory`, the options `ProcessorOptions`; `Registry.defineProcessor`, and the trampolines' target `Registry.processor`: the C side calls `registry.processor`, `anira_em_js_processor`), `defineEngine`, `keyOf` and `engineTensors`. Unit tests of the registry and of the bindings over stand-in modules (`web/test/registry.test.ts`, `bindings.test.ts`).
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`):** `web/src/abi/bindings.ts`, generated by `tools/abi/gen.py` from `abi/anira.yml` beside `enums.ts` and `layout.ts`: the class `AniraBindings` over one module instance, one typed method per entry point of the module (244 at this release: the promised, draft and Emscripten ones) under its C name, so no hand-written wrapper can drift from the ABI. The binding makes the `anira_error` on the instance's stack and throws a failed `anira_status` as an `AniraError` (`web/src/AniraError.ts`) with the entry's message (an entry without one with the status text); a `const char*` argument is a JS string (its `size_t len` computed), what an entry writes through its out parameters is returned (one value, or an object by parameter name), a `_to_json` returns its text, an `anira_bool` is a `boolean`, a 64-bit integer a `bigint`, a pointer or a size a `number`; an enumeration entry keeps its pointers. `anira.abi` is the main instance's, and the processors and engines get their scope's in their env (`ScopeEnv`: `abi`, `module`). The package's own calls go through it. The module exports the runtime methods the bindings use (`stringToUTF8OnStack`, `lengthBytesUTF8`, `stackAlloc`, `HEAPU8`, `HEAP32`, `HEAPF64`, `HEAP64`, `HEAPU64`). `web/.prettierignore` lists the generated `enums.ts` and `bindings.ts`.
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`):** one model config file for native and web. `ModelConfig.fromUrl(anira, url)` (`web/src/ModelConfig.ts`) fetches a `*.model.json`, parses it with `anira_model_config_from_json` and, for every model entry whose engine the module compiled in (`anira_enabled_engines`, exported as `enabledEngines`) or a custom engine's, fetches the entry's file relative to the config's URL and attaches it as bytes (`anira_model_config_set_model_bytes`, copied); the other entries keep their paths. `fetchModel` chooses the entries, `fetch` the fetcher (a `Fetcher`); `fromJson` takes the text and a `baseUrl`, `setModelBytes` patches one entry, `toJson` writes it back. `ContractHandle.fromUrl` / `fromJson` read a `*.contract.json` (`anira_contract_from_json`), `ContractHandle.hard(anira, { hostBlockMin, hostBlockMax, hostRate, budgetMs, warmupIterations })` builds one, `hardHostBlocks` sets the host blocks a file left to the host. The browser test's `config.html` loads `extras/models/model-pool/gain.model.json` and `gain.contract.json` as they are in the repository (`run.mjs` serves `extras/models` at `/extras/models/`; `build_web` restores or fetches the model fixtures first) and runs the ONNX entry on the module's built-in ONNX Runtime engine: the output is the input times the Static gain after the handler's latency, in Chromium and Firefox. `run.mjs` takes the pages to run after the browser.
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`; decision 10.19):** `PlanReport.find(query?)` over `anira_plan_report_find`, with the type `PlanQuery` (`{ variant?, engine?, engineId?, provider?, providerId? }`, the fields of `anira::PlanQuery` flattened; a field left out matches every plan: `ANIRA_VARIANT_ANY`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE`). It writes the `anira_plan_query` on the module's stack, so it allocates nothing on the heap, and returns the plan index `InferenceHandler.setPlan` takes, or null for a miss (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_FOUND`; where C++ returns an empty optional); a query against the pair rule throws an `AniraError` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`). `Pipeline.addEngine` calls `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine`. Tests: `web/test/plan-report.test.ts` (the record the entry reads, the defaults, null, the refusals) and the browser page `plan-find.html` (the bundled gain config and a JavaScript engine's entry: the JavaScript engine by its id, the built-in ONNX Runtime engine by its kind, a provider alone, misses, pair-rule refusals, then `setPlan` on the answer and a render that is the JavaScript engine's).
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`):** `handler.createAudioNode(audioContext, options)` (the free `createAudioNode` underneath) returns an `AniraAudioNode`, `{ node, handle, params, setStaticInput, prepare, hold, destroy }`, over the C audio path above, on any `BaseAudioContext` (an `OfflineAudioContext` too). Its options: `inputSlots` and `outputSlots` (default every Streamed slot of the side; the channel counts come from the model, so a single-stream model needs neither), `partialStreams`, `params` (the Static float32 scalars as AudioParams, by slot), `staticInputs` (the Static tensors `audioNode.setStaticInput(slot, values)` writes), `waitMs` (an offline render that waits for each inference), `workletModules` (the worklet-side code of the handler's JavaScript processor) and `timeoutMs`. It loads `worklet-scope.js` (a monotonic `performance` for the scope, without which `std::chrono::steady_clock` fails on the audio thread) into a context before its first node, then registers the context (`emscriptenRegisterAudioObject`). That setup is memoised per context as a promise, set before its first await, which the creations in flight share (a failed setup is dropped, so the next node retries), and each creation keeps its own completion words, so audio nodes created at once on one audio context (`await Promise.all([h1.createAudioNode(ctx), h2.createAudioNode(ctx)])`) all complete; a setup remembered only once it had finished let each creation in flight register the context again, one context became several module handles, each of which started its own worklet thread on the context's one `AudioWorkletGlobalScope`, and no node completed ("the audio node was not created in time", offline and live, in Chromium and Firefox). Tested with 2 and 4 nodes created at once (offline in Chromium and Firefox, live in Chromium), each rendering its own gain, and with two creations while another node of the context is destroyed (`web/test/browser/concurrent.html`, `web/test/audio-node.test.ts`); `quanta.html` renders offline contexts of 256 and 480 frames through the node, exactly.
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`):** `measureRenderGeometry(context, { hold? })` (`web/src/renderGeometry.ts`), the render geometry a Hard contract for a handler streaming through an audio node declares, per the Web Audio API, reading no `baseLatency`: `quantum`, the frames of every `process()` call, the context's `renderQuantumSize` (fixed for the context's life, `setSinkId` included: *Rendering an Audio Graph*), else 128 where the browser has no such attribute (Firefox and Safari today; a `"hardware"` context reporting 0 before it runs is refused), and `burst`, the frames the context renders back to back, always measured: the callback's size is never exposed ("a varying number of sample-frames"), and `baseLatency` is not portable (Chromium: its callback buffer, kept from the first device after `setSinkId`; Safari: one quantum; Firefox: 0), so the library never reads it. An `OfflineAudioContext` renders quantum by quantum (a burst of one quantum). On a running `AudioContext` a probe `AudioWorklet` takes the longest run of back-to-back `process()` calls in whole quanta (441 frames per callback: runs of 3 and 4 quanta of 128, a burst of 512; a quantum at least the callback's size: runs of 1), or the frames per device callback over the quantum, rounded up, whichever is more, watching 300 ms after a 100 ms lead-in and on until that settles, at most 2 s (about 1 s in headless Chromium). A run ends where a gap reaches halfway from one quantum's duration to the longest gap of the window, the gap between callbacks, wherever the window shows runs (its longest gap at least two quanta, reached at least twice, those gaps adding a tenth of the window); an evenly paced context keeps the fixed gap (half a quantum, at least 1.5 ms), which alone cuts runs short on a loaded audio thread: a call late by more than it inside every callback, as a model's JavaScript processor that unfolds an inference's input once per callback makes one, split each run (re-measured while streaming, a GuitarLSTM page went from a latency of 6139 to 5887 samples, a burst of 1920 against 2048, Steerable-NAFX from 3071 to 3069, and on Firefox, where the probe is the only source, such a burst means missed quanta; in headless Chromium, bursts of 16 quanta, loads of 0.3 to 0.8 ms per call with a call late by 3 to 12 ms every 5th, 7th, 11th or 16th, the fixed gap measured 5 to 15 quanta and the new rule 16 every time). The frames per callback catch a burst rarer than the window: CI's device calls back with 481 frames, and at a quantum of 480 Chromium renders one quantum per callback and two back to back once every 480 callbacks (about 5 s), so 300 ms of runs say 480 against a worst case of 960, and a handler prepared for 480 would miss a quantum every 5 s. The probe's worklet counts its calls in a shared word and notifies it, a Worker waits on the word (`Atomics.wait`) and times every wake with `performance.now()` (the worklet scope has only `Date.now`, whose 1 ms cannot tell 480 frames from 481: 0.02 ms per callback), and a least-squares fit of the runs' starts on the callback grid gives the period (holes where runs merged skipped, the first start and starts a stall delayed left out). The burst is the longest run or the frames per callback over the quantum rounded up, half a frame taken off so an exact fit stays one quantum, whichever is more; the probe watches until 16 run starts or more settle that number within three standard errors and a tenth of a frame. Measured in headless Chromium (renderSizeHint 2048, 2047, 480, 256 and 128 over its 2048-frame callbacks): 2048.0 +- 0.2 frames per callback after 1 s, 0.07 after 2 s; 2047 gives 4094, the same case as CI's 481/480; twelve fresh contexts gave 2048 each, and the loaded node unheld 2048 where its runs said 512. A page whose policy refuses a blob Worker gets the runs alone. `hold` takes the context's running `AniraAudioNode`s: each is held while the probe measures (`audioNode.hold(true)`, `AniraAudioNode.hold`, `anira_em_audio_node_hold`: silence, its handler left alone) and released after, whatever happens, so the audio thread is quiet (unheld, a probe beside a node with a heavy JavaScript processor measured 512 of 2048 frames). A live context renders each device callback as a run of quanta, so a handler prepared for one quantum cannot complete an inference within a run and missed about half the quanta live (the gain model: latency 1024, 364-374 of 750 quanta missed); at the measured geometry its latency covers the run (2560) and none misses. The geometry is explicit: nothing re-prepares on its own. After an output-device change the page measures again holding its nodes (`measureRenderGeometry(ctx, { hold: [node] })`), re-prepares through the node (`audioNode.prepare(contract)`) and re-reads the latency; anira does not listen for `sinkchange`. Both examples create their context with `renderSizeHint: "hardware"` where the browser has render sizes (feature-tested by `renderQuantumSize`), measure the geometry, delay a dry path by the handler's latency (a `DelayNode`) and re-measure with the node held on `sinkchange`; their check pages verify the dry delay is `latency / sampleRate`. Tests: `npm test` (`render-geometry.test.ts`) covers the run detector on synthetic timestamps (loaded schedules, callbacks of 480, 441 and 192 frames, a quantum of 2048 over a callback of 1024 -> 2048, 481/480 -> 960 with no double in the window, 480/480 -> 480, 441/128 -> 512, 192/128 -> 256, 2048/128 -> 2048, a merged callback's hole, a delayed start, an unsettled short window, an evenly paced context with a late call) and the release of held nodes on failure. `live.html` (`web/test/browser/live.html`, Chromium) requires the measured burst to equal the device's (the test reads `baseLatency` as its oracle): idle, beside a load that spins in every call and stalls in every 16th (the burst or more; the fixed gap measured 1920), and with a node held whose JavaScript processor spins 8 ms per inference (unheld it measured 512 of 2048), 0 misses at it; a node at host_block_max one quantum shows its misses only where the burst exceeds that handler's latency (else the test logs a skip), the refused `handler.prepare`, a node re-prepared mid-stream to a host_block_max past the latency (four bursts, doubled as needed), so the latency read back must change, with no miss after it; and contexts of `renderSizeHint` 256, 480, 2047 and `"hardware"` (Chromium: 128) measuring their quantum and the device's burst with 0 misses. A CI runner's device buffer is 441 frames (`baseLatency` 9.1875 ms at 48 kHz), not a multiple of 128.
- **Web (package, `@anira-project/anira`):** a JavaScript engine gets the model of its entry, and the model is fetched once. A JavaScript engine's descriptor has C `load` / `prepare` trampolines that do not cross into JavaScript (the engine runs in the Workers' scopes, not the main thread's, where the C load runs): the load reads the row of `anira_engine_load_info` as a C engine's would (`anira_model_config_model_path`, `_model_bytes`) into a record the loaded model owns (the bytes borrowed for its lifetime, the path copied), and the prepare hands that record to every `process` and `reset` call as `prepared`. `engineModel(env, prepared)` reads it (`row`, `bytes` as a `Uint8Array` view of the shared memory, `path`), and an engine's optional `load(model)` hook runs on each Worker before the first call on a loaded model. No ABI surface is added (the record is internal to `em_hooks.cpp` and `registry.ts`). `ModelConfig.fromUrl` attaches a JavaScript engine's entry's bytes on the main thread. `installation_building` adds onnxruntime-web to the Vite `optimizeDeps.exclude` advice (a dev-server dependency re-optimisation reloads the page mid-fetch). The browser test's `ort.html` runs the config `ort-gain.model.json` on a pool of two Workers and counts the requests (`/__requests` of `run.mjs`): the `.onnx` once, the output exact; `npm test` checks that `load` sees the entry's bytes once per loaded model.
- **Web (ONNX Runtime Web engine, `@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web`):** the engine is a factory for a worker module, `defineEngine(ONNXRUNTIME_WEB_ENGINE_ID, onnxRuntimeWeb({ model }))` (the id `org.anira.onnxruntime-web`), in place of alpha.1's `ONNXRuntimeWebBackend` (Removed). `onnxRuntimeWeb()` takes `model` optionally: without it, the engine creates its session in `load` from the entry's bytes, which `ModelConfig.fromUrl` attached on the main thread (copied into onnxruntime-web's own heap), or from a synchronous fetch of the entry's path when it carries no bytes; with its own `model`, the engine fetches it and creates the session, and so warms up, in its `init`, before the inference Worker's loop, and `ModelConfig.fromUrl(anira, url, { fetchModel: () => false })` then keeps the page from fetching the file twice. So a config naming `org.anira.onnxruntime-web` states the model once and it is requested once. `onnxRuntimeWeb({ warmup })`: the inferences the engine runs over zeros of the model's input shapes right after it creates a session (a dynamic extent taken as 1; default 0), so the stream's first inferences do not pay onnxruntime-web's first-run setup; a contract's `"warmup": { "fixed": n }` warms anira's built-in engines only, a JavaScript engine is not warmed up by anira (the engine descriptor has no warm-up pass), and its `load` runs at the first call on a model, inside the stream; `process` and the warm-up share one run path (`run`). `onnxRuntimeWeb({ numThreads })`: the intra-op threads one inference of the engine runs on (default 1, as alpha.1's backend). onnxruntime-web's module starts `numThreads - 1` Workers of its own in the engine's `init`, before the inference Worker's loop, and `_OrtInit(numThreads)` gives its environment a global pool of that size, which every session of the Worker shares. anira's own ONNX Runtime engine cannot have this on the web (the module starts no pthread), so this engine is the way to shorten one inference where anira's inference threads cannot run several at once: a stateful model, or one large model on one handler. Measured with onnxruntime-web 1.26 in node on Apple silicon (Asahi Linux) over the realtime inference workshop's stateful RAVE model (2048 samples at 44.1 kHz, a 46.4 ms block): 37.3 ms median and 54.9 ms worst per inference on one thread, 18.5 and 20.5 ms on two. Through this engine in Chromium 152 (anira-web-example's RAVE demo, an offline render that waits for each inference, so its time per block is one inference and anira's copies): 50.0 to 50.8 ms per block on one thread, above the block, and 36.6 to 37.6 ms on two; four threads took 47.5 ms (one run), so more is not better where some cores are slow. The engines of one worker module share its runtime and so one count; an engine asking for another fails its `init`, as does a count that is not a whole number of at least 1. `_OrtInit` runs once per runtime (alpha.1's backend ran it at every engine's `init`). An intra-op count for every engine, native too, is #222. `ONNXRUNTIME_WEB_ENGINE_ID` lives in a module of its own (`web/src/engines/ids.ts`), re-exported by the root entry, the scope entry and the engine entry: a page that only names the engine (registers it, names it in a model entry) does not bundle onnxruntime-web's glue (~25 KB) to get the id, since only the engine's own entry imports onnxruntime-web. `npm run check:bundle` (`web/test/bundle-check.mjs`, run by `build_web` after the examples) follows every static import of `dist/index.js` and `dist/scope.js` and fails on a module carrying onnxruntime-web's code, on a root entry without the id, and on an example chunk carrying it. `ort.html` of the browser test renders the gain model again through a worker module with `numThreads: 2` (`ort-threads-worker.js`, which also warms up twice), and the output stays exact.
- **Web (tests and CI):** the ABI gates on WebAssembly. The option `ANIRA_WITH_ABI_TESTS` (default OFF) adds `test/abi` alone, the one test option `cmake/validate-options.cmake` allows on WebAssembly, and the configure and build preset `wasm-abi` (`wasm-release` plus the option, `build/wasm/Abi`) builds AniraWeb and the gates together. On Wasm `anira_header_c11` / `anira_header_cxx17` compile every header, `anira/anira_em.h` with its declarations, as members of ALL; `anira_abi_layout` is a node executable whose table `cmake/abi-layout-check.cmake` (new `LAYOUT_RUNNER`) diffs against `abi/layout-0.txt`. `anira_em_smoke` (`test/abi/em_smoke.c` with the scope registry of `test/abi/em_smoke.pre.js`) runs a JavaScript processor (pre/post/reset/init/prepare/unprepare/release) and a JavaScript engine (process doubles its input; a second engine's query drops a provider from the capabilities) on one Hard handler driven by `anira_inference_thread_execute`, a pre_process hook registered without `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST` refused at the Hard prepare (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`), the JavaScript log hook, and a throwing hook caught in its trampoline: the report of a throwing JavaScript hook is rate-limited, each module instance printing one `console.error` line per second at most, naming how many throws it left out since the last one, so a hook that throws on every quantum does not print from the audio thread at the render rate; the smoke throws three times in a row and checks three failed calls and one console line. `anira_em_smoke_thread` (`test/abi/em_smoke_thread.c`, decision 9.12), a user-driven inference thread for real: the test creates a Wasm Worker of its own (`emscripten_malloc_wasm_worker`) on the module's memory, which runs `anira_inference_thread_run_loop` while main pushes blocks through a Hard handler with `anira_handler_process_wait` (a C engine doubles them; 15 of 16 at the handler's latency), then the stop: `has_exited` within 100 ms (the parked loop woken by the stop's notify), the teardown. The target `anira_abi_wasm_gates` runs the gates under node, `anira_em_smoke_thread` after `anira_em_smoke`, with `anira_em_mutex`, `anira_em_pool`, `anira_em_console`, `anira_em_runtime` and `anira_em_oom` (each with its fix or feature). `build_web` builds `wasm-abi`, runs the gates and checks that `web/wasm/AniraWeb.d.ts` exports every name of `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt` and `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` (plus `_malloc`, `_free`) before the npm build. Natively `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` include `anira/anira_em.h`, an empty companion there, and the per-file self-containment wrappers cover it on every leg.
- **Web (tests and CI):** a browser test of the built package. `build_web` installs Playwright 1.64.1 (Chromium 156, Firefox 157; its Chromium has `renderSizeHint`) with headless Chromium and Firefox in the job and runs `web/test/browser/run.mjs` in both (`node test/browser/run.mjs firefox` the second): pages served with COOP/COEP headers. `index.html` checks `crossOriginIsolated`, `Anira.create()` and that a refused call throws its `AniraError`, runs a JavaScript engine that doubles its input times a Static gain on a pool of two Wasm Workers (`pool-worker.js`) and a JavaScript processor in the worklet (`worklet-processor.js`), renders an `OfflineAudioContext` through the handler's audio node (`waitMs`, since an offline render outruns real time) with the gain an AudioParam automated mid-render, requires the output to be the input times 2 * gain after the handler's latency, serves a tensor Static input through `setStaticInput` on a second handler, and stops the pool. `config.html` loads `extras/models/model-pool/gain.model.json` and its contract unchanged and runs them on the built-in ONNX Runtime engine, `ort.html` runs the ONNX Runtime Web engine, and the pages of the features above follow (`plan-find.html`, `streams.html`, `boot-stop.html`, `concurrent.html`, `quanta.html`, `growth.html`, `oom.html`; `live.html` in Chromium); the runner then opens the examples' check pages, built and, for processor-and-engine, under Vite's dev server. A load the browser refuses fails a page even when its checks pass: a console message `Security Error: Content at … may not load or link to file:///…`, as seen in Firefox on the web example's Vite dev server (finding F3 of the web-example review). It was not reproduced from the package: the `file://` strings in `wasm/AniraWeb.js`, `workers/audio-worklet.bundled.js` and `shared/inferenceWorkerHandler-*.js` of the package of the time are Emscripten's and onnxruntime-web's `startsWith("file://")` tests, no load; the package ships no source map and its wasm no `sourceMappingURL` section, and the pre-bundled deps' maps name relative sources. Headless Firefox 132 loaded every page without the error: the package as built, the custom-worklet example under `vite` (dev server) with the package as source and as an installed tarball (Vite's pre-bundling), before and after the onnxruntime-web fix (Fixed); the same probe reports the error for a page that links a `file:///` script. Locally: with Playwright installed, `node test/browser/run.mjs [chromium|firefox] [page...]` from `web/` after `npm run build && npm run examples` and `cmake -P extras/fetch-models.cmake`. Firefox's `OfflineAudioContext` cannot `suspend`, so the checks schedule changes as automation instead.
- **Web (tests and CI):** unit tests of the package under node: `npm test` runs `web/test/*.test.ts` with node's type stripping (no build needed), and `build_web` runs it after the package build. Each suite is named with its feature above (the registry and the bindings, the plan report, the render geometry, the heap views, the audio node, the JavaScript engine's model).
- **Web (docs and examples):** two complete examples, type-checked and run in CI. `web/examples/processor-and-engine/`: `gain-processor.ts` (`INPUT_GAIN_PROCESSOR`, `defineProcessor`, one module defining the processor on the main thread and in the worklet: `prepare` / `unprepare` on the main thread allocate its per-handler memory, `preProcess` in the worklet scales the model's audio tensor by the Static input `gain`, ramping from the previous block's gain, allocation-free; `reset` forgets the ramp), `double-engine.ts` (`defineEngine`, a doubling engine standing in for the model, the context's worker module), `main.ts` (the module, the context with its worker module, the model config, the pipeline with `addEngine` and `addProcessor`, the handler with a Hard contract of one render quantum, the audio node with the processor's worklet module, the teardown) and `vite.config.ts` (the worker and worklet modules as one file each through `?worker&url`, the COOP/COEP headers), bundled by Vite. Beside it `web/examples/model-file/`, the page of the basic-usage guide: `main.ts` loads `extras/models/model-pool/gain.model.json` and its contract unchanged and runs them on the built-in ONNX Runtime engine through the audio node, `check.ts` renders it offline with the model's Static gain automated mid-render (0 error), and its `vite.config.ts` serves the repository's `extras/models` at `/extras/models/` to `vite` and `vite preview`. `npm run examples` type-checks both with `strict` against the built package's types and builds them (`vite build`), `build_web` runs that, and the browser test runs both check pages (processor-and-engine's offline with an automated gain, 0 error up to float32 rounding), built, and processor-and-engine's under Vite's dev server too. The guides include their files (`literalinclude`).
- **ABI gates:** `anira_abi_namespaces` (`cmake/abi-namespaces.cmake`, beside `anira_symbol_baseline`): the private copies keep their names. It reads every symbol of the built libanira, the local ones included (a static archive's members, the prelinked object on ELF, a shared library's full symbol table on ELF and Mach-O, the objects a DLL is linked from on PE), and fails on any `thl` symbol outside `thl::anira` (Itanium `3thl` not followed by `5anira`, MSVC `@thl@@` not `@anira@thl@@`) and on any `moodycamel` one (`10moodycamel`, `@moodycamel@@`), and when it sees no `thl::anira` symbol at all. Measured: a Debug libanira.so has 1643 `thl::anira` and 279 `anira_moodycamel` symbols and nothing foreign; a libanira.a built before the namespace fails with 221 of 2840 symbols foreign.
- **ABI gates:** gate 5, the installed package from C and beside a host's own tanh-lib. `test/contracts/install` is a `LANGUAGES C CXX` project with two consumers beside alpha.1's `consumer` and `consumer_engine`. `consumer_c` (`main_c.c`, C11) runs the raw-C sequence of the architecture document's section 9 against the installed package: `anira_check_abi`, a context, a model config, a registered engine created with `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL` in place of the built-in candidates, a handler prepared under a Hard contract with an explicit budget and a fixed warm-up, one block through `anira_handler_process`, the teardown and `anira_shutdown`; it carries gate 4's probes against the installed include directories, links through the C++ driver on a static anira and is compiled with `-Werror=function-effects` where the compiler has it. `consumer_tanh_first` (`main_tanh_first.cpp`) is a host that builds its own shared tanh-lib (at anira's pin, in tanh-lib's default namespace) and links it before `anira::anira`, on every leg and against a shared and a static anira: the host's `thl::Logger` never sees one of anira's records and anira's sink never sees one of the host's, and `run-install-test.cmake` checks with `nm` that a shared `libanira` names no `thl` symbol in its dynamic table. On a static anira the host's objects and the archive's are linked into one image, where the names keep them apart (anira's copy is `thl::anira`); a plain static archive (Ninja Multi-Config, no prelink) passes as the prelinked one does, the shape that shared one logger before the namespace. The plugin-shaped engine module's export scan also forbids `anira_*` entries. `build_install.yml` runs `consumer_c` and `consumer_tanh_first` on every leg (Linux, Windows and macOS, shared and static).
- **ABI gates:** gate 6's compile-time half covers the C gates. `-Werror=function-effects` (upstream clang 20 or later behind the GNU front end, one helper `anira_abi_function_effects`) is on `anira_header_c11` and `anira_abi_link` beside `anira_abi_rt_contract`, and on gate 5's `consumer_c`; the `desktop-tests-rtsan` preset names its run-time half, the whole suite under RealtimeSanitizer with the unload harness the one directory left out.
- **Bundled models:** the State fixture `extras/models/model-pool/stateful_accumulator.model.json` / `.contract.json` (`k_stateful_accumulator_model_json` / `k_stateful_accumulator_contract_json` in `extras/models/model_files.h`): example-models' `StatefulAccumulatorNetwork` in stereo, the pin of `extras/fetch-models.cmake` moving to `d1bc5cf` (the commit that adds the model). A 64-sample stream with a declared State pair around it, `state_in` first in the inputs and `state_out` last in the outputs (`"role": "state"` on both, `"state_source": "state_out"` on the input; per channel the running sum and a block counter), so `processed_data = data + state_in[..., 0]` and the whole stream has a closed form. The file names a row for each of the five engines. The TFLite export orders its outputs `[state_out, processed_data]` (the README of example-models; the input side keeps the declared order) and keeps the declared mapping under the signature keys `output_0` / `output_1`: the `tflite` row reaches them by position with no record (the signature runner's key order is the declared order), and the `litert` row carries a `tensors` record naming `processed_data` as `output_0` and `state_out` as `output_1` (LiteRT lists them in the file's order). `test/abi/test_State.cpp` runs the file on every engine of the build it names (the bundled-model cases of `AbiState`, TFLite and LiteRT included) against the closed form, across host blocks above and below the hop, after `anira_handler_reset` and after the contract file's fixed warm-up (which runs inside the processor when it loads and reaches neither the state nor the stream); the binding is checked against the shapes at prepare, so a swapped pair is refused, not captured as the state. `test/abi/test_ExtrasFixtures.cpp` checks the file field by field (`ExtrasFixtures.StatefulAccumulator`), since a State spec has no 2.x fixture to compare with.

### Changed

- **Breaking:** C ABI — `<anira/anira.h>` is the C11 umbrella of the C ABI (every promised header of `anira/abi/`, generated from the registry's `umbrellas:` list), no longer alpha.1's umbrella of the 2.x C++ classes. Migration: a 2.x program includes `<anira/compat/v2.hpp>` and spells `anira::v2::` (or `using namespace anira::v2;`); C++ against the 3.x API includes `<anira/anira.hpp>`; no 2.x or core C++ header is installed (the install item below).
- **Breaking:** C ABI — alpha.1's `anira_stage_phase` (pinned ahead of its consumer: `ANIRA_PHASE_PRE_PROCESS` 0, `ANIRA_PHASE_POST_PROCESS` 1, `ANIRA_PHASE_BEFORE_INFERENCE` 2, `ANIRA_PHASE_AFTER_INFERENCE` 3, `ANIRA_PHASE_PREPARE` 4, `ANIRA_PHASE_RELEASE` 5, terminator `ANIRA_STAGE_PHASE_FORCE32`) is `anira_phase` with the terminator `ANIRA_PHASE_FORCE32`, renumbered so the three inference-thread phases count in the order they run, and extended: `PRE_PROCESS` 0, `POST_PROCESS` 1 and `BEFORE_INFERENCE` 2 stay, `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` takes 3, `ANIRA_PHASE_AFTER_INFERENCE` moves 3 -> 4, `ANIRA_PHASE_PREPARE` 4 -> 5, `ANIRA_PHASE_RELEASE` 5 -> 6, and `ANIRA_PHASE_RESET = 7`, `ANIRA_PHASE_UNPREPARE = 8`, `ANIRA_PHASE_INIT = 9`, `ANIRA_PHASE_LOAD = 10`, `ANIRA_PHASE_UNLOAD = 11` and `ANIRA_PHASE_QUERY = 12` follow. The enum names the phases of both descriptors, the engine call and the engine's `load`, `unload` and `query` among them, so like the records the two lifecycles share (`anira_init_info`, `anira_prepare_info`) it names no side; the value travels in `anira_processor_ctx.phase` alone (an engine receives none, each of its slots being a callback of its own), and the web build's `enums.ts` exports `anira_phase`. `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` is the engine call itself, on the inference thread between `ANIRA_PHASE_BEFORE_INFERENCE` and `ANIRA_PHASE_AFTER_INFERENCE`, whether the running plan's engine is built in or a registered custom engine's `process`; no processor callback runs in it, and a failed engine call is logged under its word (`inference failed in session N: the engine of plan P returned S`); `test/abi/header_cxx17.cpp` pins the order. Likewise alpha.1's `ANIRA_STRUCT_STAGE_CTX` (4 in `anira_struct_id`, reserved for `anira_stage_ctx`, 64 bytes) is `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX` (4, the `anira_processor_ctx` of `anira/abi/processor.h`, 64 bytes; Added). Migration: spell the type `anira_phase` (`anira::ProcessorContext::phase()` and `anira::Processor::phase_bit` take and return it) and the struct id `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX`; recompile against the new `anira/abi/enums.h` (and take `web/src/abi/enums.ts` with it); use the names, never the numbers: a `switch` over the values compiles unchanged, and no entry point of alpha.1 consumed the enum, so no stored value exists.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — `anira_engine` and `anira_provider` are renumbered into one order, the provider axis gets the engine axis's NONE, the CPU path is a provider of its own, and a custom engine and a custom provider have enum values of their own. `anira_engine` is NONE 0, ONNXRUNTIME 1, EXECUTORCH 2, LITERT 3, LIBTORCH 4, TFLITE 5, CUSTOM `0x7FFFFFFE`: alpha.1's `ANIRA_ENGINE_LIBTORCH` 2 is 4, `ANIRA_ENGINE_TFLITE` 3 is 5, `ANIRA_ENGINE_LITERT` 4 is 3 and `ANIRA_ENGINE_EXECUTORCH` 5 is 2 (were LIBTORCH 2, TFLITE 3, LITERT 4, EXECUTORCH 5; `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` 0 and `ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME` 1 stay). `anira_provider` is NONE 0, CPU 1, CUDA 2, WEBGPU 3, VULKAN 4, DIRECTML 5, COREML 6, XNNPACK 7, CUSTOM `0x7FFFFFFE`: alpha.1's `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` 0 is removed, not aliased; `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUDA` 1 is 2, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_WEBGPU` 2 is 3, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DIRECTML` 3 is 5, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_COREML` 4 is 6, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_XNNPACK` 5 is 7 and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_VULKAN` 6 is 4 (were CUDA 1, WEBGPU 2, DIRECTML 3, COREML 4, XNNPACK 5, VULKAN 6); `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE = 0` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU = 1` are new. `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` sit at `0x7FFFFFFE`, the value just below the `_FORCE32` sentinel, so a later built-in engine or provider is appended below them and never renumbers CUSTOM; a value after the last built-in one and below CUSTOM is reserved for a later built-in engine or provider. Every list follows the one order (the word tables, the JSON word lists of the docs, `anira_enabled_engines` and the capabilities, the default candidate set). The doc of `anira_engine` no longer promises alpha.1's per-pipeline value from 0x1000 for a registered engine: wherever the pair travels it is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with its `engine_id` (the pair rule, below). `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` is no provider, the zero value (`ANIRA_BACKEND_ID_INIT`, `ANIRA_PLAN_INFO_INIT`, `ANIRA_EDGE_INFO_INIT`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_OPTION_SET_INIT` and a zeroed record hold it): a neutral model entry (`anira_model_config_set_model_provider(cfg, i, ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE, NULL)` unpins, the getter answers NONE) and no default provider (`anira_model_config_set_default_provider(cfg, ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE, NULL)` clears, the getter answers NONE); a candidate or a provider option set naming it is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, as `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` is refused as an entry's engine. `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` (JSON `"cpu"`) is the engine's plain CPU path, no delegate or execution provider: ONNX Runtime's `CPUExecutionProvider`, LibTorch, TFLite, ExecuTorch without a delegate, LiteRT's CPU accelerator; XNNPACK stays a provider of its own. Every built-in engine serves it as its first capability row, and `anira_enabled_engines`, the capability rows and edges, the plans (`anira_plan_info`), `anira_engine_load_info` and the processor context report `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` for the CPU path, which alpha.1 spelled `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` ("the engine's own CPU path"); the default candidate set is every engine of the build on CPU, each custom engine an entry names, and every pin, and under it every entry is one plan: a pinned entry on its pin, a neutral one on its engine's home provider (CPU for a built-in engine and for a custom engine that serves it, else the custom engine's first listed provider); a plan on a provider its engine cannot use here (a built-in engine's provider the capabilities lack, a custom engine's declared provider its query cleared) is dropped under the default set, as an entry for an absent engine is skipped, a variant left without a plan is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, and a named candidate on such a provider is still refused. A custom engine serves exactly what its descriptor lists: no list serves CPU alone, a list serves CPU only when it lists `"cpu"`, and the query's bitmask covers the listed providers alone. `"default"` and `"none"` are no provider's name: a model file's `"provider"` and `"default_provider"`, a provider option set's `"provider"` and a descriptor's providers list refuse them; the CPU path takes no provider options. The 2.x `anira::InferenceBackend` keeps its values (mapped by a switch, never by value). Migration: rebuild against the new header; code that stored an engine or a provider as a number (a file, a wire format) maps it by the enumerator's name; a candidate or a pin that said `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` for the CPU path says `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` (JSON `"cpu"`); "no default provider" says `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` (JSON: leave the key out); a reader that tested `provider == ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` tests `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` for a neutral entry or no default provider and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` for a plan's, a row's or a load's CPU path; a record left at its `_INIT` value or zeroed names no provider, so a candidate built that way sets `provider = ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`; a custom engine that relied on the default provider being served lists `"cpu"` beside its other providers, or lists nothing. C++: `anira::ProviderRef{}` and `ProviderOptions::Set{}` start at `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE`. Tests: `AbiPrepare.StructuralRulesAtCreate` (a NONE candidate), `AbiEngine.UnderTheDefaultSetANeutralEntryRunsOnItsEnginesHomeProvider` (the three home providers, the dropped plan, the empty table, the named candidate), `AbiTranslate.EnabledEnginesMatchTheBuild` (the engine order), `AbiEngine.TheProvidersListIsCopiedAndAnswersServes` (a list serves what it lists, the reserved words), `AProviderTheEngineDoesNotListIsNotSupportedAtCreate` (CPU refused beside a list without it), `ThePipelinesCapabilitiesListTheCustomEnginesRows`, `AbiJsonModel.AProviderKeyPinsTheEntry` (`"cpu"`, the reserved words), `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionsRoundTripInCAndJson` and `ProviderOptionSetsAreReadAtTheCallersStrideAndCheckedAtSet` (NONE and CPU refused).
- **Breaking:** C ABI — one pair rule names both axes, and alpha.1's custom twins fold into the entries that take the pair. Wherever an engine or a provider is named (a model entry and its pin, the default engine and provider, `anira_backend_id` candidates and capability rows, `anira_plan_info`, `anira_edge_info`, `anira_provider_option_set`, `anira_engine_load_info`, `anira_processor_ctx`) the id is non-NULL if and only if the value is CUSTOM: a custom engine is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with its reverse-URI id (was `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` beside it), a provider the enum does not name is `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with its name, a built-in value takes a NULL id, `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` names no engine and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` no provider, and every setter and the candidate list of `anira_pipeline_add_inference` refuse any other combination with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; the getters and records report CUSTOM (`anira_model_config_model_engine`, `_default_engine`, `_model_provider`, `_default_provider`, the plan report, the capability rows, the processor context), and `anira_enabled_engines` never lists it. alpha.1's three setters take the pair: `anira_model_config_add_model_path(config, engine, utf8_path, out_index, err)` is `anira_model_config_add_model_path(config, engine, engine_id, path, out_index, err)`, `anira_model_config_add_model_bytes(config, engine, bytes, size, ownership, release, ctx, out_index, err)` is `anira_model_config_add_model_bytes(config, engine, engine_id, bytes, size, ownership, release, ctx, out_index, err)` and `anira_model_config_set_default_engine(config, engine)` is `anira_model_config_set_default_engine(config, engine, engine_id, err)` (it gains the `anira_error*` of its siblings); alpha.1's three custom twins `anira_model_config_add_model_path_custom`, `anira_model_config_add_model_bytes_custom` and `anira_model_config_set_default_engine_custom` are removed. No function of the ABI carries `_id` in its name: every getter reads the whole pair through a status and two out-parameters (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL handle, a NULL value out-parameter or an index out of range; the id out-parameter may be NULL; written on `ANIRA_OK` only): alpha.1's value getter `anira_model_config_model_engine(config, model_index)`, which returned the engine beside `anira_model_config_model_engine_id`, both of alpha.1, is `anira_model_config_model_engine(config, model_index, &engine, &engine_id)`, and `anira_model_config_model_engine_id` is removed; the new `anira_model_config_default_engine(config, &engine, &engine_id)`, `anira_model_config_model_provider(config, model_index, &provider, &provider_id)`, `anira_model_config_default_provider(config, &provider, &provider_id)` and the processor's pair accessors `anira_processor_engine(ctx, &engine, &engine_id)` and `anira_processor_provider(ctx, &provider, &provider_id)` have the same shape. A candidate `{ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE, NULL}` does not stand for every custom entry: name each custom engine by its id (the default candidate set lists the custom engines its entries name). C++: the enum and string overloads of `add_model_path`, `add_model_bytes`, `default_engine`, `model_provider` and `default_provider` keep their spelling and map onto the pair (`model_provider(i, provider, id)` and `default_provider(provider, id)` are `model_provider(i, id)` and `default_provider(id)` for a custom provider); the getters return `anira::EngineRef{kind, id}` / `anira::ProviderRef{kind, id}` (`model_engine(i)`, `default_engine()`, `model_provider(i)`, `default_provider()`, `EngineLoadInfo::model_engine(i)` / `provider()`, `ProcessorContext::engine()` / `provider()`), `kind` `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` / `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` for a custom one, and alpha.1's `model_engine_id(i)` is gone with every `*_id()` getter. Migration: `anira_model_config_add_model_path_custom(cfg, id, path, &i, &err)` is `anira_model_config_add_model_path(cfg, ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM, id, path, &i, &err)` (the bytes and default-engine twins likewise); a built-in engine's call adds a `NULL` id; a candidate or a pin that spelled a custom engine as NONE beside an id spells CUSTOM; a reader that tested `engine == ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` for a custom engine tests `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM`; `anira_model_config_model_engine(cfg, i)` is `anira_model_config_model_engine(cfg, i, &engine, &id)` (the `_id` getter folds into its value getter), and in C++ `model_engine(i)` / `model_engine_id(i)` read `model_engine(i).kind` / `.id`.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — the handle over the core is the *context*, the C convention for the object created first, passed to everything else and possibly instantiated more than once (`libusb_context`, `zmq_ctx`, `SSL_CTX`, the GL and CUDA contexts). alpha.1's handles `anira_machine_config` and `anira_machine` are `anira_context_config` and `anira_context`, and every `anira_machine_config_*` entry of `anira/abi/config.h` that v3.0.0-alpha.1 shipped is an `anira_context_config_*` entry (since 0.2) with the same parameters and the handle type renamed: the 17 of the handle, `anira_machine_config_create`, `anira_machine_config_set_threads`, `anira_machine_config_set_log_level`, `anira_machine_config_set_log_drain`, `anira_machine_config_set_log_queue_capacity`, `anira_machine_config_set_log_flags`, `anira_machine_config_set_log_sink`, `anira_machine_config_set_log`, `anira_machine_config_set_cuda`, `anira_machine_config_set_gl`, `anira_machine_config_set_vulkan`, `anira_machine_config_set_metal`, `anira_machine_config_set_d3d12`, `anira_machine_config_set_webgpu`, `anira_machine_config_set_ext`, `anira_machine_config_set_ext_json` and `anira_machine_config_destroy`, are `anira_context_config_create`, `_set_threads`, `_set_log_level`, `_set_log_drain`, `_set_log_queue_capacity`, `_set_log_flags`, `_set_log_sink`, `_set_log`, `_set_cuda`, `_set_gl`, `_set_vulkan`, `_set_metal`, `_set_d3d12`, `_set_webgpu`, `_set_ext`, `_set_ext_json` and `anira_context_config_destroy`, and the JSON pair `anira_machine_config_from_json` / `anira_machine_config_to_json` is `anira_context_config_from_json` / `anira_context_config_to_json`. The runtime entries over it are new (`anira_context_create` / `destroy` / `probe` / `capabilities` / `byte_image_bytes` in `anira/abi/context.h`; `anira_inference_thread_create` takes an `anira_context*`; Added). In `anira/anira.hpp` alpha.1's `MachineConfig` is `ContextConfig` (the runtime object is `Context`); the JSON *machine file* is the *context file* (its keys are unchanged, and a 2.x document's `context_config` root still upgrades into it). Migration: replace `machine` with `context` in every 3.x identifier; the registry diff against alpha.1 lists the removed names one by one.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — the Hard contract's geometry is the *host blocks*, the word for what it describes, in `double`, and what the host must provide carries a `host_` prefix everywhere: the smallest and largest block the host passes per call and the host rate, counted in elements of the model's anchor tensor (`anira_model_config_set_anchor`; by default the first Streamed input, else the first Streamed output), are `host_block_min`, `host_block_max` and `host_rate` (the budget, `wait_ratio`, `on_miss` and the other terms keep their names). alpha.1's `anira_contract_create_hard(uint32_t block_min, uint32_t block_max, double rate, out, err)` is `anira_contract_create_hard(double host_block_min, double host_block_max, double host_rate, ...)` (since 0.1, signature changed), alpha.1's `anira_contract_hard_set_geometry(contract, uint32_t block_min, uint32_t block_max, double rate)` is removed and its successor `anira_contract_hard_set_host_blocks(contract, double host_block_min, double host_block_max, double host_rate)` (since 0.1) has the same order, and the new read-back `anira_contract_hard_host_blocks` (since 0.2) writes `double*` outs:
  | v3.0.0-alpha.1 | v3.0.0-alpha.2 |
  | --- | --- |
  | `anira_contract_create_hard(block_min, block_max, rate, out, err)` (`uint32_t`, `uint32_t`, `double`) | `anira_contract_create_hard(host_block_min, host_block_max, host_rate, out, err)` (three `double`) |
  | `anira_contract_hard_set_geometry(contract, block_min, block_max, rate)` | `anira_contract_hard_set_host_blocks(contract, host_block_min, host_block_max, host_rate)` |
  | (no read-back) | `anira_contract_hard_host_blocks(contract, &host_block_min, &host_block_max, &host_rate)` |
  | `anira::Hard::block_min`, `block_max`, `rate` | `anira::Hard::host_block_min`, `host_block_max`, `host_rate` (doubles) |
  | `anira::ContractHandle::hard_geometry(block_min, block_max, rate)` | `ContractHandle::hard_host_blocks(host_block_min, host_block_max, host_rate)` (doubles) |
  | contract file, `"hard"`: `"block_min"`, `"block_max"`, `"rate"` | `"host_block_min"`, `"host_block_max"`, `"host_rate"` (any JSON number) |
  | internal `capi::HardContract::m_block_min`, `m_block_max`, `m_rate` | `m_host_block_min`, `m_host_block_max`, `m_host_rate` |
  The old keys are refused with `ANIRA_ERROR_JSON`, the message naming the new key (`hard.block_max: "block_max" is now "host_block_max"`), so a file has one spelling per fact and is not silently read under the old one (`AbiJsonContract.TheHostKeysOfTheAlphasAreRefusedWithTheirNewName`); `to_json` writes no contract, and none of the bundled `*.contract.json` sets the keys. The messages use the new names: `host_block_min 0.5 exceeds host_block_max 0.25`, `host_rate -1 must not be negative`, the prepare refusal of a contract without them `contract: Hard host blocks missing (host_block_max 0, host_rate 0)` (`contract: Hard host blocks missing (...)`) and the latency calculator's `contract: host_block_max and host_rate must be positive`. `host_block_min = 0` with a non-zero `host_block_max`, legal in alpha.1, is refused: write `host_block_min = host_block_max` for a fixed block or a smaller positive one (fractional blocks, Added). Migration: a C caller renames `anira_contract_hard_set_geometry` to `anira_contract_hard_set_host_blocks` and keeps passing integers (the implicit conversion is exact for every `uint32_t`; the parameter names are not part of a C call), a caller of the read-back declares its two block outs `double`; C++ renames the `anira::Hard` fields (`Hard{.host_block_max = 512, .host_rate = 48000, .budget = 5ms}`) and the call, e.g. `contract.hard_host_blocks(1, samplesPerBlock, sampleRate)` (integer literals and `uint32_t` assignments are unchanged); a contract file renames its three keys. `anira/compat/v2.hpp` keeps its 2.x names (`HostConfig`) and fills the new fields; the web render geometry (`measureRenderGeometry`) keeps its name. The Doxygen, the guides (`usage.rst` section 1.3 (*Host blocks*) and 4.1 (*The host blocks*); `latency.rst`: B is `host_block_max` and f_s is `host_rate`; `migration.rst`: the 2.x `HostConfig` maps to the `host_` fields; the web-API pages), the JUCE, CLAP and benchmark examples, the web examples and the tests use the new names; the copy-path goldens are unchanged. Tests renamed with it: `AbiContract.TheHostBlockRules`, `AbiJsonContract.FractionalHostBlocks`, `AbiPrepare.MissingHostBlocksAreConfig`, `AbiValidate.AFlexibleWindowIsPinnedFromTheContractsHostBlocks` and `AFractionalHostBlockIsTheCalculatorsRational`, `LatencyCalculatorClosedForm.RejectsEmptyHostBlocks`.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — the elements a streamed tensor keeps from the previous window are its *overlap*, the DSP word beside *hop* (window minus overlap), where alpha.1 said *context*: the model file key `"overlap"` replaces alpha.1's `"context"` (an old `"context"` key is an unknown key, stored as an extension and failing prepare by name like every unknown key; the bundled model files are rewritten), and `anira::TensorSpec::window(min, max, overlap)` names its argument so. The C entry `anira_tensor_spec_set_window(spec, window_min, window_max, overlap)` renames its last parameter only (`context` → `overlap`, all `int64_t`), as `anira_model_config_set_tensor_name(config, model_index, canonical, export_name)` renames its last (`engine_name` → `export_name`, the tensor record's JSON spelling unchanged): `gen.py --diff-against` classifies both as BREAKING, but no C caller is affected. Migration: rename the key in a model file; nothing else moves.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — a flexible window is pinned from one anchor hop for the whole model, where alpha.1's bridge pinned each flexible window on its own from the block (`host_block_max`). The anchor hop is the anchor elements one inference consumes; the anchor, every Streamed tensor with a declared time ratio and every flexible window follow it (a tensor's hop is the anchor hop times its ratio, 1/1 for a flexible window without one), and a fixed window without a declared ratio keeps its own hop, its ratio derived from it, as before. anira takes the largest anchor hop that leaves every such tensor a whole hop within its window at or below the host block (`host_block_max` as the rational `LatencyCalculator::rationalize` recovers, rounded down), else the smallest above it, and without host blocks the smallest: a time ratio `num/den` makes the anchor hop a multiple of `den` (reduced), each window bounds it. The rule runs in `pin_anchor_hop` (`src/capi/validate.cpp`), for `anira_handler_prepare` (and, while it lived, the 2.x bridge); it takes over the separate check of declared ratios against fixed hops. Every configuration accepted before keeps its windows, except a flexible window without a declared ratio whose anchor hop is not `host_block_max` (a fixed anchor of another hop, an anchor window clamped by its range, no host blocks): it follows the anchor hop now, where it took the block, or its own `window_min` without host blocks (a fixed anchor of 2048 and a flexible output under a block of 512 ran the output at 512 per inference, a quarter-rate stream; it runs 2048). Accepted now: a block that scaled to a fractional hop runs one inference every few calls (an output at `time_ratio(1, 2048)` on a fixed anchor of 2048 under a block of 1024; a flexible window under a fractional block, refused before with "gives a fractional hop"), and a flexible window with a declared ratio beside a fixed anchor whose hop is not the block (refused before as "does not match its hop"). Still refused, with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the tensor, the hops it needs and the hops the tensors before it allow: no anchor hop fits every window and ratio (`tensor 'out': time ratio 1/2 and window [1, unbounded] need an anchor hop that is a multiple of 2 in [2, unbounded], but tensor 'in' fixes the anchor hop at 3`), a window no whole hop fits (`time ratio 2/1 and window 3 admit no whole anchor hop`), and a time ratio of 0 (`time ratio 0/5: a Streamed tensor advances with the anchor`, refused before as a mismatch). Migration: a flexible window that must advance at another rate than the anchor declares its time ratio. Documented in `anira/abi/config.h` (`anira_tensor_spec_set_time_ratio`), `usage.rst` sections 1.1 and 1.3 (*Flexible windows*, *Fractional blocks*) and the *Windows* note in `migration.rst`. Tests: `AbiTranslate.TheAnchorHopIsTheLargestStepAtOrBelowTheBlock` (3000 rounds down to 2048, 512 and 0.25 up to the step, `window_max` caps below the block), `AFixedAnchorSetsTheHopOfEveryFlexibleWindow`, `NoAnchorHopFitsEveryTensor`, `ATimeRatioScalesTheAnchorHopIntoTheFlexibleWindow` (was `ATimeRatioScalesTheFlexibleHopAndMustDivide`, whose block 1024 was refused), `AFractionalBlockPinsTheFlexibleWindowFromTheAnchorHop` (was `AFractionalBlockMaxGivesAFractionalFlexibleHop`, refused; a quarter of a hop per block), `AFlexibleWindowIsPinnedFromTheContractsHostBlocks` (its output without host blocks follows the anchor hop, 192, where it took 1) and `AbiHandlerTensor.AFlexibleWindowUnderAFractionalBlockRunsAtTheAnchorHop` (a flexible pass-through under the block 0.25 prepares, reports the fixed window's latency and moves the stream whole).
- **Breaking:** C ABI — every engine binds its tensors by name where the engine's side has names and by position otherwise, checked at prepare and reported per slot, on every built-in engine; alpha.1's tensor record bound by name only where the entry named a tensor and positionally otherwise, and accepted a name on a side its engine binds by position and ignored it. A slot whose entry's `tensors` record names it binds to the engine tensor of that name (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at prepare, listing the file's names, when the engine has none of that name); a slot without a record binds to the engine tensor of its canonical name where the side has one and to the tensor at the slot's position otherwise (ONNX Runtime's graph order, the signature's key order on TFLite and LiteRT, the method's argument order on LibTorch, the method's order on ExecuTorch); every engine tensor of a side ends up bound exactly once (a duplicate or an unbound engine tensor is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming it); whichever way a slot was bound, the engine tensor's element type, rank and every static extent are checked against the spec's engine dims (the entry's layout applied; a dynamic extent of the file matches anything), a mismatch being `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the slot, the engine tensor and both shapes; `anira_plan_slot.binding` reports `ANIRA_BINDING_NAME` / `ANIRA_BINDING_POSITION` per slot of every plan (a registered engine's `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`) and the prepare log's slot line says `bound by name` / `bound by position`. A name in a `tensors` record on a side its engine binds by position (every ExecuTorch tensor, a LibTorch output) is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at `anira_handler_create` ("binds by position; drop the name, keep the layout") instead of being accepted and ignored. The rule is one shared piece of the engine room (`anira::engine::bind_slots`, `check_engine_tensor`, `bind_side` in `src/engines/Adapter.h`) that every built-in adapter calls at prepare. Migration: drop a name the refusal names and keep its layout; a model whose canonical names match the export's tensor names binds by name without a record.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — after a miss anira no longer delivers late samples. The catch-up after a missed block (`InferenceManager::process_output`) kept a block's worth of late samples to deliver when fewer than the missing count plus the block had arrived, so the first block after a run of misses could begin with samples from earlier stream positions (late by 2 to 154 samples in the copy-oracle scenarios, by up to a whole block after an overload), as alpha.1's 2.x runtime did. Now every late sample that has arrived is discarded, and what the discards free is refilled at once (the collection places late chunks and the fills of dropped hops that waited for room): a block is either the stream at exactly its latency, a dropped hop's policy fill at its own position, or a miss under the policy (`ANIRA_MISSED`, count 0). A block that carried late samples now takes the miss policy, and a host that stays ahead of the stream keeps missing, since what arrives is late for a pop already made. Migration: none for a host that keeps pace; one that counted on the late samples after a miss gets the policy's fill instead. The copy-oracle goldens were re-recorded once for this, with Fares' authorisation (2026-10-31): 13 transcripts of `copy_path_oracle_golden.h` (`CopyPathOracle`, `TensorStems`) and 10 of `handler_copy_oracle_golden.h` (`AbiHandlerCopyOracle`); a call differs only where it delivered late samples (now a miss) and in what follows from that (ring levels, the Static row of the block, a later HOLD_LAST fill), the commit lists every one. `AbiHandlerTensor.AHostAheadOfTheFractionalBlockStarves` expects the host that pops ahead of the stream to keep missing. `test_Overload` holds with no late sample in every host block range and under every policy. Docs: `latency.rst`, `usage.rst` (section 1.3, *Miss policy*), `troubleshooting.rst`.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — a Hard call that carries more than the declared block on a stream (`ceil` of the block: `host_block_max` scaled from the anchor to the stream's hop, `ceil(b)` of a fractional block `b`) is refused with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, where alpha.1's 2.x runtime silently truncated it to the tail (a push larger than the send ring kept its last samples) or ran past the rings the latency was sized for. Every Hard entry that moves a Streamed slot checks it, on the push side and the pop side: `anira_handler_process`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, their `_multi` forms and the four `_wait` twins. The check is a compare against a figure computed at prepare (`StreamPort::m_max_samples`, from the same rational the latency calculation takes), runs after the descriptor checks and before anything moves (no ring is touched, nothing is submitted, the delivered counts are 0), is recorded last-wins in `anira_handler_rt_error` and logged once per kind as a contract violation; Static and State slots are unaffected. The 2.x shim (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`) inherits it: a 2.x host that passes more than its `HostConfig` buffer size (`ceil`) is refused through the shim, where 2.x truncated (the library's own 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` path stayed tolerant while it lived). Migration: declare the largest block you pass as `host_block_max` (`contract.hard_host_blocks(host_block_min, host_block_max, host_rate)`; on the shim, `HostConfig(largest_block, rate, true)`). Documented in `anira/abi/handler.h` (the per-call rule, now an enforced bound, and every Hard entry), `usage.rst` section 1.3 (*Host blocks*, *Fractional blocks*) and 3.2 (*The Hard entries*), `latency.rst` and the compat page of `migration.rst`. Tests: `AbiHandlerTensor.AHostBlockAboveTheDeclaredBlockIsRefused` (the bound passes, one above and four times the bound are refused on every entry under the host blocks 1..8 on a hop of 8 and under 0.25 on a hop of 1, the rings unchanged, one record, the stream going on after it) and `AbiCompat.ABlockAboveTheBufferSizeIsRefused` (the shim refuses 513 samples on a buffer size of 512 where the 2.x class takes them); `AbiPrepare.LatencyRulesAreConfigAtPrepare` pops its 4096 primed zeros in blocks of the declared 512, `OneSidedStreamingStandalone.GeneratorPushDataNeverSubmits` declares the hop it pops in one call as its block, and `OneSidedStreamingTest.GeneratorResetReanchors` pulls block by block until one pull submits (it passed one hop on a block of 512 and, refused, tested nothing). The C handler copy oracle keeps every call within its block; the scripts that passed more were rewritten, their golden transcripts recorded again once, and `AbiHandlerCopyOracle.MatchesTheV2CopyPath` runs every script on the 2.x copy path as the referee.
- **Breaking:** C++ — the enum alias of `anira_engine` in `anira/anira.hpp` is `anira::EngineKind` (alpha.1's `anira::Engine`, the name the class of a custom engine takes now): `ModelConfig::add_model_path(EngineKind, path)`, `add_model_bytes(EngineKind, ...)`, `model_engine(i)`, `default_engine(EngineKind)`, `ProcessorContext::engine()` and the `std::span<const anira::EngineKind>` candidate overloads of `<anira/compat/v3_to_v2.h>` (while the bridge lived) spell it. Migration: a consumer that wrote `anira::Engine` for the enum renames.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — libanira exports the C entry points and nothing else. The C++ classes and functions of namespace anira (the scheduler, the engine adapters, the carriers, the logger, `StatusError`, ...) lose their `ANIRA_API` decoration, and the export allowlist is `tanh_set_export_allowlist(anira SYMBOL "anira_*")` alone, where alpha.1's also admitted namespace anira: before #229 a shared libanira exported 358 C++ symbols beside its 232 C entries (alpha.1, with 88 promised C entries, exported its 2.x classes too), and now it exports exactly the C entries (`nm -D --defined-only libanira.so`: every name `anira_*`). Nothing a consumer can use goes away: `anira.hpp` and `anira/compat/v2.hpp` are header-only over the C entries, and the white-box suites link the library's objects. Gate 1 is strict: `anira_symbol_baseline` (`cmake/abi-symbols.cmake`) fails on any exported name outside `abi/symbols-0.txt` ∪ `abi/symbols-draft.txt`, apart from what the linker defines and, on PE, the exports the engine archives force (the ones `anira_exports` tolerates); `anira_exports` additionally forbids `moodycamel` and `nlohmann`. `docs/sphinx/troubleshooting.rst` no longer points at the internal `anira::capi::set_trace_failures`. Migration: a consumer that called a C++ symbol of libanira's (none is documented) calls the C entries, `anira.hpp` or the compat shim. The plugin-shaped module of the unload harness (`test/contracts/unload`) observes libanira through the C entries as well, where it called `anira::Core`'s statics: `anira_release_core_if_idle` proves that a failed construction leaves nothing behind, and on Windows its module exit destroys its instance before `anira_shutdown` (the C ABI has no forcing shutdown).
- **Breaking:** build — no test library is linked into anira. alpha.1's `cmake/test-deps.cmake` linked `gtest_main` PUBLIC into `anira` whenever tests or benchmarks were built, so googletest reached every consumer of the build tree, was installed with the package and made the installed `aniraConfig.cmake` run `find_package(GTest REQUIRED)`. Every gtest binary now links `gtest_main` itself (`anira_add_test_binary`, the unload harness, and the benchmarks through `anira_benchmark_fixture`); googletest is fetched without its install rules and the package config looks up no GTest (the `ANIRA_WITH_TESTS` / `ANIRA_WITH_BENCHMARK` variables it set for that lookup are gone with it). Migration: a project that used the build tree's `gtest_main` through `anira::anira` links its own (`GTest::gtest_main`); `docs/sphinx/benchmarking.rst` shows it.
- **Breaking:** C ABI — the pipeline's custom stage is a *processor* (decision 10.18). alpha.1's struct id `ANIRA_STRUCT_STAGE_CTX` (value 4, the `anira_stage_ctx` the design named) is `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX` (value 4, `anira_processor_ctx` of `anira/abi/processor.h`, 64 bytes); the value and the layout are unchanged. The phases (`ANIRA_PHASE_*`) keep their names, and "stage" stays the generic word (the inference stage). The words anira writes follow: the consumer column of a processor's `anira_plan_ext` rows reads `"processor"`, and its records and messages say "the processor". alpha.1's phase enum `anira_stage_phase` is `anira_phase` (terminator `ANIRA_PHASE_FORCE32`), renumbered so the inference-thread phases count in order: `ANIRA_PHASE_AFTER_INFERENCE`, `ANIRA_PHASE_PREPARE` and `ANIRA_PHASE_RELEASE` are 4, 5 and 6 (alpha.1: 3, 4 and 5), with `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` 3 and `ANIRA_PHASE_RESET` to `ANIRA_PHASE_QUERY` 7 to 12 (the C ABI's phase item carries the rest). Migration: spell `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX` and `anira_phase`, and recompile against the new values. The rest of the processor surface came after alpha.1 under stage names (Pre-release names), so no deprecation shims.
- **Breaking:** C++ — `anira::Hard::budget` is required. alpha.1's `Hard` carried `budget` as an `anira_budget_kind` (default `ANIRA_BUDGET_MEASURED`) and the figure in `budget_value` (nanoseconds, explicit only); the field is now an `anira::Budget`, which has no default constructor, so a `Hard` aggregate names it (`Hard{.host_block_min = 512, .host_block_max = 512, .host_rate = 48000, .budget = std::chrono::milliseconds(5), .warmup = ANIRA_WARMUP_FIXED, .warmup_iterations = 2}`: host blocks left at 0 beside a maximum are refused, and so is the default UNTIL_STABLE warm-up in this pre-release), as the web package's `budgetMs`. A `std::chrono` duration converts to an explicit budget; `Budget::measured()` is the measured one (refused at prepare in this pre-release). `Hard::budget_value` is gone (`budget.value()`, `budget.kind()`); `ContractHandle::hard()` reads the budget back as a `Budget`. Migration: replace `.budget = ANIRA_BUDGET_EXPLICIT, .budget_value = d` with `.budget = d`.
- **Breaking:** engines — the engine word reaches the file layout, the installed tree and the log groups. The installed package: the engines' headers go to `<includedir>/anira-engines/<engine>/` (alpha.1: `anira-backends/`), the package files that define the `anira::<engine>` targets are `aniraEngineHelpers.cmake` and `aniraEngineTargets.cmake` (alpha.1: `aniraBackendHelpers.cmake` and `aniraBackendTargets.cmake`; `aniraConfig.cmake` includes both, so a `find_package(anira)` consumer changes nothing), and the CPack component of the engine libraries is `deps-engines` (alpha.1: `deps-backends`). An engine's log records carry the group `anira.engine.<engine>` (alpha.1: `anira.backend.<engine>`; the constants `anira::log_group::k_engine_libtorch`, `k_engine_onnx`, `k_engine_tflite`, `k_engine_litert` and `k_engine_executorch`, alpha.1's `k_backend_*`). The tree: alpha.1's `src/backends` is `src/engines`, the engine room, whose namespace (`anira::backend` while it was drafted) is `anira::engine` (internal; the scheduler headers `anira/scheduler/Core.h`, `SessionElement.h` and `InferenceManager.h`, private now, name it); `cmake/backends.cmake` and `cmake/backends/<engine>.cmake` are `cmake/engines.cmake` and `cmake/engines/<engine>.cmake`; `test/backends` is `test/engines`, and its test binary `test_engines` (alpha.1: `test_backends`; `CMakePresets.json` and the mobile runner's lists follow); `docs/sphinx/custom_backends.rst` is `custom_engines.rst`. Migration: a build that adds the installed engine include directory by hand, a packaging script that selects the `deps-backends` component, or a log filter on `anira.backend.` spells the new name. Not renamed: the repository anira-project/backends, its release archives and the `ANIRA_BACKENDS_*` variables.
- **Breaking:** C++ — alpha.1's 2.x singleton `anira::Context` (`anira/scheduler/Context.h`: `Context::get_instance()` and its deprecated `get_instance(const ContextConfig&)` with the staged configuration behind it, the four- and the deprecated three-argument `create_session`) and its `anira::ContextConfig` (`anira/ContextConfig.h`) are no longer public.
