The process-wide singleton the scheduler registers with is the *core*, `anira::Core` (`anira/scheduler/Core.h` between the pre-releases, the private `src/scheduler/Core.h` now; its private state struct is `Core::State`), and it is all static: both `get_instance` overloads, the staged configuration behind the deprecated one and the three-argument `create_session` are gone, with `InferenceManager::get_core()` (alpha.1's `InferenceManager::get_context()`), and a handler needs no reference to the core (between the pre-releases a caller of `get_instance` moved to `Core::create_session(pp, config, custom, core_config)` and the other statics; that form left with the 2.x classes, and `create_session` takes the plan requests and an `anira_context_config` now). The log group `anira.context` is `anira.core`; the 2.x JSON key `context_config` is unchanged (it is the document format). The names `anira::Context` and `anira::ContextConfig` are the `anira.hpp` classes over `anira_context` / `anira_context_config` (Added). Migration: a host creates an `anira::Context` from an `anira::ContextConfig` (in C an `anira_context*` from `anira_context_create` over an `anira_context_config`), which the handler takes; a 2.x program spells the 2.x struct `anira::v2::ContextConfig` of `anira/compat/v2.hpp`; `Core::shutdown()` and `Core::make_inference_thread()`, the two members the guides showed (`Context::` in alpha.1), are `anira_shutdown` (`anira::shutdown()`) and `anira_inference_thread_create` (`anira::InferenceThread`); a log filter on `anira.context` spells `anira.core`.
- **Breaking:** C++ — alpha.1's installed scheduler thread class `anira::InferenceThread` (`anira/scheduler/InferenceThread.h`) is the library's private `anira::scheduler::InferenceThread` (`src/scheduler/InferenceThread.h`; `anira::Core::make_inference_thread` returns it). The name `anira::InferenceThread` belongs to the `anira.hpp` class over `anira_inference_thread` (Added): no class of namespace `anira` is defined both in libanira and in the header-only `anira.hpp`, on any link (an inline definition beside the library's would interpose on a shared library and resolve strong-over-weak on a static one). Migration: a host that drove the 2.x thread directly moves to `anira_inference_thread_*` or the `anira.hpp` class.
- **Breaking:** C++ — alpha.1's `ANIRA_REALTIME` is gone. The real-time attribute is `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` of `anira/abi/export.h`, clang's `nonblocking` attribute wherever clang has it, no longer gated on `ANIRA_WITH_RTSAN`: the eight real-time declarations of the 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` (`process`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, `reset`) carried it until that class left, and the Hard entries of the `anira.hpp` handler carry it; `src/utils/RealtimeSanitizer.h` (private) keeps `ANIRA_BLOCKING` alone. `anira_objects` (the OBJECT library libanira is built from) compiles with `-Wno-function-effects` under clang until the internal chain is annotated; consumer-shaped targets take `-Werror=function-effects` (gate 6). Migration: spell `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` and include `anira/abi/export.h`.
- **Breaking:** C++ — alpha.1's installed `anira::InferenceManager` (`anira/scheduler/InferenceManager.h`, private now) takes host tensors only. Its seven `float***` overloads are gone (alpha.1's `process`, `push_data`, `pop_data` and the deadline form of `pop_data`, and the `process_nowait`, `process_wait` and `pop_data_wait` that came between the pre-releases, all over `const float* const* const*` / `float* const* const*` beside `size_t*` count arrays), and with them the count-array form of `contract_wait_budget` (the tensor form measures the block by `shape[1]` of the anchored tensor); the returned `const size_t*` is the manager's own array of delivered counts (0 for every slot of a missed block), so the caller's request array is no longer overwritten. The copy core moves streamed slots only: `InferenceManager`'s stems never read the tensor of a non-streamable slot (whatever it holds: the whole Static tensor of a C `_multi` call, of any rank), report 0 for it and leave its memory alone on a miss; its non-streamed branches (`process_input`, `process_output`, the `HOLD_LAST` and the unclamped zeroing arms of a miss) are gone, and with them the manager's reference to the `PrePostProcessor`; Static tensors travel through the C handler's own store. `InferenceManager::miss_policy()` reads back what `set_miss_policy()` stored. The recorded copy-path suites `CopyPathOracle` and `AbiHandlerCopyOracle` pass with their transcripts untouched. No C entry and no registry line changes with it. Migration, for code that drove an `InferenceManager` directly: drive the C handler or the `anira.hpp` handler with one `anira_tensor` per slot. For channel pointers that is `anira_tensor_init_host_planar(&t, channels, num_channels, ANIRA_DTYPE_F32, 2, shape)` with `shape = {num_channels, num_samples}` (`{1, num_values}` for a non-streamable slot; `Tensor::from_host_planar` in C++), a slot the call leaves out is an empty tensor (`shape[1] == 0`, memory may be NULL), and in place is the same tensor on both sides.
- **Breaking:** install — anira installs its public header set alone, and the core's C++ headers are private. alpha.1 installed the whole `include/anira` directory (the 2.x classes and the core's headers with them, `anira/utils/Logger.h` and `anira/utils/RealtimeSanitizer.h` among them, now `src/utils/`); `cmake/install.cmake` now installs exactly the files `cmake/public-headers.cmake` lists — `anira.h`, `anira_all.h`, `anira_em.h`, `anira.hpp`, `compat/v2.hpp`, every `abi/*.h` and `abi/draft/*.h` — plus the generated `abi/build_info.h`; a header under `include/anira` that the list does not name fails the configure, and the per-file header gates of `test/abi` take the same list (the gates, below). The fourteen core headers that were still installed from `include/anira/` beside the C ABI once the 2.x classes had gone — `scheduler/{Core,InferenceManager,InferenceThread,LatencyCalculator,SessionElement}.h` (`scheduler/Core.h` is alpha.1's `scheduler/Context.h`), `utils/{Logger,RealtimeSanitizer,RingBuffer,RtLatch,Semaphore}.h` (`RtLatch.h` new since alpha.1) and `system/{Exports,AniraExports,AniraWinExports,HighPriorityThread}.h` — leave the public tree: the scheduler and utility headers move next to their sources under `src/` (the library includes them by relative path, the white-box suites through `src/` on their include path), and the four `system/` headers are deleted (`HighPriorityThread`, deprecated since 2.x, with `test_HighPriorityThread`; `Exports.h` and its two former names, since no C++ symbol is exported and the C entries take `ANIRA_API` from `anira/abi/export.h`). The API reference loses the pages of the private classes; the guides name them as internal. Migration: code that included `<anira/scheduler/...>`, `<anira/utils/...>` or `<anira/system/...>` uses `anira.hpp` or the C ABI (none of those classes was documented for consumers).
- **Breaking:** install — tanh-lib, concurrentqueue and nlohmann_json are private and no longer installed (`docs/anira-v3-architecture.md`, section 6b). alpha.1 linked the three PUBLIC into `anira`, installed the queue and the JSON library with it (`install(TARGETS concurrentqueue nlohmann_json)`, `include/concurrentqueue`, `include/nlohmann`) and tanh-lib beside it (`libtanh_core`, `include/tanh`), and its `aniraConfig.cmake` ran `find_package(concurrentqueue)`, `find_package(nlohmann_json)` and `find_dependency(tanh)` (the `Config.cmake.in` comment called anira's `Buffer` / `RingBuffer` / `MemoryBlock` aliases over tanh-lib's). Now anira builds tanh-lib's core (the ring storage, the logger and the inference threads' `thl::core::Thread`) itself, as `anira_tanh_core`: the repository is fetched under anira's own FetchContent name, `anira_tanh_lib`, and only populated, at the commit of `cmake/tanh-lib-pin.cmake` (the one file the install consumers read too), and the core is defined by the pinned tree's own `tanh_add_core_library(anira_tanh_core SOURCE_DIR … ABI_NAMESPACE anira STATIC LOG_COMPILED_MAX_LEVEL 4)`: a hidden static archive in the inline namespace `thl::anira` whose objects are part of libanira, shared or static (`$<TARGET_OBJECTS:anira_tanh_core>`), so libanira links no `libtanh_core` and names no `thl` symbol in its dynamic table; the queue is fetched without its own CMake, whose install rules shipped it, and the JSON library installs nothing as a subproject. The three are build-time requirements of anira's objects alone: anira's link interface and its installed package carry none of them, and `Config.cmake.in` looks up `Threads` alone. Android links `log` for the absorbed objects. anira never runs tanh-lib's top-level CMakeLists.txt, so it builds its pin whatever a parent project declared under `tanh-lib`, and a parent's `tanh_core` and anira's `anira_tanh_core` coexist in one build; the sanitizer options reach `anira_tanh_core` through `tanh_add_sanitizer`, with tanh-lib's own defines and its ASan ignorelist. A host that uses tanh-lib itself keeps a tanh-lib, a logger and a log level of its own, which anira never touches, beside a shared or a static libanira on every platform and in any link order: the host's tanh-lib is in tanh-lib's default namespace (`thl::v<major>_<minor>`), anira's in `thl::anira`, and no symbol of the two shares a mangled name, header-only code included (`docs/sphinx/troubleshooting.rst`, "A host that uses tanh-lib itself"). In anira's build tree the benchmarks and `test_InferenceHandler`, which call tanh-lib (a thread priority, a WAV reader), link `anira_tanh_core`, as anira's own; the benchmarking guide's project fetches a tanh-lib of its own for its priority call. Migration: a consumer that used tanh-lib, the queue or the JSON library through `anira::anira` links them itself.
- **Breaking:** build — the build switches stop at the build tree. alpha.1's `anira::anira` carried the `USE_<ENGINE>` definitions, `ENABLE_LOGGING`, `ANIRA_VERSION` and `TORCH_CXX_FLAGS` PUBLIC, into every consumer and the installed package. Now `USE_*`, `ENABLE_LOGGING` and `ANIRA_VERSION` reach the bundled consumers (the extras, the examples, `test_InferenceHandler`) through anira's build interface only, and `TORCH_CXX_FLAGS` the objects and the white-box suites only (no C++ type crosses the library boundary, so a consumer has no `std::string` ABI to match); `USE_ANIRA_WEB` keys on `TANH_OPERATING_SYSTEM`. Which engines a build compiled in is `anira_enabled_engines` (README, `custom_engines.rst`), and `test_EngineLinkage` reads what a consumer can read: `anira_enabled_engines` (the count, then a stride-sized array) for the engines and `ANIRA_STATIC` for the shape. Migration: a consumer that read `USE_*` from anira asks `anira_enabled_engines`.
- **Breaking:** packaging — the static libanira is self-contained. alpha.1's static `libanira.a` held anira's objects alone and needed the installed `libtanh_core.a` beside it, hidden on ELF by the `--exclude-libs,libtanh_core.a` link option `anira::anira` carried. The archive now carries tanh-lib's core objects, and on ELF (Linux, Android) it is one prelinked relocatable object with only the `anira_*` entries global (`cmake/prelink-archive.cmake`, described with the static libanira on ELF), so the `--exclude-libs,libtanh_core.a` stopgap goes with the archive it hid. Migration: a static consumer links `anira::anira` (the archive and the engines' link closure) and no tanh-lib; a hand-written link line drops `libtanh_core.a`.
- **Breaking:** binary name — the shared library is named after the ABI pair (`cmake/build-info.cmake`), not after the release. alpha.1's soname was the release major 3 (`libanira.so.3`, `anira.dll`) and its file version the release; the soname is now the ABI major, `libanira.so.0`, `libanira.0.dylib` and `anira-0.dll` (+ `anira-0.lib`) through the 3.0.0 pre-releases and 3 from v3.0.0 on, the file version is the pair (`libanira.so.0.2`), and on Mach-O the current and the compatibility version are both the pair, so a client linked against one ABI minor refuses an older library. A static PE archive keeps the name `anira.lib`. The Windows copy lists of the tests and the examples take the DLL from `$<TARGET_FILE:anira>`, and `aniraConfigVersion.cmake` is `SameMajorVersion` (alpha.1: `AnyNewerVersion`): `find_package(anira 3)` takes any 3.x and nothing else. Migration: nothing for a CMake consumer; a hand-written link line or loader path that named `libanira.so.3` / `anira.dll` names the new file; the alpha.1 zips were no binary promise. The Debian runtime package keeps its name `libanira<release major>` (tanh-tooling's `tanh_cpack_debian` names it, with no soname parameter yet).
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** a stateless model that sets no `max_instances` gets as many shared instances as the context's inference pool has threads (at least 1; 1 when the host brings its own threads), 2.x's default (`num_parallel_processors` defaulted to half the hardware threads, the default pool's size) instead of alpha.1's 1, so a pool's threads can run one model's inferences at once. The default is the new registry constant `ANIRA_INSTANCES_AUTO` (`0xffffffffu`, beside `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO`): what `anira_model_config_create` stores and `anira_model_config_max_instances` / `ModelConfig::max_instances()` read back unresolved, what `anira_model_config_set_max_instances` accepts besides a count of at least 1 (0 is still refused), and the `max_instances` key left out of a model document (`to_json` leaves it out too). `Core::create_session` resolves it, silently, when the handler is prepared, before the session is built and before the plans meet the pool's keys, so two handlers that leave the count to the pool share one loaded model; an explicit count wins, clamped to the pool as before; a stateful model or one with a declared State pair is unchanged (no shared instance, one executor per handler). The upgrade of a 2.x model document without `num_parallel_processors` keeps `ANIRA_INSTANCES_AUTO` (alpha.1 wrote half the hardware threads), and the shim's (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`) `InferenceConfig` constructors default their count to it (a document without the key; the shim reads it back as `Defaults::num_parallel_processors()`); the bridge's `anira::v3compat::to_inference_config` carried the sentinel into `num_parallel_processors` while it lived. Memory: every instance is a full copy of the model (one engine executor over the loaded model), so a model with no `max_instances` now takes up to the pool's thread count of copies; set `max_instances(1)` to keep one. Latency: the latency calculation counts on the instances (`n`), so a model whose inference takes longer than a hop gets a lower computed latency with the default than with 1. The documented default changes from 1 (`anira_model_config_create`, `anira_model_config_set_max_instances`, `usage.rst`); no signature changes. Tests: `AbiInstanceDispatch.MaxInstancesLeftToThePoolIsThePoolsThreadCount`, `.TwoHandlersLeftToThePoolShareOneLoadedModel`, `.AnExplicitCountWinsAndAStatefulModelHasNoSharedInstance`, `.MaxInstancesLeftToThePoolIsOneWithoutAPool`; the read-back, JSON upgrade, fixture, translate and engine-record tests expect the sentinel or the pool's count.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** a model entry of a custom engine that declares `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL` needs no source, where alpha.1 required a path or bytes on every entry. A JSON entry may leave out `"path"`, `anira_model_config_add_model_path` takes a NULL path for such an entry (an empty one is still refused), and C++ gains `ModelConfig::add_model(engine_id)`; `anira_handler_create` refuses a source-less entry of any other engine with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, naming the flag. The engine's load reads no path and no bytes (`anira_model_config_model_path` is NULL). A computing engine (a JavaScript passthrough, a test double) no longer carries a placeholder such as `"never-opened.bin"` and, on the web, `fetchModel: () => false` to keep `ModelConfig.fromUrl` from fetching it; the examples, tests and guides drop both. The 2.x JSON loader's `model_path` stays required (and the 2.x bridge handed the CUSTOM backend a stand-in source it never opened while it lived).
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** a version 2 document whose root also carries a 3.x key (`models` beside `inference_config`, `num_threads` beside `context_config`, ...) is refused as `ANIRA_ERROR_JSON` naming the key, instead of being read as 2.x with the 3.x key ignored.
- **C ABI, configuration (`anira/abi/config.h`, JSON):** a Static or a Buffer tensor spec may carry a Channel axis of any extent: `anira_handler_create`, `anira_handler_prepare` and the 2.x bridge (while it lived) no longer refuse such a spec with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` ("a Static tensor's Channel axis must have extent 1"). The Channel tag maps onto ring channels on a Streamed tensor only; a non-Streamed tensor has the spec's shape, the tagged axis being one of its axes, and moves as the product of its extents (the 2.x config the translator built carried it as one channel with the full dims in every shape). At most one Channel axis per spec stays the rule for every role. The rule is about the spec: a Buffer spec with such an axis is valid in a model config and a model file, while `anira_handler_prepare` refuses any Buffer spec under a Hard contract (see the Static entries under Added).
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** the three miss policies of alpha.1 are implemented in the scheduler's starvation path, so the `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` default of `anira_contract_create_hard`, of `anira::Hard{}` and of the bundled contract files is truthful (alpha.1's bridge refused every policy but BYPASS while the 2.x runtime delivered zeros on a miss): `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS` delivers zeros (the 2.x behaviour), `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST` repeats the last delivered block of every streamed output (one block-sized buffer per output channel, allocated at prepare), `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` copies the block the call pushed into the anchored input into the output on the process forms (the smaller of the input's and the output's sample count, zeros past the input; a call that pushed another slot, and a pop, deliver zeros); a Static output holds its stored value under every policy (Added). A missed block returns `ANIRA_MISSED` under every policy, with every delivered count 0 (the policy governs the buffer's content, the status says the block was missed), the stream stays time-aligned, and an output whose request is 0 is left untouched. BYPASS is refused at prepare with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming `on_miss` when the anchor is an output (no anchored input to pass through: a generator, or a named output anchor) and when a streamed output's channel count differs from the anchored input's; hence `rave_funk_drum_encoder.contract.json` and `rave_funk_drum_decoder.contract.json` spell `"on_miss": "zeros"` (1 audio channel against 4 latents, and the decoder's output anchor), every other bundled file keeps the default, and the Hard contract a version 2 document is upgraded to carries `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS` (what the 2.x runtime delivered on a miss).
- **C ABI, handler (`anira/abi/handler.h`):** a dropped hop is filled under the miss policy, and `rt_error` reports it. A hop the scheduler drops because every inference slot is busy gets the contract's `on_miss` fill (Fares' decision), where alpha.1's 2.x runtime gave it zeros under every policy: `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS` zeros, `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` the dropped hop of the anchored input itself (the dry signal at its own stream position), `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST` the head of the last block the output delivered, `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` what `anira_miss_fn` makes of the hop: it is also called once per dropped hop, at the drop, on the driver thread, with hop-shaped arrays (every Streamed input slot's dropped hop and every Streamed output slot's hop to fill, `[channels, hop]` of the ring dtype, packed, the handler's memory; every other slot empty). The fills are made at the drop and kept until their ordered placement in a ring the manager allocates at prepare (`ceil(latency / hop) + 2` hops: a fill held longer could only reach the host late; an older one is placed as zeros). `anira_handler_rt_error` reports a drop as `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY` under its own latch kind (`RtKind::Dropped`, `RtLatch::record_drop`); an entry's `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY` return still never lands there. `AbiRtLatch.CapacityNeverLatches` is `AnEntrysCapacityNeverLatchesADroppedHopDoes`. Docs: `usage.rst` (section 1.3, the reset note), `latency.rst` (*When the inference does not keep up*), `troubleshooting.rst`, `logging.rst`, `anira_miss_fn` and `anira_handler_rt_error`.
- **Internal (the core on the 3.x types):** no behaviour change, every figure, stream and copy-path golden as before.
  - **The processor runs on the chunk.** `Core::pre_process`, the inference thread and `Core::post_process` call the handler's `capi::ProcessorRunner` with the chunk itself, and each phase returns its status; the 2.x `PrePostProcessor` leaves the session, `Core` and the inference thread, with the runner's struct table and its lookup by buffer address, the chunk's status field and the per-claim re-pointing of the descriptors. The plan's 2.x backend leaves the core with it (`PlanRequest` / `PlanSlot::m_legacy_backend`, `SessionElement::plan_backend`).
  - **The chunk's storage is anira's:** one allocation per chunk, every slot's buffer on a 64-byte boundary, the descriptors built once by the session's prepare; a State slot has no buffer (its pair is bound per inference). The 2.x `BufferF` vectors of the chunk are gone.
  - **The ring of a Streamed slot lives in its stream port** (`capi::StreamPort::m_ring`), sized in place by every session prepare; the session reads it through the port vectors (`SessionElement::send_ring` / `receive_ring`), and `m_send_buffer` / `m_receive_buffer` with the placeholder rings of the Static and State slots are gone.
  - **The logger's level is `anira_log_level`** (D11): `anira::set_log_level` / `get_log_level` / `to_thl_log_level` and the ONNX Runtime and LiteRT mappings take the C enum, `default_log_level()` moves into `src/utils/Logger.h` (`ANIRA_LOG_ERROR` in a release build, `ANIRA_LOG_INFO` otherwise, as before), and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO`'s count is `anira::Core::default_num_threads()`, beside `sanitize_config`.
  - **The session reads the 3.x configuration directly; the translation into the 2.x objects is gone** (`src/capi/v3_to_v2.{h,cpp}`). The C handler installs the contract snapshot (`m_contract`) and what the validator derived for its variant (`m_derived`, a `capi::Derived`, which carries `m_exclusive`) before it builds the session, and the session, `Core`, the manager, the inference thread and the latency calculation read them, with the model config, by reference: the extents, hop and channel count of every tensor, the anchor, the exclusivity, the contract's host blocks, budget and wait ratio. The handler's `m_inference_config` and `m_host_config` are gone. `SessionElement` and `InferenceManager` take the model config, the derivation and the Hard contract, `SessionElement::prepare` / `Core::prepare_session` / `InferenceManager::prepare` take no host configuration (and `InferenceManager::prepare()` clears `last_block_missed()`, as its documentation says), and `LatencyCalculator` takes `(const capi::Derived&, const capi::HardContract&, uint32_t num_instances)` (the scheduler headers forward-declare the private types). The session's instance count is resolved by `Core::create_session` into the session (`m_num_instances`; 1 for an exclusive session, as before) instead of being written back into the caller's configuration. The host blocks stay double from the contract to the core: the host block in hops comes from the contract's own `host_block_max` (the float narrowing of the 2.x `HostConfig` goes), and a slot's block in samples (`LatencyCalculator::block_samples`, the most one call carries and the held block of `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST`) from that rational alone. The suites' latency figures are unchanged, the fractional host blocks included. `validate`'s `backend_of` is `engine_enabled`, the missing-host-blocks refusal moved from the translator into the handler's prepare (same status, same message), and `test_Translate` is `test_Validate`, which checks what the validator derives instead of the 2.x objects made from it.
- **Internal (the core on the 3.x types):** the port invariants are enforced.
  - The ports never move. A side's ports are `capi::Ports`, made once with their count by `anira_handler_create` (`anira_handler` takes the two counts). The type has no copy, no move and nothing that changes its count, where a `std::vector<Port>` only promised it in a comment. The session's rings, the processor runner and every processor frame hold the ports' addresses across calls; they take `const capi::Ports&` / `capi::Ports&`.
  - Every tensor with a stream has a stream port. `capi::stream_port_refusal(derived, inputs, outputs)` names a slot with a hop but without a stream port, or a side whose port count is not its tensor count. `anira_handler_create` and `anira_handler_prepare` assert it in a debug build and refuse with `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL` in every build, before anything reads a ring. `SessionElement::send_ring` / `receive_ring`, which dereference the stream port of every slot with a hop, assert it too.
  - Pinned by `static_assert`s in `test_Handler.cpp` (no copy, no move, no resize: the same check sees `std::vector`'s), by `AbiHandler.ThePortsKeepTheirAddressesAcrossPrepares` (every port, a stream port's ring and a static port's value at their create addresses through two prepares of different host blocks) and by `AbiHandler.EveryTensorWithAStreamHasAStreamPort` (the prepared handler holds it; a derivation that gives the Static slot a hop, and one with a missing output, are named).
- **Internal (the core on the 3.x types):** one host<->ring copy path, over `anira_tensor`. `InferenceManager`'s `process_input`, `process_output` and the three miss policies (`hold_output`, `bypass_output`, `clear_output`) are written once over `const anira_tensor*`, one tensor per slot in slot order, and every float face ran on it bit for bit: the 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` of the snapshots kept its signatures, its in/out count protocol and its return values and presented the caller's channel pointers as planar float32 tensors that prepare builds once (a call stores `shape[1]` and one pointer per channel; nothing is allocated on the driver thread), through `anira::PlanarFloatAdapter`, whose `PlanarFloatAdapter::present_slot` treats a NULL channel array as a slot the call does not carry, an empty tensor, as the adapter's header promises (a 2.x single-form call (`InferenceHandler::process` / `push_data` / `pop_data` with a tensor index) could hand it a slot whose channel array was NULL with a count above 0, since the per-slot arrays keep what earlier calls left; found by a full local clang-tidy sweep with the LLVM 20.1.0 wheel, clang-analyzer-core.NullDereference, which CI's clang-tidy 20.1.8 does not report). The float core was recorded first (`test_scheduler` suite `CopyPathOracle`, `test_abi` suite `AbiHandlerCopyOracle`: every float a call writes and every float it leaves alone, under the three miss policies) and the rewritten core reproduces the recording (`AbiHandlerCopyOracle` reproduces its six recorded transcripts through the tensor entries with `handler_copy_oracle_golden.h` byte-identical; the driver reproduces `num_out=[..]` of the recorded multi calls and says so; the C-face oracle `test/abi/handler_copy_oracle_golden.h` was recorded again for its Static lines alone, in the five transcripts that have a Static slot, where a call carried a non-empty Static element or named a Static slot, and only in the call label, the Static slot's request and `delivered` / `num_out` word and the Static slot's float row, every status, `rt_error=`, ring level, `settled:` line and row of a Streamed slot byte-identical, which a masked comparison of the two files showed; the manager-face oracle `test/scheduler/copy_path_oracle_golden.h` is untouched by it). On `InferenceManager`, as the C++ face the tensor entries call: `process_nowait(inputs, outputs)`, `process_wait(inputs, outputs, budget, outcome)`, `push_data(inputs)`, `pop_data(outputs)`, `pop_data_wait(outputs, budget, outcome)` and `contract_wait_budget(inputs, outputs)` over `const anira_tensor*` arrays, returning the manager's own array of delivered counts, and the two 2.x behaviours in the same form: `process(inputs, outputs)`, the dispatcher on `blocking_ratio`, and `pop_data(outputs, wait_until)`, the deadline pop. A slot's tensor is a host block of the logical shape `[channels, samples]` (`[1, values]` for a non-streamable slot) in any of three descriptions, for any channel count: planar (`ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR`, `byte_offset` and `strides[1]` inside each plane), one block read by `strides` (interleaved is `{1, channels}`, contiguous `{samples, 1}`), or one block with all-zero strides (packed). Each channel moves between host memory and the ring in one strided ring call, with no intermediate copy; a unit stride is the ring's plain block copy, as before. The request is `shape[1]`; a slot whose `shape[1]` is 0 is not touched and its memory is not read; a descriptor is never written; in place is the same tensor on both sides. The copy is dtype-generic: the element type is the tensor's, which must be the slot's ring dtype (float32 for a non-streamable slot), nothing converts, and `HOLD_LAST` holds bytes of the ring's element type. The stems trust their descriptors (the caller validates them; `src/scheduler/TensorRun.h` carries that check, `check_host_tensor`, beside the run helper) and check the dtype only: a mismatched input is not pushed, a mismatched output is zero-filled and reports 0 samples, logged once through the real-time queue under the new site `RtSite::TensorDtypeMismatch`, so a float32 call on a session prepared with a non-float32 `RingDtypes` entry never reports a delivered block over memory it did not write. On a `BYPASS` miss, input and output memory that overlap under two different descriptions are undefined; the identical run (in place) is left where it is.
- **Internal (the core on the 3.x types):** the session of a C-created handler runs one processor, the processor runner (`ProcessorRunner`, `src/capi/processor.cpp`), whether its pipeline has a processor or not: without one it runs the two default bodies, which are the streamed branches of the 2.x default `PrePostProcessor`, and both recorded copy-path oracles stay byte-identical. Static tensors travel through the handler's Static store (the float atomics the processor inherits from the 2.x processor are not used). `anira_ring` (`src/utils/RingBuffer.h`) gains `set_owner` / `hop` / `owner` (the hop of its slot and the owning session's `anira::RingOwner`: the latch a refused accessor records into and the processor running on the driving thread), set by `SessionElement::prepare`; `SessionElement::ThreadSafeStruct` gains `m_processor_status`, which `Core::pre_process` and `InferenceThread::do_inference` read after the processor's hooks (a 2.x processor never wrote it); `anira::RtLatch` gains `record_any` and `RtKind::Other`, so that a processor's status without a kind bit of its own still reaches `rt_error`; `InferenceManager::session()` hands the processor runner the session it binds to.
- **ABI tooling and gates:** release: `on_tag.yml`'s `publish_npm` checks before it publishes: `gen.py --check` on the committed C ABI files and, after its wasm build, the same `.d.ts` export check as `build_web` (exactly the names of `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt` and `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt`, plus `_malloc`, `_free`). Its `needs:` gains `build_web` (now also a reusable workflow, `workflow_call`), so the node ABI gates and the npm build of the tag pass before anything is published; a hyphenated version such as `3.0.0-alpha.2` goes to the `next` dist-tag.
- **ABI tooling and gates:** generator: a 64-bit sentinel define of `web/src/abi/enums.ts` (`ANIRA_DYNAMIC`, `ANIRA_UNBOUNDED`) is typed `bigint` instead of its literal type, which the documentation's TypeDoc pass (sphinx-js) cannot serialize now that the package root re-exports the enums.
- **Build:** anira's concurrentqueue is `anira_moodycamel`. The queue's templates carry no ABI namespace, so in a static libanira their instantiations under `moodycamel::` would merge by name with a host's own concurrentqueue on every platform. `target_compile_definitions(anira_concurrentqueue INTERFACE moodycamel=anira_moodycamel)` renames the token in every TU that includes the queue (the objects and the white-box suites); nothing is patched on disk, no public header names the queue, and the export check of `test/contracts/unload` forbids `anira_moodycamel` beside `moodycamel` (measured: the prelinked static archive names `10moodycamel` in 53 symbols before, in none after).
- **Build:** static libanira on ELF (Linux, Android): one prelinked object, only `anira_*` global. A static `libanira.a` is linked into its consumer's image, where hidden visibility does not keep anything apart: a consumer's reference to a symbol binds to the archive's hidden definition like to any object's. `cmake/prelink-archive.cmake` now links anira's and `anira_tanh_core`'s objects into one relocatable object (the compiler driver's `-r`, with a clang driver's `--target` when cross-compiling, `--build-id=none` so that a consumer's image carries one build-id note, and none of the build's compile flags, so that no sanitizer or coverage runtime is linked into the object; a flag that changes the relocatable output, such as `-m32` on a 64-bit host, is not supported) and makes every defined global hidden symbol of it local except the `anira_*` entries (`readelf -sW` + `objcopy --localize-symbols`): anira's C++ internals are unreachable from the consumer's objects (tanh-lib's code and the queue's are kept apart from a host's on every platform by their names, `thl::anira` and `anira_moodycamel`), while the weak COMDAT instantiations stay as they are (localizing those lets the final link discard a group under its references; with the namespaces only anira's own instantiations share a group) and undefined references (the C++ runtime, the engines) stay global. The archive holds that object and an empty C++ translation unit that names its link language, so CMake links a consumer with the C++ runtime as before; that source is written only when its text changes, so a reconfigure re-archives nothing. A multi-config generator (one prelinked object cannot hold the objects of several configurations) and a toolchain without `readelf` and `objcopy` keep the plain archive, and the configure log names the path taken (`anira: static libanira prelinked (ELF; only anira_* global)` or `... is a plain archive (<reason>)`); the static Android legs of `build_test_mobile.yml` check that both trees' `libanira.a` is the prelinked object, so a fallback there fails instead of passing silently; Mach-O and PE archives keep their objects (anira's internals stay hidden there, and tanh-lib's and the queue's are separate by name), and a shared libanira and Wasm are unaffected. `consumer_tanh_first` runs against every static package too (the gate 5 item).
- **Extras and examples:** the advanced benchmark selects its model config and its contract in one call: `model_and_contract(model, buffer_size)` returns both (`ModelAndContract`, the contract loaded from the model's contract file), replacing `model_config_of`, whose name said half and which handed the contract file back through an out-parameter. `docs/sphinx/benchmarking.rst` shows the new shape. The simple and cnn-size benchmarks and the fixture had no such out-parameter.
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`):** `anira.hpp` refuses `ANIRA_CXX_MANUAL_INIT` and `ANIRA_NO_PROTOTYPES` for good (anira links its entry points; there is no `anira_get_proc_address`), where alpha.1 refused them until the handler half landed; `ANIRA_CXX_NO_EXCEPTIONS` stays unavailable in this pre-release.
- **Built-in engines:** The built-in engines have the init level a custom engine has. The core hands out one engine object per compiled-in engine while a loaded model of the engine holds it (`Core::builtin_engine`, `engine::BuiltinEngine`, the twin of a custom engine's carrier) and runs its init once per object, with the same `anira_init_info`, under the lifecycle lock, right before the engine's first load; what an engine keeps per process moved there from every load: ONNX Runtime's environment (one per process, its logger at the level in effect at init; every session of every loaded model is created over it), LiteRT's environment (likewise, with the accelerators its registration finds, which a load's record is checked against), ExecuTorch's `runtime_init()`, LibTorch's intra-op thread count and c10 log level; TensorFlow Lite keeps nothing per process. The core holds the objects weakly: every loaded model of the engine holds the one object and the last to let go frees it on its own thread, so a failed load leaves nothing behind and the last unload frees the runtime's environment right there; the next load makes a new object whose init runs again (ExecuTorch's `runtime_init()` stays once per process). The context's probe asks the object for the engine's providers (`BuiltinEngine::providers`, the runtime asked at every probe, without the lifecycle lock). A load before the engine's init is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, for a built-in and a custom engine alike. Between the pre-releases the objects were first kept for the core's life and freed by the unload hook, at process exit too: on macOS, where libanira stays mapped and dyld runs an image's C++ static destructors before its terminators, a failed create left an ONNX Runtime environment behind that the hook released after ONNX Runtime's own statics were gone (`OrtEnv::Release` takes a static mutex: `mutex lock failed: Invalid argument`, the process aborting after `LibraryUnload.FailedCreateLeavesNoState`). Test: `CoreLifecycleTest.OneEngineObjectPerBuiltInEngineWhileSomethingHoldsIt`. Internal: `make_builtin_loaded` takes the engine object (`make_builtin_engine` makes one), `builtin_providers(engine)` and `litert_providers()` take no level, and `engine::Model` carries no log level.
- **Built-in engines:** ONNX Runtime runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/OnnxRuntimeAdapter.cpp`, file-local over the engine room's `Model`, replacing alpha.1's `OnnxRuntimeProcessor`): the environment is the engine object's, one per process (above; anira's log level on it, and ONNX Runtime keeps one environment per process behind every handle; the web build creates it with a global thread pool of one intra-op thread through `Ort::ThreadingOptions`, since a WebAssembly session cannot spawn its own, and the environment copies the options, which die at the end of the init); what one load shares between its executors is one session-options object (one intra-op thread), immutable after load and freed with the last of them, and each executor owns one `Ort::Session` over them, kept per executor so that no two executors share a session's allocator; every input of a call an `Ort::Value` created over the descriptor's memory and every output a value pre-created over the output descriptor's memory and handed to `Session::Run`, so no result is copied and a declared State pair will bind in place (the copied form is gone; a shape ONNX Runtime cannot honour fails the FIXED warm-up at load, within `anira_handler_prepare`, with its text); the float32 rule (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the engine and the first tensor of another dtype, before the file is opened); a descriptor the adapter cannot hand the engine fails the chunk with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` and a failing run with `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`, both latched by the scheduler, nothing cleared or recorded inside the adapter; the FIXED warm-up runs through the same process path over the instance's own scratch.
- **Built-in engines:** LibTorch runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/LibTorchAdapter.cpp`, replacing alpha.1's `LibtorchProcessor`): one TorchScript module per executor (a module is not shareable between threads, so every executor loads its own, with its own copy of the weights), what one load shares being the record and the binding tables alone; every input of a call is a `torch::from_blob` view over the descriptor's memory (no deleter, nothing owned, the pointer read anew per call), so the swap of the struct's memory is gone (`AbiProcessor.TensorsKeepTheStructsMemoryAcrossLibTorchInferences` asserts one input block per struct across uses); the input slots bind to the entry method's arguments after `self` by name where the entry's tensors record or the canonical name matches one and by argument position otherwise (a record name the method lacks is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` listing the arguments; a bound argument must be a tensor; every argument without a default value must be bound, a trailing argument with a default stays with it, so RAVE's `decode(z, from_forward=False)` binds its one input), the outputs bind by position to the method's returns (a tuple of tensors, one tensor or a tensor list; the schema's arity is checked where it says); the schema carries no shapes, so an output's dtype and shape are checked against the record at load, on the warm-up's first result or, for a record without a warm-up, on one forward on the probe (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming both shapes), never first at the first chunk, and every result is checked again per call (`ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`, nothing written); the result tensors are the engine's own and are copied once into the output descriptors' memory (a `memcpy` of a contiguous result, a `copy_` into a view over the memory otherwise); a module without the entry method is `ANIRA_ERROR_MODEL_LOAD` at load instead of a `c10::Error` at the warm-up.
- **Built-in engines:** ExecuTorch runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/ExecuTorchAdapter.cpp`, replacing alpha.1's `ExecuTorchProcessor`): what one load shares is one data loader and one `Program` (the .pte parsed once), and each executor owns one `Module` over the shared program with its own loaded entry method (the runtime's `Module` is not thread-safe; its constructor over a shared program is the documented way to run one program from several); every method is loaded with the XNNPACK delegate's runtime options `workspace_sharing_mode = 0` (a workspace per delegate instance) and `weight_cache_enabled = false` (no process-wide weight cache), which the delegate reads at its init, so two executors never meet on the process-wide mutexes the prebuilt runtime otherwise takes around every call, at the price of a workspace and an unpacked copy of the weights per executor. The engine's init also sets those options for the process (`executorch::runtime::set_option`: a workspace per delegate instance, no weight cache, what every method load names in its map too) and reads them back (`get_option`), warning when the runtime refuses them or reports other values, so a renamed option key of a later release cannot bring the shared workspace and its mutexes back in silence (`engine::executorch_xnnpack_options` reads them for the tests). Every method is loaded, warmed up and executed under `NoThreadPoolGuard`, so the delegate captures no thread pool and every call runs inline on the inference thread that made it (alpha.1's process-global pin of the XNNPACK thread pool to one thread through the deprecated `_unsafe_reset_threadpool(1)` is gone with it); both sides bind by position (the method meta carries no tensor names) and are checked against the method metas at load, whose sizes are the planned upper bound of every axis with nothing marking an axis dynamic, so an extent at or below the bound matches and one above it, or another rank or element type, is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming both shapes; every input of a call is copied from its descriptor into the instance's staging tensor (the program's inputs are the runtime's own) and every memory-planned result copied out into its output descriptor; the FIXED warm-up runs over the staging tensors.
- **Built-in engines:** TensorFlow Lite runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/TFLiteAdapter.cpp`, replacing alpha.1's `TFLiteProcessor`): what one load shares is one `TfLiteModel` (the flatbuffer parsed once) and one options object, and each executor owns one interpreter with its signature runner; the model's first signature through its `TfLiteSignatureRunner` when the file has one (the slots bind to the signature's keys by name where the entry's tensors record or the canonical name matches one and by the runner's index order otherwise, which lists the keys in key order: `output_0` before `output_1`), the interpreter itself for a file without a signature (the tensor names, the interpreter's order), every input the record's engine dims differ from resized to them before the allocation, every tensor checked against its allocated dims and type at load, every block copied from and into the descriptors' memory with `TfLiteTensorCopyFromBuffer` / `CopyToBuffer` per call (the swap of the struct's memory is gone; the interpreter's own alignment keeps the copy until the aligned chunk storage), and `reset` re-initialising the model's variable tensors through `TfLiteInterpreterResetVariableTensors` before the first inference of a new stream of an exclusive handler (no bundled model has variable tensors: the call is proven to be made, not to matter).
- **Built-in engines:** LiteRT runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/LiteRtAdapter.cpp`, replacing alpha.1's `LiteRtProcessor`): the environment is the engine object's, one per process (above, carrying anira's log level); what one load shares is one model and one compilation options object, and each executor owns one compiled model with its managed buffers; the model's first signature (LiteRT synthesizes one for a file without signatures, keyed by the tensor names), the slots bound to the signature's keys by name or, without a name match, by the key order of the signature (its tensor names sorted, as TensorFlow Lite's signature runner lists them: `output_0` before `output_1`, while the bundled exports list `output_1` first in the file), so one file binds the same on both engines, checked against the signature tensors' ranked types at load (a dynamic extent of the file matches anything), one managed host buffer per signature tensor in the signature's index order typed with the record's engine dims, filled from and read into the descriptors' memory through a lock and a memcpy per call. The bundled gain model loads and runs on LiteRT now: the earlier warm-up refusal ("Cannot auto-resize tensor args_0_1: no dims_signature exists") was a rank-1 buffer for the export's rank-3 static gain, which the file's litert row now describes.
- **The core:** The core (internal) reads the context config itself: `anira::Core::register_context` and `Core::create_session` take a `const anira_context_config&` (the struct behind `anira_context_config_create`), so the C enums `anira_wait_strategy`, `anira_log_level` and `anira_log_drain` reach `InferenceThread`, the log queue and the drain unconverted; `InferenceThread` takes an `anira_wait_strategy`; `create_session` and the `SessionElement` constructor take an optional `anira::RtLatch*` (the handler's real-time latch, the session's own when absent); the consumed-or-fail walk over a context's extensions runs once, at `anira_context_create`. A 3.x handler is a user of the core (`Core::register_handler` / `unregister_handler` / `get_num_handlers`), so `anira_shutdown` and `release_core_if_idle` refuse while one lives; `Core::get_wait_strategy` reports the strategy the pool runs. The three reconciliation warnings say "log level mismatch", "log drain mismatch", "wait strategy mismatch" ("a new context or session requested ...") instead of naming the 2.x config struct. A direct caller of `Core::create_session`, the in-tree white-box tests, builds an `anira_context_config` (they include `src/capi/handles.h`).
- **The core:** The scheduler runs every engine through one interface, the engine room's adapter (`src/engines/Adapter.h`, the shape of the C descriptor of `anira/abi/engine.h`). A session's plan table (`SessionElement::m_plans`, one `PlanSlot` per plan with the two pointers of the C lifecycle, the loaded model it runs on as a shared `anira::engine::Loaded` and the session's own `anira::engine::Prepared` over it, declared after the `Loaded` so that it dies first, its engine, provider and engine id) replaces alpha.1's five `#ifdef` processor pools of the core, the five typed processor members and the custom-processor pointer of the session and the five-way engine switch of the inference thread. `Core::create_session` takes the plans as `engine::PlanRequest`s (a C-created handler asks for exactly its plans through the `InferenceManager` constructor over requests, and the table is the session's from create), pools loaded models by the record of the model (`engine::Model`: engine, id, path or bytes identity, entry, per-slot names, dims and dtype, the shared slots, warm-up, and a custom engine's variant as canonical JSON; whether a session runs the model exclusively is the session's property, `engine::PrepareRequest`, not the record's) and the carrier of a registered engine instead of alpha.1's `InferenceConfig::operator==` (two handlers with different resolved windows never share; budget, blocking ratio and processing spec no longer keep equal models apart), and releases a pool entry by pointer identity when the plan table of the last registered session lets go. The engine room is three classes, the C lifecycle's levels: `Loaded` (one loaded model, the C `loaded` pointer: `init(const anira_init_info&)`, the engine's init once per object with the facts of the core, which the core calls right before the first `load`; `load(const Model&)`, once, under the lifecycle lock, which keeps a copy of the record, loads the model (`do_load`) and sizes the shared call slots to the record's `instances`; `prepare(const PrepareRequest&)`, once per session that runs the model, whose result the session's plan slot holds; it holds the shared slots and, once instead of five times, the spin-and-claim loop alpha.1's processors each carried, `claim_and_run`: a shared call claims the first slot whose busy flag was clear and the claimed index travels in the context, `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` and no engine call on a model without a shared slot), `Prepared` (one session's handle over a loaded model, the C `prepared` pointer: `run(ctx, reset_first, stop)`, what the inference thread calls, copies the context, writes the loaded pointer into it and, for an exclusive session, flags the call `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` with `instance` 0, resets first when asked and runs it on what the session keeps; a shared session's call goes through the claim loop) and `Executor` (one call slot of a loaded model, the engine's executor, what one process call runs on and never two at once: `process`, `reset`, `warm_up`), with `ExecutorLoaded` / `ExecutorPrepared` as the shape of the five built-in engines: `do_load` loads the engine's shared object, makes one executor over it (the probe, whose view of the model the binding rule runs over) and hands it to `adopt`, which makes the executors of the shared slots with `make_executor` (the probe is slot 0) or keeps the probe as the spare the first exclusive session takes when the record has no shared slot, and warms every one of them up with the record's FIXED warm-up; an exclusive session's prepare takes the spare or one more `make_executor`, warmed up there, and its `Prepared` owns it. `InferenceThread::inference` builds an `anira_engine_ctx` per chunk over the struct's descriptors (`ThreadSafeStruct::m_input_tensors` / `m_output_tensors`), runs the plan slot's `Prepared::run` with it, telling it to reset first at the first inference of a new stream of an exclusive session (the chunk's dispatch stamp against the plan slot's engine generation, adopted there), and treats an engine status other than `ANIRA_OK` as a failed chunk: zeros at its stream position, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` latched with one log line naming the status, and no `after_inference` (the rule of a failed hook; a 2.x `PrePostProcessor::after_inference` override, run through the compat shim, no longer runs behind a failed engine). The five built-in engines are adapters of the descriptor shape (above), and a registered engine runs behind `DescriptorAdapter`. The processor context's tensor accessors hand out a copy of the chunk's descriptor instead of rebuilding one per question, and `ProcessorRunner::bind` reads the engine and the provider of a plan from the session's table.
- **The core:** Session-exclusive is two halves. The scheduling half stays in the scheduler: one chunk of the session in flight at a time and in order under the dispatch gate, with a reset at every new stream, for a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair. The ownership half moved from the model to the handler: the exclusive handler's prepared handle owns its executor (the built-in engines' `ExecutorPrepared` takes the loaded model's spare or makes one at prepare, a registered engine builds its own under `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`), so a stateful model is pooled and loaded once like any other, with `instances` 0, and prepared per handler, where alpha.1 prepared it once per handler with `instances = 1` and never shared it; exclusivity left the pool key (`engine::Model` does not carry it, `engine::PrepareRequest` does) and `Core::create_session` does not bypass the pool for it. A built-in plan's prepare that throws inside `Core::create_session` (an exclusive session's own executor, its warm-up) leaves no pool entry behind: the slot is placed in the session's plan table before its prepare runs, so the rollback finds it. `AbiEngine.EqualConfigsShareOneLoadedModelStatefulOrNot`, `AbiEngine.ASessionExclusiveHandlerHasNoSharedSlotOnAnyEngine` and `Adapter.AnExclusiveSessionRunsOnItsOwnExecutorAndResetsWhenAsked` pin it.
- **The core:** Every input and output ring of the pipeline is an instantiation of `RingBufferT<T>` with `T` the element type of the slot's ring dtype, and the ring dtype is real: nothing in anira converts. `anira_ring` (`src/utils/RingBuffer.h`, private; the type `anira::RingBuffer` names) holds one instantiation per scalar dtype the ABI pins (float32, float64, float16, bfloat16, int8, uint8, bool8, int16, int32, int64, with `T` the dtype's C type and the three that have none stored as their bits; no two dtypes share a ring; float32 unless a slot says otherwise), chosen at prepare: `SessionElement::prepare` and `Core::prepare_session` take the per-slot `RingDtypes` and the caller's `CustomLatencies` (alpha.1's `std::vector<long>`, named), which the 2.x `InferenceManager::prepare` filled from its arguments until the 2.x classes left. The ring's block API (`push_block`, `pop_block`, `peek_past_block`, `push_fill`, `push_zeros`, `discard`, `pop_windows`, which holds the batched sliding-window pop of alpha.1's `PrePostProcessor::pop_samples_from_buffer`) takes the caller's dtype, returns 0 and writes nothing on a disagreement, and never converts. The typed Hard entries of `anira/abi/handler.h` are what reaches these rings across the ABI. The registry doc of `anira_contract_hard_set_ring_dtype` (`anira/abi/config.h`) and `anira::ContractHandle::hard_ring_dtype` state the no-conversion rule: the ring dtype is the element type the typed Hard entries carry and the ring holds as is; alpha.1's "data only in this pre-release" sentence and its pre- and post-processor conversion on the inference thread are gone. A ring dtype that differs from its spec's dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_prepare` unless the pipeline's processor fills the phase that moves that ring (a name that is not a Streamed tensor likewise).
- **The core:** The empty candidate set of a pipeline is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, not `NOT_SUPPORTED`: zero plans is a configuration the caller can fix, not a limit of the build (`none of the N model entries runs on a candidate backend (candidates: ...)`). The candidate filter of the extension walk takes `anira_backend_id` rows.
- **The core:** Real-time failures are latched (`src/utils/RtLatch.h`, private): a refused `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` or `_wait` entry records its status in `anira_handler_rt_error` and logs one Error record per kind through the real-time queue, flagged `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_CONTRACT_VIOLATION` (`"<entry>: <status text>"`), later occurrences count; prepare and reset re-arm and log the count. The operational real-time sites of the scheduler (`RtSite`, named in `k_rt_site_names`: missing samples, an output nobody pops, a dropped inference; the fourteen sites of the scheduler and the five engines when the latch landed, the engines' five no-model sites since gone with the 2.x engine path) and a failed inference log their first occurrence since the last prepare and count the rest; the drain (the `anira-log` thread, or `anira_drain_log` under `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_MANUAL` and on WebAssembly) reports a condition whose count grew at most every 10 seconds ("still failing, N more suppressed") without re-arming it. A failed inference — an engine exception or status, or a custom processor or a `before_inference` / `after_inference` hook that throws — zero-fills its output, sets `rt_error` to `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` and emits one latched record; every inference-thread body runs under a catch, so no exception ends a pool or user thread through tanh-lib's top-level handler, and a throwing instance is never left busy: the engine room's one claim loop releases the busy flag on every exit path (`src/engines/ProcessingGuard.h`), the RAII busy-flag guard alpha.1's LibTorch processor alone had.
- **The core:** The core owns the real-time log drain thread: a low-priority `thl::core::Thread` named `anira-log` of anira's own (`LogDrainLoop`, `src/scheduler/LogDrainLoop.h`) runs `Queue::drain()` every drain interval while a session or a context exists (tanh-lib's `DrainThread` is no longer constructed), and the last user's release, `anira_context_destroy` or `anira_shutdown` stops it and flushes the queue through the sinks on the calling thread. `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_TRACE_FAILURES` is admitted by `anira_context_config_set_log_flags` and the `flags` of `anira_log_desc`.
- **Web (module, build):** the module links `-pthread -sWASM_WORKERS -sAUDIO_WORKLET` (Emscripten's hybrid mode; `cmake/build-wasm.cmake`), names `-sINCOMING_MODULE_JS_API=wasmMemory,wasmBinary,locateFile,instantiateWasm,print,printErr`, builds the shipped Release module with `-sASSERTIONS=0` (`wasm-debug` keeps 1: emsdk's Web Audio library asserts `instanceof window.AudioContext`, which an `OfflineAudioContext` fails; alpha.1's module kept `ASSERTIONS=1` in every build) and exports `emscriptenRegisterAudioObject`; the environment is `web,worker,worklet`. Every C and C++ object of the Wasm build, the fetched dependencies' included, compiles with `-pthread` (`CMakeLists.txt`, in place of `-matomics -mbulk-memory`), as do the node gates of `test/abi`. The emsdk stays at 4.0.23, the shared tanh-lab pin: the prebuilt ONNX Runtime WASM archive is LTO bitcode of that toolchain, and on emsdk 6.0.13 (libc++ 22) every model load of the built-in engine fails (`ONNX Schema NhwcMaxPool: failed validating the check`); the move waits for a matching archive (anira-project/backends#24). The built-in ONNX Runtime engine creates no thread on WebAssembly: its global thread pools are one thread each, the calling one, inter-op included (a pthread started from the browser's main thread runs only once that thread yields, so the join at the environment's release never returned). The runtime never exits: a `-pthread` link of emsdk 4.0.23 without the `noExitRuntime` symbol defines `keepRuntimeAlive()` as `runtimeKeepaliveCounter > 0` (`src/lib/libcore.js`: pthreads must be able to exit), although `EXIT_RUNTIME` is 0, so the first Emscripten callback into C that ran with no keepalive held (an `emscripten_set_timeout` callback) ended in `maybeExit` → `exit` → `proc_exit`, which terminated the pthreads and set `ABORT`, and every later callback into C (a timer, an audio worklet's creation) was dropped without a message. The library itself depends on the `noExitRuntime` symbol (`src/platform/emscripten/em_runtime.cpp`, `EM_JS_DEPS`, pulled into every WebAssembly link of anira by the `INTERFACE` link option `-Wl,--undefined=anira_em_runtime_anchor`), so `keepRuntimeAlive()` is `noExitRuntime || ...` with `noExitRuntime = !EXIT_RUNTIME`, in every module that links anira and in every instance of its glue (the page, the Workers, the worklet); AniraWeb links `-sEXIT_RUNTIME=0`. An app that sets `EXIT_RUNTIME=1` owns its runtime's lifetime. The node gate `anira_em_runtime` (a `--no-entry` consumer with none of AniraWeb's flags; a program with `main()` includes `noExitRuntime` on its own, and so does Emscripten's default incoming Module API) runs a second `emscripten_set_timeout` callback after a first one; without the anchor it fails. The Worker reaper runs on `emscripten_set_timeout`; the browser test's `boot-stop.html` creates four audio nodes and renders through the last one after the reaper's timers fired; without the flag it fails (checked: OOM, the reaper stopped after its first timer).
- **Web (module):** the module's memory grows. AniraWeb links `-sALLOW_MEMORY_GROWTH=1 -sINITIAL_MEMORY=268435456 -sMAXIMUM_MEMORY=2147483648` (it was a fixed 512 MiB, alpha.1's 8192 pages), and `Anira.create` makes a shared memory of 256 MiB growing to 2 GiB; `Anira.create({ memory: { initial, maximum } })` sizes it in bytes, `initial === maximum` fixes it (the opt-out). Sizes the module cannot run on (below 256 MiB, a maximum below the initial size or above 2 GiB) are refused with an `AniraError` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`), and a memory the browser cannot reserve with one (`ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`) naming the sizes and the way to a smaller maximum. A shared memory's maximum is reserved as address space at creation and committed as it is touched: free on 64-bit browsers (V8 reserves 8 GiB per wasm32 memory whatever the maximum, SpiderMonkey 4 GiB plus a guard), and capped at 2 GiB for 32-bit and constrained devices; Chromium 156 and Firefox 157 accept up to 65536 pages (4 GiB). The memory never shrinks. A growth gives the memory a new buffer, so every heap access of the package goes through one helper, `heap(module)` of the generated bindings (exported from the scope entry for processors and engines), which rebuilds the realm's views once after a growth; the processor and engine trampolines call it before every call, and anira's boot gives each inference Worker's and the worklet's `Module` its `wasmMemory`, which AniraWeb lists in `EXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS`: unexported, it is a getter-only property of `Module` under `ASSERTIONS`, where in a Debug build (`wasm-debug`) the boot's assignment throws in strict mode (`TypeError: setting getter-only property "wasmMemory"`), so no inference Worker would enter its loop and the worklet's registry would never boot (the ABI gates' glue is a sloppy-mode script, where the assignment fails silently). The C audio path still allocates nothing, so the audio thread never grows the memory; a JavaScript processor's realm rebuilds its views at its first call after a growth (a small JavaScript allocation). The cost of the glue's own checks with `-pthread` (Emscripten's `pthreads-mem-growth`), headless, medians of 5, growing against fixed: the worklet's work per quantum (60 s offline, not waiting) 59.5 against 56.3 ms in Chromium 156 (+6 %) and 48.9 against 46.7 ms in Firefox 157 (+5 %); a render waiting for a JavaScript engine on two Workers 468 against 469 ms; a binding call 127-132 ns in both. `growth.html` grows the memory from 256 to 531 MiB through 56 handlers and renders exact through the Workers and the worklet with a node from before the growth and a handler from after it, grows and re-prepares a node mid-stream (offline: suspended at the half in Chromium, from a timer in Firefox; live in Chromium) with no wrong sample, and checks the refused sizes; `heap.test.ts` checks the rebuild; `oom.html` creates its module with a fixed 512 MiB, which fills in moments, and `anira_em_oom` keeps its own fixed memory. The defaults stay AUTO: an AUTO pool of 16 Workers with two RAVE models (about 530 MiB of instances beside the core's 169 MiB) did not fit the fixed 512 MiB (#218) and fits now.
- **Web (module):** `num_threads` and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` are no longer coerced to 0 on WebAssembly (alpha.1's `default_num_threads()` returned 0 there, and its 2.x `JsonConfigLoader` coerced a file's `num_threads` to 0): the pool's threads are Wasm Workers, so a context config that leaves the threads at AUTO gets a pool of Workers on the web, and a host that drives inference itself sets `num_threads` 0. `ANIRA_WAIT_BLOCKING` still becomes `ANIRA_WAIT_SPIN_BACKOFF` (an idle Worker parks on the wake word) and `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_THREAD` `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_MANUAL`.
- **Web (docs):** the Anira Web guides describe the typed layer: `index` (the threads anira starts, configs once, the worker module as the one web-only context field), `installation_building` (the three entry points, what a page needs: cross-origin isolation, self-contained worker and worklet modules, `optimizeDeps.exclude`, the dev server; the example's `vite.config.ts` included), `basic_usage` (a bundled config on the built-in ONNX Runtime engine through the audio node, step by step from `web/examples/model-file/main.ts`, raw calls through `anira.abi`; the processor-and-engine example's `main.ts` included), `architecture` (the main thread, the pool of Wasm Workers, the audio thread, the scope registry, where each phase runs, the bindings), `audio_nodes` (in place of alpha.1's `custom_audio_worklets`: the C render callback, its options, the worklet scope), `custom_pre_post_processing` (`defineProcessor`, `addProcessor`; the example's `gain-processor.ts`), `custom_inference_backends` (the worker module, `defineEngine`, `addEngine`, ONNX Runtime Web; the example's `double-engine.ts`); every included file is type-checked by `npm run examples` and run by the browser test. `audio_nodes.rst` gains "A Model with Several Streams", notes that a device buffer need not be a multiple of the quantum, and notes resampled contexts: a context of 44.1 kHz on a 48 kHz device renders 2048 × 44100 / 48000 = 1881.6 context frames per callback (runs of 14 and 15 quanta of 128, a burst of 1920), which the probe measures (it times the callbacks and counts at the context's rate) and Chromium's `baseLatency × sampleRate` (2048) overstates. The API reference lists the new classes and functions. The migration page ("Anira Web: from the 2.x TypeScript classes") maps alpha.1's 2.x classes and the pre-release runtime to the typed layer, the single-slot options to `inputSlots` / `outputSlots` among them; `web/README.md` and the README follow. `Fetcher` names the fetch the loaders take (sphinx-js cannot cross-reference `typeof fetch`).
- **Web (docs):** what an inference Worker and the worklet can see (`web-api/architecture.rst`, linked from the JavaScript engines page): every Worker and the AudioWorkletGlobalScope is its own realm, only the WebAssembly memory is shared, so processors and engines are defined in the realm that runs them and a pool of N threads builds N engine instances; once the engines' `init` resolved, a Worker's C loop blocks (`memory.atomic.wait32`) and its event loop never runs again until the stop, so `process` is synchronous and `init` may be async; the channels after init are the Static inputs, a processor's `prepared` pointer and an app block in the heap with `Atomics`, never a `postMessage`; the worklet realm has no `fetch` and no `performance`. All of it unchanged since 2.x. Asynchronous engines (onnxruntime-web `session.run()`, a WebGPU `mapAsync` readback, tfjs `data()`) are not supported; they are recorded for M3 in `docs/anira-v3-architecture.md` (a Worker that leaves its blocking loop with `Atomics.waitAsync`, completing the job's ticket later). The `anira/anira_em.h` header doc and the guide say a js_key is the 32-bit FNV-1a hash of the name and a registry refuses a second name with a taken key (`npm test` forces a collision pair). The Doxygen of the thread default and the WebAssembly coercions (the thread default is half the cores on WebAssembly too, the true reasons `Blocking` and the drain thread stay coerced there: the queue has no semaphore and an idle Wasm Worker parks on the wake word, the JavaScript log sink runs on the main thread), of `InferenceThread`, `InferenceQueue`, `set_non_realtime` and `v2::ContextConfig`, the usage guide and the coercion warnings no longer describe the pre-Worker runtime.
- **Build (internal):** anira is compiled once, into the OBJECT library `anira_objects`, which carries every source, the symbol policy and every build-time requirement (anira's include directories, the engines, the `USE_*` switches, tanh-lib, the queue, the JSON library, the sanitizer flags); `libanira`, shared or static, is linked from those objects alone. The test binaries are white-box or black-box (`anira_add_test_binary(<target> [WHITE_BOX] ...)`): `test_utils`, `test_scheduler`, `test_engines` and `test_abi` read anira's private classes, so they link the objects with `src/` on their include path and `ANIRA_STATIC` defined, while `test_handler`, the ABI gates, the unload harness, the install consumers and the examples link `anira::anira` and see what a consumer sees. No consumer-visible change; it is what lets the C++ classes leave the library's export table. `docs/sphinx/contributing.rst` states the rule.
- **Build (WebAssembly):** AniraWeb links `anira::anira` alone: no wrapper archive (alpha.1 linked the 2.x wrappers of `src/emscripten-wrappers/`), no `--whole-archive`, no `-sEXPORT_KEEPALIVE`; the export list pulls every archive member it names. `build_web` compares the module's `.d.ts` with `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt` and `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` (plus `_malloc`, `_free`) in both directions: a missing name and an extra export both fail. `codecov.yml` ignores `src/platform` in place of the wrappers, and `clang_tidy.yml`'s comment names only `src/platform/`.
- **Build:** tanh-lib is pinned at v0.5.0 (`a62f61649edcb6666de041830d1ef97c7f02e17f`, tanh-lab/tanh-lib#84: `tanh_add_core_library` and the inline ABI namespace) in `cmake/tanh-lib-pin.cmake`, the one source of the commit for anira and its install consumers, where alpha.1 pinned v0.3.0, and tanh-tooling moves from v0.2.8 to v0.3.0 on both sides (anira's `.clangd`, `cmake/tanh/` and `lint.yml`; anira and the pinned tanh-lib pin the same tag). v0.4.0 brings the strided ring calls (tanh-lab/tanh-lib#50: `thl::core::RingBuffer<T>::push_block`, `pop_block` and `peek_past_block` take a `stride`, counted in elements, so one channel of an interleaved block moves between the host's memory and a ring in one copy, without a de-interleaved block in between), which anira's ring forwards (below).
- **Build:** every configure preset exports the compile database, and clangd follows the tree configured last. `CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS` is `ON` on the preset roots (`desktop-base`, `ci-base`, `wasm-debug`, `wasm-release`, `docs`), so all 34 visible configure presets inherit it and the `clang-tidy` preset drops its own copy. Only `clang-tidy` set it before; a desktop or ci tree's `compile_commands.json` held nothing but the fetched tanh-lib's five entries (tanh-lib sets the variable in its own directory scope), so clangd and a local clang-tidy saw none of anira's sources. A fresh `desktop-tests-debug` or `ci-tests-static` tree now lists 159 entries (37 under `src/`, 83 under `test/`). The Visual Studio generator ignores the variable. `.clangd` reads `build/compile_commands.json` instead of alpha.1's `build/desktop/Debug/`, and the new option `ANIRA_LINK_COMPILE_COMMANDS` (default OFF, top-level builds only) links it to the configuring tree's database (`tanh_link_compile_commands`, `cmake/tanh/compile-commands.cmake`, tanh-tooling v0.3.0). Every `desktop-*` preset and `wasm-debug` / `wasm-release` turn it on; `clang-tidy` turns it off, and the `docs`, `ci-*`, sanitizer and mobile presets never set it, so a tidy sweep or a sanitizer run leaves the link where it was. Configuring `desktop-tests-debug` shows clangd the tests, `wasm-debug` the Emscripten branches. Reconfigure a development preset once to create the link; for the wasm trees, start clangd with `--query-driver=**/em++` (`docs/sphinx/contributing.rst`). `.vscode/settings.json` is tracked (the folder stays ignored otherwise): it enables `cmake.ctest.allowParallelJobs`, without which the CMake Tools Test Explorer runs one `ctest` process per test, sequentially, ignoring any job count; since anira registers one CTest entry per gtest case, a full local run took 126 s that way against 16 s for a single parallel `ctest` on a 32-core machine.
- **Build/CI:** no job count is pinned anywhere. Every test preset of `CMakePresets.json` lost its `execution.jobs: 4`, and the shared `cmake-test` action resolves the job count from the runner instead (its `CTEST_JOBS` input, default `auto`); the shared `cmake-build` action defaults `CMAKE_BUILD_PARALLEL_LEVEL` to `auto` (both ci-actions `v0.3.15`), and the six `--parallel 4` call sites in the workflows and in `.github/scripts/build_ios_xcframework.sh` (the Android install legs, the four iOS builds and the install-consumer build) detect the core count instead. A job count in a preset is a property of whichever machine happens to run it: it capped a 32-core workstation running `desktop-tests-rtsan` / `-asan` / `-tsan` by hand, and the fixed 4 oversubscribed the 3-vCPU `macos-latest` runners by a third. CI parallelism is unchanged on the 4-vCPU Linux and Windows runners and matches the hardware on macOS. Locally, `ctest --preset ...` follows `CTEST_PARALLEL_LEVEL` or an explicit `-j`; ctest ignores that variable whenever any job count reaches the command line, which is why the presets had to give the number up for it to work. One deliberate exception: the benchmark leg keeps `-j 1` in its `CTEST_ARGS`, since running benchmarks concurrently would corrupt their timings, and the action appends `CTEST_ARGS` last precisely so an explicit override still wins. The `timeout` values are unchanged.
- **Unload (ELF and Mach-O):** a library unloaded while an inference is still running aborts with a message instead of hanging. The library-unload hook ran `Core::shutdown()`, which joins the pool, from inside `dlclose`, where the unloading thread holds the loader lock (glibc's `dl_load_lock`) for the whole close. A pool thread that needed that lock mid-inference could never finish, so the join hung the host for good: LibTorch's first inference on a thread registers its `thread_local` destructors through `__cxa_thread_atexit`, which takes the lock, and a runtime's lazy `dlopen` or a custom engine can do the same. Returning without the thread would unmap the code under it. The hook now tells every pool thread to stop (`InferenceThread::request_stop`), waits up to two seconds for every pool thread to leave its loop and for the inferences in flight (`InferenceThread::get_num_in_flight()`, a process-wide atomic every thread that runs jobs keeps from the dequeue of a job to its end; idle threads count nothing), and joins as before once both hold. Waiting for the loops matters because a thread checks its stop flag and then dequeues: one that passed the check just before the stop takes one more job, counted only from its dequeue, possibly after the hook's first look (a queue run on a 4-vCPU runner hung that way in `ABuiltInInferenceInFlightAtUnloadNeverHangs`). When a thread or an inference is still running at the bound it writes `anira: the library is being unloaded while an inference is still running (N in flight, M inference threads not stopped); destroy every anira handler, or call anira_shutdown(), before unloading. Aborting instead of hanging.` to stderr with `write(2)` (not through anira's log queue or sinks, which may be gone or be what is stuck) and calls `std::abort()`. A correct host (every handler destroyed before the unload) sees no change: the pool is gone before the hook runs. At process exit the hook runs as well (`_dl_fini`), but no loader lock is held there, so it keeps waiting without a bound: an exit handler registered when the core is first put to work tells the hook that the process is exiting (a core first put to work before `main` is not told). Exit behaviour, established with the tests below against the old hook: a program that exits with an inference in flight exited normally before (the join waits, a 3 s inference included; LibTorch mid-first-inference exited 0 in 20 of 20 runs) and still does; with the bounded wait applied at exit too the 3 s case would abort, which the exit handler prevents. Windows is unchanged: the CRT runs the static destructor from `DllMain` under the loader lock, where nothing may be waited for, and it also runs at exit after `ExitProcess` has ended every other thread, so an in-flight count would be stale there. macOS: the order of an image's exit handlers and its terminators at `dlclose` under dyld is not verified; if dyld runs the handlers first, the hook takes the unload for an exit and joins without a bound, the earlier behaviour. The hook's comment no longer claims that pool threads never take the loader lock. Tests (`test/contracts/unload`): `LibraryUnloadDeathTest.AnInferenceStillRunningAtUnloadAborts` (a module engine that never returns), `AnInferenceWaitingForTheLoaderLockAtUnloadAborts` (glibc: a module engine that takes `dl_load_lock` through `dlopen`, the investigation's hang made deterministic), `ABuiltInInferenceInFlightAtUnloadNeverHangs` (the investigation's case: the built-in engine with blocks in flight either unloads cleanly or aborts with the message), `ExitWithASlowInferenceInFlightExitsNormally` and `ExitWithABuiltInInferenceInFlightExitsNormally`, and `LibraryUnload.AnInferenceThatFinishesWithinTheWaitUnloadsCleanly` (a 300 ms inference in flight: the hook waits, joins and the library unmaps). Against the old hook the three unload death tests hang until their watchdog; skipped on Windows and, for the unload cases, on Apple. The module gains `unloadtest_create_engine` and `unloadtest_engine_entered`.
- **Internal:** alpha.1's `src/capi/translate.{h,cpp}` is split by what it does. `src/capi/validate.{h,cpp}` (the history follows it) holds the section-2 validator (`validate`, the rules, `Derived`), the plan table (`PlanKey`, `matching_plans`, the default-set rule), what the pipeline's processor and engines mean to it (`ProcessorFacts`, `EngineFacts`) and the per-slot facts it resolves (`ring_dtypes_of`, `host_domains_of`, `state_links`). The translators into the 2.x configuration objects (`make_inference_config`, `make_host_config`, `make_context_config`) moved beside the exported bridge, into `src/capi/v3_to_v2.cpp`, and went with the 2.x runtime at the cut-over; `test_Translate` is `test_Validate` (suite `AbiValidate`: what the validator derives, which the session reads). No behavior change.
