- **Internal:** the session's ring (`anira::RingBuffer`, the C tag `anira_ring`, a private header since the core's headers went private) is typed only. The float face of alpha.1's installed `anira/utils/RingBuffer.h` (an alias of tanh-lib's float ring) is gone: `push_sample`, `pop_sample`, the three-argument float block calls, `get_future_sample`, `get_past_sample`, the 2.x getter names (`get_available_samples`, `get_available_past_samples`, `get_num_channels`, `get_num_samples`) and the two-argument `initialize_with_positions`. Every call names the dtype of the caller's memory (the typed `push_block` / `pop_block` / `peek_past_block` / `push_fill` and `available` / `available_past` / `num_channels` / `capacity`), as the C accessors of `abi/processor.h` and `anira::RingView` do; the compat shim's `anira::v2::RingBuffer` view keeps its 2.x names over the C accessors. The dtype-checked `push_block`, `pop_block` and `peek_past_block` forward tanh-lib's `stride` with `size_t stride = 1` as their last parameter (1 or more; 1 is the packed run and the unit-stride path of the storage; a disagreeing dtype still returns 0 with nothing written; a push larger than the capacity keeps its tail and a pop beyond the available count value-initialises the strided tail and nothing between), the host↔ring copy path passes each channel's run with its step, `element_size()` reports `sizeof(T)` of the instantiation behind the ring's dtype, and `pop_windows` is unchanged. The four ring tests whose subject was the face (alpha.1's `SingleChannelPushPop` and `DimensionsAndTypedVariant`, with `TheFloatFaceIsTheFloat32Arm` and `TheFloatFaceKeepsItsThreeArguments`) go with it, their storage semantics being tanh-lib's own suite's.
- **CI:** every workflow moves from `tanh-lab/ci-actions` v0.3.10 to `v0.3.16` (the `build_test` gate allows one version repo-wide). The range brings the tidy inputs (v0.3.13, below), the libcurl headers in the Linux tool setup and a coverage export that tolerates one invalid `.profraw` and reports it (v0.3.14: the `static-executorch` coverage leg had lost a race on the 248-byte profile of `anira_abi_layout` three runs out of five), the job-count inputs of `cmake-test` and `cmake-build` (`v0.3.15`, above), and the `runs_on` input of the reusable workflows (tanh-lab/ci-actions#15, below).
- **CI:** every Linux job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` instead of `ubuntu-latest` (the six Linux legs of `.github/build_test_matrix.json`, the benchmark, examples and install matrices, and the workflows' own jobs). GitHub moved `ubuntu-latest` to Ubuntu 26.04 (resolute), for which apt.llvm.org builds LLVM 21 and later but no 20, so the LLVM 20 that the tooling is pinned to could not be installed and every Linux leg failed in `setup-cpp-build-tools` (`apt-get update`: `llvm-toolchain-resolute-20` has no Release file, exit 100). The tidy job was already on `ubuntu-24.04`, and so are ci-actions' sanitizer legs; the coverage legs run inside ci-actions' reusable workflow, whose runner was hard-coded to `ubuntu-latest`, hence ci-actions `v0.3.16` (tanh-lab/ci-actions#15: a `runs_on` input defaulting to `ubuntu-24.04`, and `setup-cpp-build-tools` failing with a message that names the missing LLVM release). Moving to LLVM 21 instead would change the clang-format and clang-tidy output against the tanh-tooling pin.
- **CI:** a pull request's `clang_tidy` run checks only the `.cpp` files the pull request changes (`CHANGED_FILES_ONLY` of `tanh-lab/ci-actions`' `clang-tidy-check`, which reads the list from git, no token), so the job is the three-minute configure and build plus those files, instead of the 20-minute sweep that any header change used to force. The merge queue and a manual run (`workflow_dispatch`) still sweep every source, and so does a pull request that changes `.clang-tidy` or the workflow itself (the two files that change tidy's own verdicts). A header or build-file change no longer widens the set: what it breaks in an untouched file fails in the merge queue, not on the pull request. `clang_tidy result` reports on every pull request, so it can be a required check and the merge queue's full sweep gates a merge (as a path-filtered, unrequired check its failure in the queue did not block one): the workflow's `paths:` filter is gone, and a pull request that changes no `.cpp` file and neither `.clang-tidy` nor the workflow skips the fixture restore, the tool setup and the build, so its job is a checkout and one script (`tanh-lab/ci-actions/changed-files` run first, with the same sweep pattern the tidy action uses).
- **ABI gates:** gate 4 is strict and runs on every preset. `anira_header_isolation` (now `test/abi/header_isolation.cpp`, beside the other header gates; alpha.1's `test/contracts/header_isolation.cpp` compiled the 2.x umbrella with include directories added by hand, on desktop only) links `anira::anira` and nothing else, so it compiles every public header (the C umbrellas, `anira.hpp`, `compat/v2.hpp`) with exactly the include directories and definitions a consumer gets, under the strict flags, and its probes fail the compile with a named `#error` the moment anything private resolves through them: anira's own `src/` headers, an engine header, tanh-lib, the queue, the JSON library, gtest or benchmark (checked by adding each include directory by hand: the matching probe fires). A build-driving CTest on desktop as before, and now a member of ALL on Android, iOS and in the WebAssembly arm of `test/abi`; the include and define lists it spelled by hand are gone. Gate 4 compiles `<anira/anira.h>`, now the C umbrella, as C11 and C++17, and its per-file gates take the list of `cmake/public-headers.cmake`. The header gates follow the install set: alpha.1's `anira_header_coexist` (the 2.x umbrella beside the C headers) and `anira_header_cxx20_with_v2` (the 2.x umbrella beside `anira.hpp`) are gone with their subject; `anira_header_compat_with_hpp` compiles the C umbrella, `anira.hpp` and the shim in one TU, both orders, under the strict consumer flags; `anira_header_scheduler_coexist` compiles the library's scheduler classes beside `anira.hpp`, the internal check that no class of namespace anira is both the library's and `anira.hpp`'s.
- **ABI gates:** gate 5's alpha.1 consumers run on `anira.hpp`: `consumer` is a handler round trip over a custom engine, `consumer_engine` a model config beside `onnxruntime_cxx_api.h`.
- **ABI tooling:** `tools/abi/gen.py` models what the tensor records need: a `union` entity and a named union member of a Tier-1 struct, their arms being leaves or unnamed struct types with a member name (never a truly anonymous struct, which ISO C++ does not have); one layout tree behind `abi/layout-<major>.txt`, `test/abi/generated/test_layout.c` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts`, with one row per member and, inside an unnamed struct or union, one per member of it, written as the C designator (`field anira_sync_token.u.vk.value offset 16 size 8`; a union block starts with `union` instead of `struct`); `function_type: true` on a callback (`typedef R (ANIRA_CALL name)(...)`, so `ANIRA_PTR(name, slot)` is a function pointer) with `forward: true` on the struct it names (the `typedef struct X X;` is emitted ahead of the first entity that names `X`); a header-level `draft: true`, which belongs to a file under `draft/` holding `status: draft` functions only (guard `ANIRA_ABI_DRAFT_*`, names in `abi/symbols-draft.txt`); `struct_id:` linking a record to its `anira_struct_id` constant, required on every Tier-1 record, from which `src/capi/generated/struct_sizes.inc` (the table behind `anira_sizeof`) is generated; and the refusal of a member name repeated within one C scope, an `ANIRA_PTR` slot taking both `name` and `name_bits` (`--check` exits 1: `config.h:anira_vulkan_desc: duplicate field device in anira_vulkan_desc`, the run recorded in the pull request). `--diff-against` classifies a changed union arm (an appended one included) as a Tier-1 layout change, a callback turned function type as a signature change and a removed `callback_safe` as a break, and admits whatever happens to a `status: draft` function (changed, removed, promoted) as outside the promise. `padding_free: true` on a Tier-2 struct asserts in `test_layout.c` that `sizeof` equals the sum of the member sizes on every target; an array extent below 1, a function type as a parameter and a malformed member list are registry errors, and `layout.ts` refuses a generated line over 90 columns. `--check` covers 67 generated files at this tag (58 when the tensor records landed).
- **ABI tooling:** `cmake/abi-layout-check.cmake` reads the ABI major from the name of the table it is given and says which rule was broken: while the major is 0 the table grows with the registry (run `gen.py --write`, commit `abi/layout-0.txt` with the headers; if `gen.py --check` is clean the compiler disagrees with the generator's model), from the freeze on a Tier-1 layout changes only with `ANIRA_ABI_MAJOR`.
- **Tests:** the suite is partitioned for the 3.x runtime, written as a comment above the test binaries in `test/CMakeLists.txt` (decision 22 of the M2 plan: every test file targets one API and moves once) and named in `docs/sphinx/contributing.rst`: (a) 2.x hosts through `anira/compat/v2.hpp`, (b) rewritten on `anira/anira.hpp`, (c) white-box over the private headers (`WHITE_BOX`, above), (d) retired with their subject, (e) `test_EngineLinkage`, the configure-time linkage rule at run time over `anira_enabled_engines` (API-free when the partition was first written). No suite drives a 2.x session any more.
  - (a) `test_InferenceHandlerApi.cpp` and `test_OneSidedStreaming.cpp` include the shim alone, spell `using namespace anira::v2` and `ContextConfig(2)`, and run over the C handler. The one-sided mocks are `anira::Engine` objects under `anira.v2.custom` through one `KernelEngine<Kernel>` template (ParamFill, MeanPlusParam, a counting copy); the two `#ifndef ANIRA_WITH_RTSAN` regions are gone, so the non-realtime and blocking generator cases run under RTSan; an out-of-range custom-latency index expects `anira::Error` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`); the custom-processor constructor takes a `PassthroughEngine`. `GeneratorPushDataNeverSubmits` and `AnalyserPushOnlyNeverStalls` stay in suite `OneSidedStreamingStandalone` with what the handler shows (the calls, the ring fill, one hop of demand submitting once, the entry count through `anira_handler_num_entries(handler.native())`); their struct-pool halves and `TwoSidedPushWithoutPopIsGatedNotOverwritten`, which read the session through `Core::get_sessions()`, moved verbatim into `test/scheduler/test_OneSidedStreamingInternals.cpp` (suite `OneSidedStreamingInternals`, in `test_scheduler`) with the helpers they need. `test_Compat` holds the extras' CNN and HybridNN processors copied verbatim against `anira::v2` (bit-equal to the 2.x class on every oracle engine; HybridNN reads the layout-applying shape getter), the single form that carries one slot per side, and the generator's clamped Static count.
  - (b) `test_InferenceHandler` runs `anira::InferenceHandler` with `HybridNNProcessor` and the bypass engine. `test_anira_hpp` covers the handler half (every contract form of `prepare`, the Hard forms and their `_wait` twins over a stereo stream, the Static pair, `set_plan` out of range, `InferenceThread` on a context without pool threads, `log_rt`).
  - (d) Retired with the bridge and the 2.x classes (the Removed items): among them `test_InferenceConfig.cpp`, `test_CoreConfig.cpp` (alpha.1's `test_ContextConfig.cpp`) and `test_JsonConfigLoader`; alpha.1's `test/support/extras_fixtures.h`, which served `test_InferenceHandler`, `test_JsonConfigLoader` and `test_ExtrasFixtures` the 2.x configurations, is gone with them.
- **Tests: every suite checks the 3.x side against an independent oracle.**
  - **The copy-path recordings run on C handlers' own sessions** (`anira_test::oracle::HandlerSession`, `test/support/copy_oracle.h`). `CopyPathOracle` and `TensorStems` drive the tensor stems of a C handler's `InferenceManager` directly, below the C entries and their block rule, over the C twins of the 2.x configurations the transcripts were recorded on. `NonStreamableRouter` keeps the Static values in the handler's Static ports, and `PlanarFloatAdapter` is sized from the ports; the two helpers of the 2.x float face (`PlanarFloatAdapter.h`, `NonStreamableRouter.h`) left the library for `test/support/` and keep the C handler's store only. `AbiHandlerCopyOracle.MatchesTheManagerCopyPath` has the same stems of a C handler as its referee. Both goldens are unchanged.
  - **The scheduler suites run on C handlers.** `test/support/session_cases.h` describes a case by its slots (elements, channels, hop), the host blocks, the budget, the wait ratio and the instances. The sizing and latency tables of `SessionElement` and `InferenceManager` are written this way, and the lifecycle, failure, plan-switch, one-sided, pooling, ordering and user-thread suites run the same way; every expected figure is unchanged. `test_ContextConfigMismatch` keeps alpha.1's name. A few cases went with the 2.x paths they tested: the roundtrip fallback for a backend without a model; `set_backend` on a table without that backend, and the 2.x default plan table; the manager's clamp of a custom latency below the model's own (the C prepare refuses that contract; `RefusesAFigureBelowTheInternalModelLatency`, alpha.1's `ClampsToInternalModelLatency`, asserts the refusal and that the internal latency itself is accepted); the empty plan table (the validator refuses a pipeline with no plan).
  - **The logger, ExecuTorch entry-point and C ABI suites run on C handlers too.** `anira_test::Instance` (a context and a prepared handler over the mono pass-through) is shared by every suite that only needs a live session. `ExecuTorchModelFunction` names the method through the model entry's `entry` extension. `test_Context`, `test_Log`, `test_Firewall` and `test_Thread` get their live session from `anira_test::Instance` or a C handler, as do `test_InferenceThread`, `test_ProcessorPooling` and `AbiInferenceThread.ServesASessionWithoutAPool` (the two thread cases check that a user-driven thread runs one block). `test_ExtrasFixtures` compares what the validator derives for every bundled file (the shapes, the TensorFlow layout, the channels, hops and latencies, the budget, the warm-up, the state) with the 2.x fixture literals, with no 2.x `InferenceConfig` involved.
  - **Closed forms.** `anira_test::DelayLine` is the closed form of a sample-wise stream (the processed input behind the reported latency), and `anira_test::gain_peak` is the gain model's Static output. `test_Handler`'s oracle and `test_Compat`'s runtime half (the shim's handler alone: `Shim`, `KernelShim`) check every block against them, and the latencies against literals of the closed form. `test_Compat`'s configuration half checks every getter against its value computed from the constructor's arguments (`expect_getters_of`), and the loader against the values of the documents.
  - **The oracle of a neural model, which has no closed form:** `anira_test::EngineOracle` (`test/support/engine_oracle.h`) reloads the record of a C handler's plan on an executor of its own and runs the model directly in the engine room on windows built by hand (`receptive_window`, `batched_windows`). `AbiHandler.TheCnnStreamIsTheModelOnItsWindows` streams the steerable-nafx CNN through a C handler with no processor on every plan and expects the latency priming, then per block the CNN on its 15380-sample window (the CNN otherwise runs in the examples; `test_InferenceHandler` runs GuitarLSTM). `AbiCompat.CnnMatchesThroughTheUsersProcessor` and `HybridNnMatchesThroughTheUsersProcessor` run the extras' two 2.x processors once, as a 2.x user's program on the shim, against a C handler with the default fill and with the extras' `HybridNNProcessor`, and check the C handler's whole stream against the oracle as well as against the shim. `AbiHandlerTensor.AnInterleavedBlockMatchesAPlanarBlockOnEveryPlan` compares two C handlers of the stereo gain model, one fed interleaved and one planar, and checks both layouts against the closed form (the input delayed by the latency, gain 1). `AbiProcessor.NoProcessorRunsTheWindowFillEndToEnd` (a window-sum engine, hand-computed stream) and `PopWindowsLaysOutEveryWindowWithItsHistory` (a hand-computed layout with an offset and four batches) pin the default fill end to end and the window layout, beside `test_RingBuffer`'s cases of `pop_windows`; the default fill's unit cases (`DefaultPreProcessFillsTheWindowHeadWithHistory`, `DefaultPreProcessTreatsAMultichannelTensorAsChannels`) are the C twins of alpha.1's 2.x `HistoryFillsTheWindowHead` and `MultichannelTensorIsNotAWindow`. Each oracle fails on a window shifted by one sample.
  - **The pins the 2.x suites held come back on the 3.x core.** `CoreLifecycleTest.AutoThreadsAreHalfTheHardwareThreadsAtLeastOne`: `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` against the closed form over `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (`Core::default_num_threads`), the prospective pool size and the pool a first session builds (alpha.1's 2.x `ContextConfigValues.PlatformDefaults` held the formula). `ContextConfigMismatchTest.TheCoreClampsAQueueCapacityOutsideItsRange`: a configuration that reaches the core with a log-queue capacity of 10 or 100000 gets a queue of 64 or 65536 records and the warning naming both figures; 64 is not reported. One fresh core per figure (`release_core_if_idle`). `ExtrasFixtures.ThePrepareResolvesTheFilesInstanceCount`: the bundled files leave the instance count to anira, and a prepare on a pool of three threads resolves it to 1 on the stateful RNN (an exclusive session) and to 3 on the gain model.
- **Tests:** the tests of the new runtime that check what they claim, the suite names and the 2.x documents.
  - `LibraryUnloadDeathTest.ExitWaitsForTheInferenceBeforeTheRuntimesStaticsGo`: the inference waits for the exit (`unloadtest_hold_until_exit`: an exit handler registered after the prepare, so it runs first, releases it), and once released over the live runtime state it writes a marker the test expects on stderr (not a 300 ms sleep and an exit status of 0 alone).
  - `AbiHandler.SetPlanSwitchesTheRunningPlan` carries a gain of 0.5 through the multi form (the engines halve the stream, the custom row's pass-through copies it) and alternates between each engine's plan and the custom one, so every block's closed form says which plan ran it; without the switch it fails (on a Static gain of 0 the engines' plans answered silence).
  - `AbiEngine.TheQueryRunsAtCreateAndPrepareReusesItsAnswer` re-prepares two handlers on two threads, so one thread unloads the previous loaded model while the other prepares a new one, which the engine contract allows (`anira_engine_prepare_fn`: the prepares and unprepares of different loaded models may run at once). The gain engine of `test/abi/test_Engine.cpp` locks its bookkeeping: a mutex guards its shared counters and pointer lists in load, unload, prepare and unprepare (prepare's overlap watch stays outside it, so the check that anira serialises the prepares of one loaded model still sees an overlap). Unsynchronised, ThreadSanitizer reported the race 4 times in 20 runs at 90fefb7 (never an assertion); 0 reports in 40 runs on both trees with the lock.
  - The suites say engine: alpha.1's `ExecuTorchProcessor` / `LibTorchProcessor` are `AdapterExecuTorch` / `AdapterLibTorch`, `BackendLinkage` is `EngineLinkage`, and the plan-switch suite is `PlanSwitch`.
  - The test suite's 2.x SimpleGain documents (`test/abi/fixtures.h`, `test/support/v2_documents.h`) carry TFLITE- and LITERT-qualified `tensor_shape` rows with the gain at `[1, 1, 1]`, as the bundled files' TensorFlow rows do (below), and `AbiValidate.UpgradedSimpleGainDerivesTheV2FixturesFigures` (alpha.1's `AbiTranslate.UpgradedSimpleGainEqualsTheV2Fixture`) expects them. `AbiProcessor.Int16RingWithAConvertingProcessorOnEveryEngine` includes TFLite.
- **Docs:** the guides describe the 3.x runtime only. `docs/sphinx/usage.rst`, the usage guide:
  - Section 1: 1.1's State role points to 3.4; 1.2 names the registered id and the refusal of an unregistered one and gains the **Providers** bullet of the model configuration (the two-axis id, neutral and pinned entries, the `"provider"` key, the pin's refusal); 1.3 spells the required `Hard::budget`, states the on_miss, wait and ring-dtype semantics, the host domain beside the ring dtype, and with the contract-file section describes `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK`, the two-step for a parsed `"callback"` and the `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` declaration a backup function needs under clang; 1.5 names the `host_domains` key.
  - Section 2 is "Processors", the C descriptor as the guide describes it: the one processor per pipeline, the phases of one chunk with the edge directly before and after the engine and the thread each phase runs on, NULL against filled slots and the explicit "call super", the context accessors per slot with the `entry` index and `anira_handler_num_entries`, the ring accessors with the no-conversion rule and the ring-dtype exception, the hop rule, the failure rules, the Static snapshot and the write order of a chunk, what a State slot answers in each phase, what the phases bind and flip and that a State pair takes any dtype the engine accepts, the flags against the placement, the processor's slots with its `init`, and the shared lifecycle (the prepare record of `anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, the per-handler `prepared` pointer, `reset`, `unprepare`, `release`, the `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_*` names), with an int16-converting processor in C that keeps its scratch behind `prepared` and frees it in `unprepare`, and its twin, an `anira::Processor` whose `prepare` returns the `Processor::Prepared` with the scratch, defined after the nested `Prepared` class it allocates.
  - Sections 3 to 5 (the handler, prepare and the plan report, the Hard entries over tensors and their `_wait` twins, Static tensors, one-sided streaming, reset) are written on `anira::InferenceHandler` of `anira.hpp`, beside the C spelling of section 3.2, and call the tensor entries. 3.2 "The C handler" lists the entries of `anira/abi/handler.h` in the reference style of 1.6 and 3.1: the Hard entries over host tensors first (one tensor per slot, the three descriptions of memory with a planar example over JUCE's channel pointers that needs no cast and an interleaved example that assigns `strides = {1, channels}` after `anira_tensor_init_host`, in place, the empty tensor, the refusals and their order, `delivered`), then a host with channel pointers (one planar tensor built once over the pointer array, `shape[1]` stored before each call) and the status and counts of every entry; the one processor and what prepare checks for it; and the registration of an engine (the entry, the slots, the context, the engine's three levels, the refusals, the pool rule with the provider and its options, the lifecycle-lock rule, `engine_flags` and `binding` in the report). 3.3 "Runtime tensors" covers the factories and their refusals, the NULL rules of the accessors, planar host memory, release and ownership, sync tokens, the draft folder and its promotion rule and the MSVC `/wd4201` note. 3.4 "State tensors" covers the declared pair with the bundled StatefulAccumulatorNetwork's file, feed and capture, a failed inference, reset, plan switches, what the host and a processor see, the validation rules, the host domain's override of a State pair's domain, the aliasing under a plan, and the engine rows the fixture names and why.
  - 4.1 describes the host's block range and its price: the latency covers every block size held from call to call, and a host that changes the size between calls can still miss a block in rare sequences (#228; measured: 1 and 2 misses in 480 varying calls on SimpleGainNetwork and GuitarLSTM, 314 and 159 under the fixed contract). *Fractional blocks*: the example is a fixed-block host (the running count assumes one block size), no longer `prepareToPlay`, and a plugin anchors on a stream at the host rate with its range. 4.3 says that the handler selects plans and that a custom backend is a registered engine. 5.3 shows the Static entries; 5.5 says what a reset re-initialises (declared state, the engine's own state, the processor's own state).
  - Section 6 "Teardown and unloading": a plugin destroys its handlers, then its contexts, before its host unloads it, from its instance teardown or module-exit entry point, and never from its own static destructors or `DllMain`.
  - Providers across the guide: the capabilities as the runtimes report them with the provider options extension (3.1), the candidates' rule (a plan per entry and candidate, the syntax at add and the availability at create), the plan report's `provider_id`, and `set_plan` keeping the state.
  - 2.x code is pointed at `anira/compat/v2.hpp` (as in `custom_preprocessing.rst` and `custom_engines.rst`).
- **Docs:** `docs/sphinx/custom_engines.rst` is "Custom Engines" (alpha.1's custom-backends guide), rewritten around `anira_engine_desc` and `anira::Engine`: what an engine is and where it sits; the registration and its refusals; the descriptor's slots with its `providers` list; the lifecycle with its records and the context, walked from the outermost level in (`init` with `anira_init_info` once per engine object, `load` with `anira_engine_load_info` once per pooled model and `unload`, `prepare` with the shared `anira_prepare_info` and the loaded pointer once per handler and `unprepare`, `reset` for exclusive handlers, `process` on a claimed slot or under `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE`, `release`), the load record's `provider` / `provider_id`; the adapter rule; the flags (the fourth, `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS`, honoured per plan) and where a registered engine appears in the reports; declared state over the two buffers and the dtype rule, with the aliasing paragraph; pooling and lifetime, the provider in the pool key; a gain engine in C filling the eight slots by name and in C++ returning a `Loaded` from `load` whose `prepare` returns a `Prepared`, both listing a provider (`providers()` in C++); the version-neutral section on driving a bundled engine from a custom one, kept with its example as an `Engine::Prepared` that owns the engine's objects; and a note sending `BackendBase` readers to the migration guide. The guide (and the README) say that the `USE_<ENGINE>` definitions are no longer on the installed `anira::anira`: which engines a build compiled in is `anira_enabled_engines`.
- **Docs:** `docs/sphinx/custom_preprocessing.rst` is rewritten onto the processor and `Processor` / `Processor::Prepared` (alpha.1: the 2.x `PrePostProcessor`): when a processor is needed, the rules, one registration and one prepared object per handler with its scratch, the phases and `reset` on it, why `unique_ptr`, the lifecycle with its `init` and the shared record (its examples take `anira_prepare_info` / `PrepareInfo`), a normalising processor, a spectrogram processor whose transform runs in `pre_process` under `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`, the split over the entry scratch that moves a heavy transform to `before_inference` / `after_inference` in C (the C spectral example over `prepared`, `reset` and `unprepare`), Static and State tensors in a processor, the integration in C and C++ (the C++ examples define `prepare` after the nested `Prepared` class it allocates), with one note pointing 2.x readers to `migration.rst`.

It names the clang-tidy changed-files rule.

The docs of `anira_shutdown`, `anira_release_core_if_idle`, `anira_context_destroy` and `anira_handler_destroy` (`abi/anira.yml`, regenerated; doc-only, no symbol changes), `anira::Context` and `anira::shutdown()` in `anira.hpp`, `anira::v2::InferenceHandler`'s destructor, and the private `anira::Core` (the pool-lifetime section, `shutdown()`, `make_inference_thread()`) state the teardown rule. `anira.hpp`'s preamble names `anira::v2::JsonConfigLoader`, and the `Engine(std::string id)` and `Pipeline::add_engine` docs state the `anira.v2.custom` exception to the `anira.` prefix rule. The aliasing promise (`ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS`) says what holds: the addresses stay put across the chunks of aliasing plans (a flipping plan's chunk in between moves the pair), and what a failed call of an aliasing engine left in the buffer is the engine's (the last-good rule of a flipping pair does not apply); the Engine class doc lists the flag, `EngineContext::inputs()` names the load record, and the capabilities enumerators say their rows' strings are valid until the next probe. `ANIRA_TIMEOUT` and `ANIRA_MISSED` reference each other (a `_wait` entry's timeout is `ANIRA_MISSED`). The doc of `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` states the rule that one table spells every phase: `k_phase_words` / `phase_word` of `src/capi/words.h`, beside the provider and engine words (the lower-case names of the `anira_phase` values, `pre_process` to `unload`), from which every message anira writes about a slot takes the phase's word (a processor phase that fails or that an accessor refuses, a processor's or an engine's refused init, load or prepare: `the engine 'x' refused load: it returned N (...)`; a failed engine call: `inference failed in session N: the engine of plan P returned S`; the ring-dtype and real-time-promise refusals that name a processor phase); the texts are unchanged, and the inference value's doc says what is logged (no report row carries a phase). Test: `AbiProcessor.EveryPhaseHasOneWord`. - **Docs:** the v3 roadmap (section 11 of `docs/anira-v3-architecture.md`, with the matching notes in `docs/anira-v3-c-abi-decision-record.md`) follows Fares' decision of 2026-10-29. M2 lists its pull requests: PR 9 (the web hooks), PR 11 (the lean web runtime, brought forward from M5: Wasm Workers, Wasm Audio Worklets, generated TypeScript bindings, one configuration for native and web), PR 10, 10b, 12 and 13, with the exit criterion and `alpha.2` unchanged. A new milestone M2b, after `alpha.2` and exiting with `alpha.3`, completes the Hard contract and adds model variants: PR 4 (MEASURED / UNTIL_STABLE budgets, moved out of M2, so PR 12 no longer depends on it), PR 4b (inline execution), PR 7b (State initial values) and the variant axis (one plan per variant and candidate, per-plan budgets with the worst case reported, `thl::RCU` natively and the atomic on Wasm, a stateful switch clearing state at the next boundary). M3 Async now exits with `alpha.4`; M4 gains the emsdk 6.x move at its end (the prebuilt ONNX Runtime WASM archive must be rebuilt with it, anira-project/backends#24); M5 is folded into M2b and M6, which opens with typedoc, the Sphinx C-API page, the JSON benchmark sweep, `[[deprecated]]` on `compat/v2.hpp` and the abidiff baseline at `beta.2` before the freeze; `docs/sphinx/migration.rst` states the `[[deprecated]]` timing to match. A corruption of the architecture document (four NUL-delimited `CONTEXTRY` placeholders left by the machine-to-context rename) reads "machinery" again.
- **Docs (Web):** the guides under `docs/sphinx/web-api/` describe the 3.x package only (their final shape is the typed layer's: `audio_nodes`, `defineProcessor`, `defineEngine`, under the Web items); `installation_building` also covers the module's exports, the `wasm-abi` preset and its gates, and `custom_inference_backends` the built-in `org.anira.onnxruntime-web` engine. The reference pages of the 2.x classes are gone and `AniraError` is new. `web/README.md` and the root README's web section show the 3.x setup, installed from the `next` dist-tag, and `migration.rst` gains the web section, from the 2.x TypeScript classes onward.
- **Naming:** the guides, the README, the configure-time messages and the tests say engine where they said backend for an inference engine (backend is the pair, an engine on a provider, everywhere the 3.x vocabulary applies; ExecuTorch's own word backend is quoted as its delegate); the test suites follow (above).
- **Examples:** the JUCE plugin (variants 0-7), the CLAP plugin and the three benchmarks run on `anira.hpp` (alpha.1: the 3.x configuration handed to the 2.x handler through the bridge).
  - **The handler:** a `Context`; a `Pipeline` of the model's inference stage in the handler's initializer, one chained expression, `Pipeline().add_engine(...).add_inference(...).add_processor(...)` (the bypass engine `org.anira.examples.bypass` behind the "BYPASS" setting, the clear engine behind the advanced benchmark's baseline and, for GuitarLSTM, `HybridNNProcessor`); one `anira::InferenceHandler`; the audio is one planar tensor over the host's channel pointers. The CLAP plugin's `clap_init` checks `anira_check_abi`.
  - **Backend choice (JUCE and CLAP):** the parameter listener stores the choice in an atomic. `processBlock` / `process` applies it through `PlanReport::find` and `set_plan`, over the `PlanReport` of the last prepare (nothing changes when the build or the model has no such plan); there are no plan tables built at prepare.
  - **The host's block range:** the JUCE and CLAP plugins declare the host's real block range on the Hard contract instead of a fixed block: the JUCE plugin `hard_host_blocks(1, samplesPerBlock, sampleRate)` in every variant, both handlers of variant 7 included (JUCE names the largest block only), the CLAP plugin `hard_host_blocks(max(1, minFrameCount), maxFrameCount, sampleRate)`. Every call already carried its own block. The reported latency, which both report to the host, covers every block size of the range: at 48 kHz and a 512-sample maximum the CNN reports 4602 samples instead of 3584, SimpleGainNetwork 1024 instead of 512, GuitarLSTM (the CLAP plugin's model) 767 instead of 512. Under a fixed contract, a host sequence of varying blocks missed 314 of 480 blocks of SimpleGainNetwork and 159 of GuitarLSTM. `examples.rst` and `usage.rst` (section 4.1) describe the range and its price. The benchmarks keep fixed blocks: they choose the block themselves.
  - **JUCE variant 1** gives each model entry the embedded file named like its path in one loop over `BinaryData::namedResourceList`; there is no per-engine switch any more. **JUCE variant 7:** the encoder and the decoder are two instances of one small struct, a model with its contract, handler and plan report.
  - **Benchmarks:** the cnn-size and advanced benchmarks run one candidate per run (plan 0). The simple benchmark finds its ONNX Runtime plan with `PlanReport::find`.
- **Examples:** the four minimal-inference examples that link anira (ONNX Runtime, LibTorch, LiteRT, TensorFlow Lite) read the model path and the tensor shapes their engine is fed from the bundled model files with the 3.x read-back (`examples/minimal-inference/minimal_model.h`: `anira::ModelConfig`, `SpecView`, the entry's layout), no longer through `anira/compat/v3_to_v2.h`, and hold their data in `std::vector` instead of anira's 2.x `BufferF` / `MemoryBlock`.
- **Examples (Web):** `web/examples/model-file` picks its plan in the report of every prepare (the first and the device change's re-prepare) with `PlanReport.find` and `InferenceHandler.setPlan`; both web examples set the page's `AudioContext` options where they create it, without a one-call helper.
- **Bundled models:** the fixtures describe their TensorFlow exports. `gain.model.json` and `stereo_gain.model.json` carry on their tflite and litert rows a `tensors` record with a layout for the static gain (the export holds it at rank 3, `[1, 1, 1]`, against the spec's `[1]`; the adapters' shape check refuses the rank-1 form and says so with both shapes) and, on the litert row, the keys `output_0` / `output_1` for the two outputs (LiteRT lists them in the file's order, the peak first). (`stateful_accumulator.model.json`, above.)
- **Pre-release names (no tag carried them).** The spellings and shapes of the `v3` snapshots between v3.0.0-alpha.1 and this release, whose final forms the items above describe; old → new (what it was):
  - **C ABI:**
    - `anira_processor_phase` / `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_PHASE_FORCE32` → `anira_phase` / `ANIRA_PHASE_FORCE32` (the phase enum's name between alpha.1's `anira_stage_phase` and its final name, through the stage → processor rename; the renumbering that gave `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` the value 3 happened before `anira_phase`).
    - `anira_processor_prepare_info` / `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_PREPARE_INFO_INIT` → `anira_prepare_info` / `ANIRA_PREPARE_INFO_INIT` (the processor's prepare record of `anira/abi/processor.h`, before it moved to `anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, was shared with the engine and grew the tail words `flags` and `reserved`; a processor renamed the type in the signature of its prepare function (`const anira_prepare_info* info`) and the initializer where a test filled one by hand, every field it read keeping its name and meaning).
    - `anira_engine_prepare_info` / `ANIRA_ENGINE_PREPARE_INFO_INIT` → `anira_engine_load_info` / `ANIRA_ENGINE_LOAD_INFO_INIT` (the engine's prepare record, 64 bytes on LP64 and 44 on ILP32, with `instances` at least 1; it became the load slot's record, `instances` 0 for a model whose handlers are exclusive, and the provider fields grew it to 96/64).
    - the one-level engine `prepare` (`anira_engine_prepare_fn` without `loaded`, which loaded the model of the row and sized its instances) → `load` (`anira_engine_load_fn`) / `unload` (`anira_engine_unload_fn`) per loaded model, `prepare` (`anira_engine_prepare_fn` with `loaded`) per handler, and `init` (`anira_engine_init_fn`, with `anira_processor_init_fn` of the same shape) per object; `anira_engine_desc` had eight slots in the order `process`, `reset`, `prepare`, `unprepare`, `load`, `unload`, `init`, `release` at 96 bytes on LP64 and 56 on ILP32 before `providers` (112/68) and `query` (120/72) were appended. The migration of a snapshot engine: move the model loading of the old `prepare` into `load` and hand what it loaded back through `out_loaded`; move what the old unprepare freed into `unload`; keep in the new `prepare` only what the engine holds per handler, or hand back NULL when it holds nothing; an engine of a stateful model (`ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL`, a declared State pair) that counted on `instances = 1` and kept its per-stream state behind the old prepared pointer builds that state in `prepare` when `info->flags` carries `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`, its own executor included; a descriptor filled by name from `ANIRA_ENGINE_DESC_INIT` / `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_DESC_INIT` compiles unchanged with the new slots NULL, a positional initializer is rewritten by name; a processor changes nothing unless it wants `init`.
    - `anira_engine_ctx.reserved_ptr0` → `anira_engine_ctx.loaded` (same offset 48; `abi/layout-0.txt` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts` renamed the row; an engine that read `reserved_ptr0`, which nothing should have, reads `loaded`), and `anira_engine_ctx.flags` → its first bit `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE`.
    - `anira_processor_ctx.reserved_ptr0` / `reserved_ptr1` → `engine_id` / `provider_id` (two of the record's pointer slots; `anira_processor_ctx` stays 64 bytes with every offset unchanged).
    - `anira_processor_engine_id` / `anira_processor_provider_id` (the id alone) → `anira_processor_engine(ctx, &engine, &engine_id)` / `anira_processor_provider(ctx, &provider, &provider_id)`.
    - `anira_model_config_default_engine_id` (`_default_engine_id`), `_model_provider_id`, `_default_provider_id` (id getters beside the value getters, as alpha.1's `_model_engine_id` was) → folded into `anira_model_config_default_engine`, `anira_model_config_model_provider` and `anira_model_config_default_provider`, which read the pair (the C++ `*_id()` getters likewise into `anira::EngineRef` / `anira::ProviderRef`).
    - a custom provider as `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` beside its name → `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with its name (the pair rule; a custom engine as `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` beside its id → `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM`, as alpha.1's getters spelled it too).
    - the processor's `prepare(handler, report, user_data)` of the first processor release → `anira_processor_prepare_fn` `(info, user_data, out_prepared)` with the per-handler `prepared` pointer every `anira_processor_fn` receives (the first form gave a processor nothing per handler to hang its per-entry scratch on, so two handlers of one pipeline shared one scratch through `anira_processor_desc.user_data`; the C++ `anira::Processor` followed).
    - the custom engine's id given to `anira_pipeline_add_engine` → given to `anira_custom_engine_create(engine_id, desc, out, err)`, with `anira_pipeline_add_engine(pipeline, engine, err)` taking none (one engine under two ids, in one pipeline or two, was legal; the id was part of the pool key, `engine::Model::operator==`). Migration: pass the id to `anira_custom_engine_create` and drop it from `anira_pipeline_add_engine`; a program that added one engine under two ids creates two engines.
    - C++ `anira::ProcessorPrepareInfo` and `anira::EnginePrepareInfo` → `anira::PrepareInfo` and `anira::EngineLoadInfo`; `Engine::prepare(const EnginePrepareInfo&)` returning `Engine::Prepared` → `Engine::load(const EngineLoadInfo&)` returning `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Loaded>` plus `Loaded::prepare(const PrepareInfo&)`; a snapshot `Engine` split its old `prepare` into the two, what the old `Prepared` loaded once moving to the `Loaded`, what it kept per handler or per stream staying; a `Processor` renamed the parameter type of its `prepare`.
    - C++ `anira::Engine` default-constructed and added as `Pipeline::add_engine(id, impl)` → `explicit Engine(std::string id)` and `Pipeline::add_engine(impl)` (the additions drop their first argument).
    - `anira_enabled_backends` → `anira_enabled_engines` (C++ `anira::enabled_backends()` → `anira::enabled_engines()`; it lists engines, one row per engine on the CPU path), and C++ `anira::Engine::available(const InitInfo&)` → `anira::Engine::query(const InitInfo&)` (the word of the descriptor's `query` slot it fills).
    - `anira/abi/machine.h` → `anira/abi/context.h` (the runtime header's first name, with the snapshots' `anira_machine_*` runtime entries) and C++ `Machine` → `Context`; the clock (`anira_now_ms`, `anira_now_ns`) and the shutdown family (`anira_shutdown`, `anira_release_core_if_idle`, `anira_has_core`) moved out of `anira/abi/context.h` into `anira/abi/core.h` while ABI 0.2 was untagged.
    - `anira_context_drain_log` and `anira_context_num_inference_threads` (the per-context twins of the first draft) → gone; `anira_drain_log` (`anira/abi/log.h`) and `anira_num_inference_threads` (`anira/abi/thread.h`) are the entries, since the queue and the pool are the core's.
    - the host-block names of the snapshots: `anira_contract_hard_geometry` (the read-back, since 0.2) → `anira_contract_hard_host_blocks`; `anira_contract_hard_set_host_blocks(contract, block_min, block_max, rate)` → `anira_contract_hard_set_host_blocks(contract, host_block_min, host_block_max, host_rate)`; `anira_contract_hard_host_blocks(contract, &block_min, &block_max, &rate)` → `anira_contract_hard_host_blocks(contract, &host_block_min, &host_block_max, &host_rate)`; `ContractHandle::hard_host_blocks(block_min, block_max, rate)` → `ContractHandle::hard_host_blocks(host_block_min, host_block_max, host_rate)`; web `ContractHandle.hardGeometry` → `hardHostBlocks`; web `ContractHandle.hard(anira, { blockMin, blockMax, rate, budgetMs })` → `ContractHandle.hard(anira, { hostBlockMin, hostBlockMax, hostRate, budgetMs })`; web `contract.hardHostBlocks(blockMin, blockMax, rate)` → `contract.hardHostBlocks(hostBlockMin, hostBlockMax, hostRate)` (TypeScript renamed the option keys of `ContractHandle.hard`).
    - the twelve float32 Hard entries of `anira/abi/handler.h` → removed, the tensor entries of the same name taking their place: `anira_handler_process_f32`, `anira_handler_process_f32_inplace`, `anira_handler_process_f32_multi`, `anira_handler_push_data_f32`, `anira_handler_push_data_f32_multi`, `anira_handler_pop_data_f32`, `anira_handler_pop_data_f32_multi` and the five `_wait` twins `anira_handler_process_f32_wait`, `anira_handler_process_f32_inplace_wait`, `anira_handler_process_f32_multi_wait`, `anira_handler_pop_data_f32_wait`, `anira_handler_pop_data_f32_multi_wait` (added and removed within this cycle, PR 3 and #190; `v3.0.0-alpha.1` does not carry them, it has no handler entry at all, so only builds from `v3` snapshots are affected; 161 promised names and 167 wasm exports after the removal). Each one was the tensor entry of the same name over a planar float32 tensor, which is what its body built through `anira::PlanarFloatAdapter` (`anira_handler::m_float_adapter`, gone); kept, they would have frozen twelve names at 3.0.0, a second count protocol (`num_out` of the multi forms was the request and the result at once, and a missed block once zeroed the request of a host that reused the array) and a float-only family on a surface that is dtype-generic. `ANIRA_ABI_MAJOR` is 0, which is what makes the removal legal; `tools/abi/gen.py --diff-against <ref>` lists the twelve as `BREAKING` and exits 0 (`--strict` exits 1; no CI step runs either). Gone with them: the float32 ring refusal of the float forms (a tensor of another dtype than its slot's is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`) and the `### Fixed` entry of this cycle about `num_out` after a miss. The migration of a snapshot host: at prepare, call `anira_tensor_init_host_planar(&block, channels, num_channels, ANIRA_DTYPE_F32, 2, shape)` once over the channel-pointer array with `shape = {num_channels, max_block}`; per callback store `block.shape[1] = n` and call the tensor entry of the same name; `_inplace` was `anira_handler_process(h, &block, &block, i, &delivered)` in the one-slot form of the time (same tensor as input and output); separate buffers are two tensors, the input's over `const float* const*` with `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY` ORed into `flags`; a `_multi` form takes one tensor per slot, where the float forms accepted a NULL pointer at a request of 0; `delivered` replaces the `num_out` protocol.
    - the Hard entries' signatures of #189 and #190: `anira_handler_process(h, &in, &out, 0, &delivered)` (one index shared by both sides) → `anira_handler_process(h, &in, 0, &out, 0, &delivered)`; `anira_handler_process_wait(h, &in, &out, timeout_ms, 0, &delivered)` → `anira_handler_process_wait(h, &in, 0, &out, 0, &delivered, timeout_ms)`; `anira_handler_pop_data_wait(h, &out, timeout_ms, 0, &delivered)` → `anira_handler_pop_data_wait(h, &out, 0, &delivered, timeout_ms)` (one index shared by two unrelated lists only ever worked for a model whose stream stands at the same position on both sides; the Breaking lines of this cycle about handler entries concern builds from `v3` snapshots alone).
    - the Static slot of the snapshots' `_multi` forms: the flat `[1, values]` description, its clamp and the clamped count (`delivered` clamped to the tensor's size for a Static output) → the whole tensor in the spec's shape and dtype (`[1]` for the bundled gain models, where the flat form was `[1, 1]`); the empty element `shape[1] == 0` → a rank of 1 or more with any extent 0; a single form naming a Static slot → `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; a Static output zeroed or repeated per miss policy → the stored value; under `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` the stored value is filled first. The 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` kept its behaviour (the count of values inside the `float***` block, the clamp, the miss rules).
    - `anira_em_pipeline_register_js_engine(pipeline, engine_id, js_key, hook_mask, engine_flags, err)` → `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine` with the same parameters (as the pipeline's members say add).
    - the processor hook bits of `anira/anira_em.h`: `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_AFTER` 8 → 16, `_PREPARE` 16 → 32, `_RELEASE` 32 → 64, `_RESET` 64 → 128, `_UNPREPARE` 128 → 256, `_INIT` 256 → 512 (`PRE` 1, `POST` 2, `BEFORE` 4 unchanged), `ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION` 1 → 2: one index space with the phases; a C caller that spelled the bits by name recompiles, one that spelled numbers changes them.
    - `anira_em_alloc_thread_stack`, `anira_em_free_thread_stack` and `anira_em_init_instance` (the per-instance stack and thread-local block of the alpha.2 runtime of the snapshots) → removed (Emscripten gives every Wasm Worker and the worklet its own); `anira_em_hooks_version` → removed (the registry object carries `version`, `ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION` stays its value); the trampolines' read of `Module.anira` → the scope's registry; the node gate `anira_em_smoke_thread`'s second instance of the module in a `worker_threads` Worker → a Wasm Worker the test creates.
    - the 2026-10 web layer (against #213 only; no alpha shipped it) → the names of `anira.hpp`:
      | 2026-10 web layer | follows `anira.hpp` |
      | --- | --- |
      | `Handler` (`Handler.ts`) | `InferenceHandler` (`InferenceHandler.ts`) |
      | `handler.latency(slot)` | `handler.getLatency(slot)`, and the new `handler.getLatencies()` |
      | `Contract` (`Contract.ts`) | `ContractHandle` (`ContractHandle.ts`) |
      | `contract.setGeometry(min, max, rate)` | `contract.hardHostBlocks(hostBlockMin, hostBlockMax, hostRate)` |
      | `model.attachBytes(i, bytes)` | `model.setModelBytes(i, bytes)` (chains) |
      | `pipeline.addInference(model)` | `pipeline.inference(model)` |
      | `x.ptr` (Context, ModelConfig, Contract, Pipeline, Handler) | `x.native()` |
      | `AniraError.code` | `AniraError.status` |
      | `anira.setLogHook(fn)`, `scopeRegistry().logHook` | `anira.setLogSink(fn)`, `scopeRegistry().logSink` |
      | `context.threads` | `context.numInferenceThreads()` |
      | `ContractHandle.hard({ warmup })` | `ContractHandle.hard({ warmupIterations })` |
      | `ModelEntry { engine, engineId }` (`fetchModel`) | `ModelEntry { engine: EngineRef { kind, id } }` (`id` empty beside a built-in engine) |
      | `pipeline.addProcessor(name, { hooks })` | `pipeline.addProcessor(name, { phases })` |
      | `InferenceHandler.create(...).prepare(contract)` chaining to the handler | returning the `PlanReport` |
      | `setStaticInput` float32 and rank 1 on the handler, numbers as float32 on the node | dtype-generic on both |
      | the `threads: 0` sentence ("a host that drives inference itself") of `Context` | gone |
    - the snapshots' hand-rolled web runtime (U288 of the web area) → the typed layer: configs, pipelines, contexts and handlers built over the raw `_anira_*` exports of `AniraWeb.module` (record offsets from `abi/layout.ts`), a JavaScript engine as a `JSBackend` subclass registered with `AniraWeb.registerJSEngine` (`JSBackendBase` before), JavaScript pre/post-processing as a `JSProcessor` added with `AniraWeb.addJSStage` (`JSPrePostProcessor` before), the inference thread `core/InferenceThread` over a context, and the Worker's `'stopped'` message replaced by the `has_exited` poll.
    - the compound `engine:provider` string of the snapshots' model and context files (`"onnxruntime:coreml"`, a `provider_options` set for `litert:gpu` or `executorch:xnnpack`) → two keys, `"engine"` and `"provider"` (migration: move the suffix into the key; the compound survives as a label in messages, `backend_label`).
    - the snapshots' `provider_options` handling → per backend: the kind was consumed per kind, not per backend (a set for `litert:gpu` or `executorch:xnnpack` was accepted in any build with ONNX Runtime, copied into the matching plans' pool key and then ignored, which could cause a needless second load), a custom engine never saw its set (the load record had no options field), a set for the default provider, a misspelled backend word and a count of options without arrays were silently ignored, and `anira_ext_provider_options.sets` was indexed at the library's record size although its doc promised each record read within its own `struct_size`, as `anira_pipeline_add_inference`'s candidates were.
    - `InferenceManager::set_plan_backends` / `SessionElement::set_plan_backends` (the plan table handed to the session before its prepare, throwing `std::logic_error` on a prepared session) and `SessionElement::m_plan_backends` (`index -> backend`) → gone: the table is the session's from create; the 2.x session's own table (one row per configured model in `m_model_data` order, then every other backend of the build, `set_inference_backend` / `InferenceManager::set_backend` selecting the first plan on the backend, a backend no plan runs on leaving the selection unchanged and logged once, `RtSite::BackendWithoutPlan`, which a 2.x table never reached) went with the 2.x engine path; `PlanSwitch.ABackendWithoutAPlanLeavesTheSelection` (the 2.x selection by backend on a table with no row for it) with it.
    - the engine-free pass-through the C handler ran for an `anira.v2.custom` row (the 2.x `BackendBase::process` of a legacy loaded model per session, never pooled, its slots bound by position and reported `ANIRA_BINDING_POSITION`, the 2.x path binding by position) and its default-provider rule in the provider check → an engine added under the id; the test rigs' `attach_processor`, which attached a 2.x `BackendBase` twin to a C handler's custom plan → gone (the 2.x gates stayed for the 2.x rigs); the bridge, which had no pipeline, kept `anira.v2.custom` as its one custom id, served by the 2.x pass-through, until the cut-over.
    - the planned C++ `stage::CustomBackend` of the design document → dropped: `Pipeline::add_engine(impl)`; the JS hook object's phase table is stated by phase name.
    - the custom engine's query called at every `anira_handler_prepare` (`check_providers`, off the main thread) → asked once at `anira_handler_create`, its answers reused at prepare (`QueryUse::Reuse`); the snapshots' C++ `available` → `query`.
    - the snapshots' `AbiContext` `HostOnlyCapabilityRows` (one host edge per backend row at `ANIRA_RUNG_STATIC`, a Host-only report) → `AbiContext.TheCapabilitiesListTheRuntimesProviders`; `AbiRtLatch.CapacityNeverLatches` → `AnEntrysCapacityNeverLatchesADroppedHopDoes`; `ATimeRatioScalesTheFlexibleHopAndMustDivide` → `ATimeRatioScalesTheAnchorHopIntoTheFlexibleWindow`; `AFractionalBlockMaxGivesAFractionalFlexibleHop` → `AFractionalBlockPinsTheFlexibleWindowFromTheAnchorHop`.
    - the default-engine fallback of the snapshots (silent, to plan 0; a default engine with several plans started on the first of them) → the first plan of the default engine on the default provider, with one Warning at prepare naming what was asked and the plan it starts on.
  - **C++, engines and the core:**
    - `anira/utils/RtLatch.h` → `src/utils/RtLatch.h` (the latch header, installed between the tags until #229 made the core's C++ headers private).
    - `anira::Stage` → `anira::Processor`, `Stage::Prepared` → `Processor::Prepared`, `StageContext` → `ProcessorContext` (the custom stage of `anira.hpp`, decision 10.18; `PrepareInfo` and `InitInfo` kept their names).
    - `namespace anira::stage` (`stage::Inference`, `stage::Custom`), `stage::Inference::engine()`, `Pipeline::AnyStage`, `Pipeline::add`, the initializer-list constructor of `Pipeline` and `Pipeline::inference` → `Pipeline::add_inference`, `Pipeline::add_processor` and `Pipeline::add_engine` (the stage objects of the first pipeline: an engine belongs to the pipeline, which the inference stage's model entries name by id, not to `stage::Inference`; `anira::Pipeline p{anira::stage::Inference(model).engine(e), anira::stage::Custom(s)};` is `anira::Pipeline p; p.add_engine(e).add_inference(model).add_processor(s);`).
    - `Pipeline::register_engine` → `Pipeline::add_engine` (the C++ addition of a custom engine; was `register_engine`).
    - `HybridNNStage` → `HybridNNProcessor` (`extras/models/hybrid-nn/HybridNNProcessor.h`, the extras' HybridNN processing); `anira::v2::LegacyProcessorStage` → `anira::v2::LegacyProcessor` (the compat shim's 2.x processor as a 3.x processor).

    - In `anira/anira_em.h`: the stage hook bits → `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*` (the engine hooks keep `ANIRA_EM_ENGINE_HOOK_*`), `anira_em_stage_desc_init` → `anira_em_processor_desc_init`, `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_stage` → `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_processor`, `anira_em_pipeline_register_js_engine` → `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine` (as the pipeline's members say add), and the registry method the module calls `stage` → `processor` (the Emscripten companion's JS processor and engine).

    - `anira::ContextConfig` (`anira/ContextConfig.h`, the 2.x struct) → `anira::CoreConfig` (`anira/CoreConfig.h`, with `LogConfig` / `LogDrain` / `WaitStrategy` / `LogLevel` unchanged) → removed with the 2.x classes (the 2.x handler took a `CoreConfig`, the same struct under its truthful name, and the 2.x `InferenceManager` constructor mapped it onto the C struct through `anira::capi::make_context_config`, gone with them; its 2.x spelling is `anira::v2::ContextConfig`, its 3.x successor `anira::ContextConfig` of `anira.hpp`).

    - `NoLibTorchModel` .. `NoExecuTorchModel` (five real-time sites of the inference thread) → `RtSite::NoModelForBackend` (`k_rt_site_names` shortened with it) → gone with the 2.x engine path.
    - `run(ctx, chunk, reset_first)` → `run(ctx, reset_first, stop)` (`engine::Prepared::run`: the chunk left with the 2.x engine path, the stop signal came with the claim loop's backoff); `SessionElement::m_engine_generation` → `SessionElement::PlanSlot::m_engine_generation` (the reset boundary, the plan's since the per-plan reset).

Every request (`initInferenceWorker`, `registerEngine` / `unregisterEngine`, `registerStage` / `unregisterStage`, `destroy`, the worklet's `configure`) carried a `requestId` its reply and its error echo, and `waitForWorkerMessage(target, type, requestId)` rejected only on an error of that request, never on another one's in flight on the same target (`request()` posted and waited); `InferenceWorker.stop()` freed the Worker's stack only after the Worker answered `'destroyed'` (it destroyed its engines and called into the module no more), where it had freed it right after posting `destroy`.

Its copy rule `samplesToCopy` (`web/src/workers/delivery.ts`: the whole quantum on `ANIRA_MISSED`, nothing on a failure, the delivered count otherwise) followed a first worklet that copied 0 samples on a miss, so every miss was silence. `worklet-base.ts` declared `AudioWorkletProcessor`, `AudioParamDescriptor` and `registerProcessor` globally, so `workers/worklet-base.d.ts` carried them, and `src/worklet.d.ts` went.

### Deprecated

- **C++ (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`):** every entity of `namespace anira::v2`, the 2.x source-compatible face: removed one minor release after 3.0.0; no `[[deprecated]]` attribute through the alpha releases, one on every entity before 3.0.0 (the roadmap revision of 2026-10-29).

### Removed

- **Breaking:** C++ (2.x classes) — the 2.x `anira::BackendBase` (`<anira/backends/BackendBase.h>`, `src/engines/BackendBase.cpp`) and the 2.x engine path of the core: subclassing `anira::BackendBase` goes with the 2.x runtime and gets no shim. Gone with it: the two 2.x `InferenceManager` constructors (over a `BackendBase*` and a `CoreConfig` or a context config), `InferenceManager::set_backend` / `get_backend`, the 2.x plan table (`engine::legacy_plan_requests`, `engine::model_of`, `engine::engine_of`), the legacy adapter (`engine::LegacyLoaded` / `LegacyPrepared`, `src/engines/LegacyAdapter.{h,cpp}`) with the `ChunkBuffers` its 2.x virtual took (the engine room's `process` / `run` / `run_claimed` take the context alone), the `Source::Legacy` / `Source::Roundtrip` plans, the CUSTOM-first initial selection of `Core::create_session` (the C handler selects its initial plan), the `NoModelForBackend` and `BackendWithoutPlan` real-time sites, `capi::make_context_config` and `test_BackendBase`. Migration: a custom engine is an `anira::Engine` (`anira.hpp`, or `anira_engine_desc` in C) added to the pipeline under its id, which `docs/sphinx/migration.rst` maps virtual by virtual; a 2.x custom backend is an `anira::Engine` constructed with `anira::v2::k_custom_engine_id`, and `anira::v2::PassthroughEngine` is the 2.x base pass-through. The adapter suites build their `engine::Model` records by hand (`anira_test::model_record`) and run over plain float buffers.
- **Breaking:** C++ (2.x classes) — the library's own 2.x classes of alpha.1, which nothing in anira used once the core ran on the 3.x types (D10 of the PR 10b digest), with their headers, API pages and suites: `anira::InferenceConfig` with `ModelData`, `TensorShape`, `TensorShapeList` and `ProcessingSpec` (`<anira/InferenceConfig.h>`, `src/InferenceConfig.cpp`, `test_InferenceConfig`); `anira::PrePostProcessor` (`<anira/PrePostProcessor.h>`, `src/PrePostProcessor.cpp`, `test_PrePostProcessor`); `anira::HostConfig` and `ReferenceStream` (`<anira/utils/HostConfig.h>`, and the `HostConfigReference` cases of `test_SessionElement`, whose anchor rule `test_Validate` covers); `anira::InferenceBackend` (`<anira/utils/InferenceBackend.h>`); `anira::Buffer`, `BufferF` and `MemoryBlock` (`<anira/utils/Buffer.h>`, `<anira/utils/MemoryBlock.h>`, `test_Buffer`); and `<anira/utils/helperFunctions.h>` (`test_HelperFunctions`). alpha.1's installed `anira/utils/RingBuffer.h` and `anira/scheduler/InferenceThread.h`, private now (`src/utils/RingBuffer.h`, `src/scheduler/InferenceThread.h`), no longer include `Buffer.h`. Migration, one name for one name:
  | 2.x (removed) | 2.x spelling of `anira/compat/v2.hpp` | 3.x |
  |---|---|---|
  | `anira::InferenceConfig`, `ModelData`, `TensorShape`, `ProcessingSpec` | `anira::v2::InferenceConfig`, ... | `anira::ModelConfig` plus a Hard `anira::ContractHandle` |
  | `anira::HostConfig` | `anira::v2::HostConfig` | the Hard contract's host blocks, the model config's anchor |
  | `anira::InferenceBackend` | `anira::v2::InferenceBackend` | `anira::EngineKind` (`anira_engine`) |
  | `anira::PrePostProcessor` | `anira::v2::PrePostProcessor` (run through `anira::v2::LegacyProcessor`) | `anira::Processor` (`anira_processor_desc`) |
  | `anira::Buffer` / `BufferF` / `MemoryBlock` | `anira::v2::BufferF` (a view) | `anira_tensor` / `std::vector` |
  | `anira/utils/helperFunctions.h` | | the standard library |
- **Breaking:** C++ (2.x classes) — alpha.1's 2.x context configuration, `anira::ContextConfig` of `<anira/ContextConfig.h>` (`anira::CoreConfig` of `<anira/CoreConfig.h>` in the snapshots), with `anira::LogConfig` and the `anira::LogLevel` / `anira::LogDrain` / `anira::WaitStrategy` enums with their `to_string`, `default_log_drain()`, `default_num_threads()` and the `anira::LogLevel` overload of `default_log_level()` (the name stays inside the private core: `anira::default_log_level()` of `src/utils/Logger.h` answers the `anira_log_level` since D11, and `Core::default_num_threads()` is `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO`'s count), with its suite `test_CoreConfig`; its last user was the logger. Gone before it: its fields `m_anira_version` and `m_enabled_backends` (`anira::CoreConfig::m_anira_version` and `m_enabled_backends`), with the cross-session version and enabled-backends compares of the core (inside one copy every session shares one header; across two copies nothing can see the other); what the build compiled in is `anira_enabled_engines`, what is usable here `anira_capabilities_backends` on a probed context. Migration: `anira::ContextConfig` of `anira/anira.hpp` (the C `anira_context_config`), or the shim's `anira::v2::ContextConfig` with its `LogConfig` and enums; code that read the two fields uses the two entries.
- **Breaking:** C++ (2.x classes) — the 2.x `anira::JsonConfigLoader` (`<anira/utils/JsonConfigLoader.h>`, `src/utils/JsonConfigLoader.cpp`) and its suites `test_JsonConfigLoader` and `test_JsonConfigLoaderErrors`. Migration: `anira::ModelConfig::from_file` with `take_legacy_contract()`, and `anira::ContextConfig::from_file`, which read a 2.x document as it is; in the 2.x spelling, `anira::v2::JsonConfigLoader` of `<anira/compat/v2.hpp>` (strict: what the 2.x loader logged and skipped is an `anira::Error`). `test_Compat`'s loader case reads the four version-2 documents against their own values (`TheLoaderReadsTheDocuments`, formerly `TheLoaderMatchesTheLiveLoader`). `ExecuTorchModelFunction.JsonConfigCarriesModelFunction` reads the entry extension of the upgraded document.
- **Breaking:** C++ (2.x classes) — the 2.x runtime class `anira::InferenceHandler` (`<anira/InferenceHandler.h>`, `src/InferenceHandler.cpp`). Its name is the `anira.hpp` class over the C handler; a 2.x program builds its handler through `<anira/compat/v2.hpp>` (`anira::v2::InferenceHandler`), whose behaviour is the C handler's. No class of namespace `anira` is defined both in libanira and in the header-only `anira.hpp` any more (the library's scheduler thread is `anira::scheduler::InferenceThread`).
- **Breaking:** C++ (transitional) — alpha.1's bridge `<anira/compat/v3_to_v2.h>` (`namespace anira::v3compat`: `to_inference_config`, alpha.1's `to_context_config` (`to_core_config` in the snapshots), `to_host_config`, `enabled_engines`), which turned the 3.x configuration into the 2.x objects the 2.x handler took; alpha.1 announced it for alpha.1 only. Migration: build an `anira::InferenceHandler` from a `Pipeline` of the model config and prepare it with the contract instead (the usage guide, section 3); `anira::enabled_engines()` lists the engines of the build.
- **Breaking:** C++ (benchmark fixture) — the benchmark fixture is no longer part of the library: `<anira/benchmark.h>` and `<anira/benchmark/ProcessBlockFixture.h>` are not installed, libanira does not link Google Benchmark, and the installed package config does not look for it. The fixture lives in `examples/benchmark/fixture/` (the static target `anira_benchmark_fixture`), rewritten over `anira.hpp`: `initialize_repetition(ModelConfig, Hard, plan)` after the benchmark body prepared an `anira::InferenceHandler` and picked the plan with `set_plan` (`PlanReport::find`), `push_random_samples_in_buffer()` and `process_buffer()` over one planar tensor; the timing, the real-time factors and the summary lines are unchanged; `calculate_min` / `_max` / `_percentile` and `random_sample` are `anira::benchmark::` functions of the fixture header. `ANIRA_WITH_BENCHMARK` builds the benchmark examples and their fixture (also without `ANIRA_WITH_EXAMPLES`). A project of your own copies the two fixture files (`benchmarking.rst`).
- **Breaking:** Web — alpha.1's 2.x web layer. `src/emscripten-wrappers/` (the 225 `EMSCRIPTEN_KEEPALIVE` `uintptr_t` entry points over the 2.x classes, archive `anira::wasm_wrappers`) and the TypeScript classes over it (`web/src/wrappers/`: `InferenceConfig`, `InferenceHandler`, `PrePostProcessor`, `JSPrePostProcessor`, `ModelData`, `ProcessingSpec`, `TensorShape`, `HostConfig`, `BufferF`, `RingBuffer`, the `Vector*` classes, `InferenceBackend`, the wrapper `InferenceThread`), `web/src/utils.ts` (the `AniraWeb` factory fields) and `backends/JSBackendBase.ts`. Migration: the typed TypeScript API over the C ABI and `anira/anira_em.h` (Web, Added): configs, pipelines, contexts and handlers over the module's `_anira_*` exports (record offsets from `abi/layout.ts`), a JavaScript engine and a JavaScript processor registered on the pipeline, the inference threads anira starts; the intermediate runtime of the snapshots is under Pre-release names.
- **Breaking:** engines — alpha.1's five public processor classes `anira::OnnxRuntimeProcessor`, `anira::LibtorchProcessor`, `anira::ExecuTorchProcessor`, `anira::TFLiteProcessor` and `anira::LiteRtProcessor` (`anira/backends/<Engine>Processor.h`, their API pages and their `anira.h` lines) are removed: each engine is an internal adapter of the engine room now (`src/engines/<Engine>Adapter.cpp`, Changed). Migration: a custom engine (`anira::Engine`, or an `anira_engine_desc` in C; a 2.x custom backend is an `anira::Engine` under `anira::v2::k_custom_engine_id`) that drives a bundled engine itself links the matching `anira::<engine>` target as before.
- **Breaking:** Web — the runtime of the 2.x TypeScript package alpha.1 published, replaced by the typed layer over the C ABI and the threads anira starts from C (the TypeScript classes over the 2.x C++ classes, `web/src/wrappers/`, and their wrapper archive are the C ABI area's item): `AniraWeb` (`AniraWeb.create(config, memory)` over a pre-allocated `WebAssembly.Memory`, `spinUpInferenceWorker(workerOrUrl?)`, `registerProcessor` / `unregisterProcessor`, `registerPrePostProcessor` / `unregisterPrePostProcessor`, `registerAudioWorkletForContext`, `configureAudioWorklet(audioContext, inferenceHandlerPtr, prePostProcessorPtr, audioWorkletNodeName, { inputChannels, outputChannels, maxBufferSize, ... })`), `createAniraWasm` (one module instance per thread), the inference worker (`setupInferenceWorker`, the message protocol with `waitForWorkerMessage` and its message types, `InferenceWorker`, `ONNXRuntimeWebBackend`), the worklet (`AniraAudioWorkletBase`, the default `inference-processor`), the helpers (`getAniraVersion`, `drainAniraLog`, `randomSample`, `fillBuffer`, `pushBufferToRingbuffer`) and the subpath exports `./workers/*` (`./workers/inference-worker`, `./workers/audio-worklet`, `./workers/worklet-base`). The package's entry points are now `.`, `./scope` (what a worker or worklet module imports: the registry, `defineEngine`, `defineProcessor`, the bindings and the enums) and `./engines/onnxruntime-web`, beside `./wasm/*`. Migration (`docs/sphinx/migration.rst`, "Anira Web: from the 2.x TypeScript classes"): `Anira.create()` for `AniraWeb.create()` (`Anira.create({ memory: { initial, maximum } })` takes the sizes where alpha.1 took a memory); `anira.abi.anira_*` for the raw `_anira_*` calls, `anira.module` for the heap helpers; a context with `threads` and a `workerModule` for the Workers (`defineEngine` in that module for a `JSBackendBase` subclass registered with `registerProcessor`), and `await context.ready()` where `spinUpInferenceWorker` was awaited; `defineProcessor` in the worklet's module (loaded with `createAudioNode`'s `workletModules`) and `pipeline.addProcessor` for a `JSPrePostProcessor` and a worklet subclass; `handler.createAudioNode(audioContext, { params, staticInputs, waitMs })` for `registerAudioWorkletForContext` and `configureAudioWorklet`, whose `inputChannels` / `outputChannels` become `inputSlots` / `outputSlots`, lists of Streamed slots whose channel counts come from the model (default every Streamed slot of the side, so a single-stream model needs neither; `partialStreams` leaves a Streamed slot unserved); `onnxRuntimeWeb({ model })` from `@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web` for `ONNXRuntimeWebBackend`; `anira.version` and `anira.drainLog()` for `getAniraVersion` and `drainAniraLog`.
- **Extras and examples:** the extras' 2.x processors `CNNPrePostProcessor`, `CNNBypassProcessor`, `HybridNNPrePostProcessor`, `HybridNNBypassProcessor` and the advanced benchmark's `ClearCustomProcessor`: the examples and the tests run `HybridNNProcessor`, `BypassEngine` and `ClearEngine` (the CNN takes anira's default fill).

### Fixed

- **Runtime:** overload: a hop that finds every inference slot busy is dropped on its own, in place. Before, the submission dropped one hop per call and returned (`Core::new_data_submitted`, as in 2.x): with host blocks above the hop (1024 against 480) the remaining hops stayed in the send ring, which filled up and overwrote its oldest samples (`thl::core::RingBuffer` is delay-line friendly), so the windows left the hop grid (the 7744 of the macOS CI) and the output lost those samples for good: after one spike of all slots busy it ran early and missed every other call for as long as it ran. And the zeros of a dropped hop went into the receive ring at once, ahead of the inferences submitted before it, so the catch-up after a miss discarded them and delivered the late results instead. Now every hop the call completes is dropped when no slot is free, and each costs exactly one hop of fill at its own stream position, behind the chunks claimed before it (`ThreadSafeStruct::m_fills_after`, `SessionElement::owe_fills` / `place_pending_fills`): every hop of input yields one hop of output, in order, the windows stay on the grid, and the output is back at its latency once the late inferences are collected. `test/scheduler/test_Overload.cpp` drives one deterministic spike (an engine behind a gate) through block 1024 > hop 480, 480 = hop, 256 < hop and the 1..1024 range at its maximum and with varying blocks, under every miss policy, plus a stateful model: every sample is the model's output at the latency or the policy's fill, the windows are on the grid and in order, and the last calls are exact; all of it fails on the previous code. No late result is delivered after the late span either (the catch-up, under Changed).
- **Runtime:** a backend switch while audio runs could run two engines for one chunk, or none. `InferenceThread::inference()` loaded the session's backend atomic again in every engine block, and the blocks were independent: a `set_inference_backend` / `anira_handler_set_plan` that landed during an engine call ran a second engine on the same chunk when the new backend's block came later in the function (double cost, a missed budget, a Stateful model stepped twice), and one that landed between two blocks could skip every block, leaving the chunk's output tensors as its struct last held them. The fix made `inference()` take the backend as a parameter and dispatch through one `switch`, so exactly one processor ran per call; the engine room keeps the rule, the inference thread running the one plan the chunk was stamped with (below).
- **Runtime:** one chunk could be handled under three different backends. `Core::pre_process`, the inference thread and `Core::post_process` each loaded the session's backend on their own, so a switch that landed while a chunk was queued or in flight told its `pre_process`, its `before_inference` / `after_inference` hooks, its engine and its `post_process` different backends (a 2.x pre/post processor that fills tensors per backend then fed one backend's layout to another). The chunk is now stamped with one load at submission (`SessionElement::ThreadSafeStruct::m_plan`, the dense plan index, written in `Core::pre_process`) and every later step resolves the stamp through the session's plan table: a switch applies from the next submitted chunk on, and a chunk that is queued or in flight finishes on the plan it started on. Documented on `InferenceHandler::set_inference_backend` (now the compat shim's) and, while it lived, `InferenceManager::set_backend`, whose doc no longer claimed a session reinitialization.
- **Runtime:** a handler's loaded models and prepared handles could outlive its re-prepare or destroy: the inference thread that ran a handler's last inference kept the job (the session's `shared_ptr` and its struct) in its `m_inference_data` until its next dequeue, so while another handler kept the core's pool alive the released session, its plan table and the loaded models and prepared handles in it lived on until that thread picked up another job, and a registered engine's `unprepare` (the deletion of a C++ `Engine::Prepared`) ran late, on that inference thread, instead of before `anira_handler_prepare` or `anira_handler_destroy` returned as promised (with one handler the pool's teardown hid it; the 2.x path freed the same session's memory and its processors late, unobserved). `InferenceThread::process_dequeued_inference` lets go of the job's data before the session's active count drops, on every exit path and never as the last reference (the drainer holds its own while the count is above zero), so the manager's reference is the last and the session dies on the thread of the entry that releases it; `AbiEngine.UnprepareIsNotDeferredByAnInferenceThreadKeepingTheLastJob` pins it.
- **Runtime:** process exit with an inference in flight (Linux, macOS): the core's exit handler now stops the inference pool and waits up to two seconds for the inferences in flight before the exit handlers registered ahead of it run, the destructors of the static state an engine's runtime builds at its first load among them (ONNX Runtime's environment and type tables: the models load in the session's create, before the prepare starts the pool and registers the handler). Before, the pool ran on until the library's fini pass (the unload hook), so an ONNX Runtime inference still running at `exit()` could run over its torn-down type tables ("GetElementType is not implemented", the engine call failing with `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`) and then crash the process: `LibraryUnloadDeathTest.ExitWithABuiltInInferenceInFlightExitsNormally` died with SIGSEGV on the TFLite + ONNX Runtime shared CI leg, the one leg whose built-in engine there is ONNX Runtime, when the runner was slow enough to leave an inference in flight. The SIGSEGV was a consequence of the same race, not a second fault. The unload hook still joins the pool at exit without a bound, and an unload (`dlclose`) keeps its bounded wait and its abort. `test/contracts/unload` gains `ExitWaitsForTheInferenceBeforeTheRuntimesStaticsGo`, whose module engine keeps static state built before the prepare and exits with its own status when an inference finds it torn down: deterministic, it fails without the change. `docs/sphinx/troubleshooting.rst` describes the exit.
- **Runtime:** process exit from inside a lifecycle call (Linux, macOS): a process that ends from inside an engine's init or load (`exit()` from a runtime's fatal-error handler, say) exits with its own status instead of hanging. The load runs inside `anira_handler_prepare` under the core's lifecycle lock, and the exit runs the core's exit handler (its pool stop) and the library-unload hook (`Core::shutdown()`) on that thread, both of which took the lock: a deadlock, the exit handler's since the exit fix above, the unload hook's before it. The lock (`detail::LifecycleMutex`) knows its holder now; the two exit paths take `lock_unless_held()` (the interrupted call never resumes, so the state is the thread's alone), and its `try_lock` answers false to the holder (`report_rt_latches`, `release_core_if_idle`), where `std::mutex`'s was undefined. `LibraryUnloadDeathTest.ExitInsideAnEnginesLoadExitsNormally` (the first instance: the unload hook alone) and `ExitInsideAnEnginesLoadWithAPoolRunningExitsNormally` (another instance's pool running: the exit handler too) run a module engine whose load writes a marker and calls `exit(0)`; both hit the watchdog without the change and exit 0 with the marker with it. The load and init callbacks' documentation (`anira_engine_load_fn`, `anira_engine_init_fn`) and `docs/sphinx/troubleshooting.rst` say so.
- **Engines:** LibTorch on Linux aarch64: the LibTorch engine's init now pins OpenBLAS to one thread (`openblas_set_num_threads(1)`, looked up with `dlsym`) beside `torch::set_num_threads(1)`. The Linux aarch64 LibTorch package does its matrix products in OpenBLAS (the x86_64 packages in MKL, which `torch::set_num_threads` governs; macOS in Accelerate, Windows arm64 in Eigen), and `torch::set_num_threads` does not reach OpenBLAS's own worker pool: every product of an inference fanned out over one thread per core, whose workers wait for the next one spinning in `sched_yield`, so beside anira's inference threads they took every core, efficiency cores included.
